Extended fixture, each item's origin asserted beside its scope and context (10.5: a `subtree-coherence` item's origin is every `changed` node in its scope; a `parent-consistency` item's is every `changed` node in A's subtree — every changed branch's, skipped nodes included): a `changed` node with a `changed` ancestor generates no own item (skipping rule) — a top-level section `P` and its child `Q`, each with its own text edited, yield `P`'s `subtree-coherence` item and no item scoped at `Q`, that item's origin exactly `{P, Q}`, the skipped node included, never `{P}` alone (`P`'s parent being the root, no `parent-consistency` item arises); scope of `subtree-coherence` is the node plus all descendants; multiple changed nodes sharing an ancestor A yield one `parent-consistency` item for A against the union of branches — with A a top-level section whose child `X` and grandchild `Y` (beneath A's child `K`) each have their own text edited, A's single item has context exactly `{X, K}` and origin exactly `{X, Y}`, both branches' changed nodes, never one branch's alone and never `K`, the head of `Y`'s branch, while `K`'s item has context and origin `{Y}`, and `X`'s and `Y`'s `subtree-coherence` items have origins `{X}` and `{Y}`; for a change two levels beneath A (A → B → C, only C `changed`), A's `parent-consistency` item's context is exactly `{B}` — the branch head, A's child on that branch (10.5: each changed branch enters as one context node) — never `C`, while B's item's context is `{C}`, the identities asserted (T10.4-1's deep-edit sensitivity presupposes this context node), and both items' origin is exactly `{C}`, the changed node, never the branch head `B`; and with both `B`'s and `C`'s own text edited instead (A → B → C), `B` is visited and `C` skipped: `B`'s `subtree-coherence` item has origin exactly `{B, C}`, and A's `parent-consistency` item context exactly `{B}` and origin exactly `{B, C}` — the skipped node included — never `{B}`, the visited nodes alone, which a product drawing origins from visited nodes gives.
* **T10.5-2 Blocking chains.** A's `parent-consistency` item is blocked by, per changed branch, the item whose scope node is A's child on that branch: the child's `subtree-coherence` item when the child is itself visited, otherwise the child's `parent-consistency` item (10.5); chains extend to the top-level ancestor, the root receiving no item (10.5: one per non-root ancestor); only those two kinds block `parent-consistency` items; `metadata-consistency`, `dependency-consistency`, and `code-impact` items have empty `blockedBy`.
* **T10.5-3 Metadata/dependency/code items.** One `metadata-consistency` per `metadata-changed` node (context: added and removed `d` targets; its payload's metadata pair asserted by T10.7-13; its `reason` informational, 10.2, and never asserted); one `dependency-consistency` per node, a root included, having in the current graph a dependency edge — `depends` or `embeds` alike (T10.5-7) — to a target present on both sides of the baseline (5.6) whose effectiveHash changed (context: those targets; origin: the originating nodes of those targets' categories, a target's `metadata-changed` counting only when its `d` targets changed) — an edge to a target added since the baseline yields no such item, the change being reviewed at its source — both halves of 10.5's note staged (a new `d` edge makes the source `metadata-changed`, a new embedding makes it `changed`): a fixture node whose only affected target was added since the baseline gets no `dependency-consistency` item, its new `d` edge surfacing as its own `metadata-consistency` item; a second node whose only affected target entered through a new `{text(...)}` embedding likewise gets no `dependency-consistency` item — the new embedded reference changes its own content (5.5), it is `changed`, and the change is reviewed via its own `subtree-coherence` item; one `code-impact` per impacted location, through impact edges of either kind, `references` or `embeds` (T10.5-7) (context: the impact-edge targets that make it impacted, added and deleted included; origin: the originating nodes of those targets' categories, counted as for `dependency-consistency`). Current-graph edges (10.5: a dependency edge in the current graph): `X` whose only `d` reference to `T` stands at the baseline alone — removed since, while an edit to `T`'s upstream `U` changed `T`'s effectiveHash — gets its `metadata-consistency` item (context `{T}`) and no `dependency-consistency` item; a product reading edges from either graph gives `X` one. Origin counting: `X` depending on `T` and `T` on `U`, with `U`'s text edited and `T`'s tags edited (and, in a second staging, `T`'s coverage attribute instead; neither metadata edit changes an effectiveHash, 5.5, 5.6), gives `X`'s `dependency-consistency` item origin exactly `{U}`, `T`'s tags- or coverage-only `metadata-changed` not counting; a twin in which `T`'s `d` set changes instead, gaining a reference to an unedited `W`, gives exactly `{T, U}`. A code location whose marker targets `T` — transitively impacted in each staging, `T`'s subtreeHash unchanged — has origin exactly `{U}` under the first stagings and `{T, U}` under the twin. One item per location (10.5: one `code-impact` item per impacted code location; a kind and scope node produced more than once in one derivation yields a single item): T9.2-4's location, impacted in both code categories, staged in a baseline (`path-blocks`) session, gets exactly one `code-impact` item, its context exactly the two targets and its origin exactly their originating nodes — failing a product that derives one item per code category.
* **T10.5-4 Item order.** A fixture with items of all kinds across two files asserts the total order: requirement-scoped first by depth deepest-first (roots 0; T10.5-7 and T10.5-8 order root-scoped items after deeper ones), then kind order `subtree-coherence`, `metadata-consistency`, `dependency-consistency`, `parent-consistency`, then file path bytes, then document order; `code-impact` items last by location identity; after deleting a scope node, absent-scope items order after present ones by identity then item `id` (10.5 ordering rule); `status`/`next`/`export` all present this order.
* **T10.5-5 Re-derivation on updated.** Resolving an item `updated` re-derives (10.5). Matching: a generated item whose kind and scope node match an existing item is that item — it keeps its `id`, status, note, and recorded state and takes the generated scope, context, and origin (its `reason` too, informational, 10.2, and never compared) — and 10.4 alone then decides whether it reads `invalidated`, a context-set change marking it so. Fixture: two visited top-level sections `N` and `V`; `N`'s `subtree-coherence` item resolved `no-change` with `--note`, then a child section added under `N`, then `V`'s `subtree-coherence` item resolved `updated`: `N`'s item keeps its `id`, its note, and its `current` byte-for-byte; its scope and origin, which reads presented without the added child before the re-derivation (10.4: as last assigned), now include it (an added node is `changed`, 5.6); and it reads `invalidated` (`N`'s subtreeHash changed), never `unresolved` — its stored status kept, `list` counting it `no-change`. A changed origin by itself invalidates nothing (T10.4-7(b)). Items no longer generated remain with their `blockedBy`, never recomputed — asserted on a non-empty one: in a path-blocks session over a top-level `A` holding `K1`, and a top-level `V`, with `K1`'s and `V`'s text edited before `create` (`K1`'s item; `A`'s `parent-consistency` item, `blockedBy` `K1`'s item; `V`'s item), `K1`'s edit is reverted and `V`'s item resolved `updated`. `A`'s item, no longer generated, keeps `blockedBy` exactly `K1`'s item in `export`; `status` reports it blocked while `K1`'s item — no longer generated either — is unresolved, and `resolve` on it is refused in T10.7-14's form, nothing modified; once `K1`'s item is resolved, `A`'s reads unblocked and resolves. A product recomputing `blockedBy` for every item empties `A`'s, unblocking it (`next` alone cannot tell the two apart, `K1`'s deeper item coming first). Reads present an item no longer generated with its `context` as last assigned (10.4: its current context set) — which, once a re-derivation has reassigned it after the item's last resolve, differs from its recorded context set (T10.4-6(b)). New items — the `subtree-coherence` item of each newly visited node among them — enter in item order, created `unresolved` (10.2 — the discriminating fixture: the triggering item resolved `updated`, whose status must not propagate to the new item). A node newly `changed` beneath a `changed` ancestor is skipped and gets none: with a top-level `P`'s text edited before `create` and its child `Q`'s after, `P`'s item resolved `updated` leaves no item scoped at `Q`, `P`'s item now with origin `{P, Q}` — a product adding an item per newly `changed` node gives `Q` one. A node newly visited because its `changed` ancestor reverted gains its item: with `P`'s and `Q`'s text both edited before `create` (`P`'s item, origin `{P, Q}`), `Q`'s text edited again and `P`'s edit reverted, then `P`'s item resolved `updated`: `Q`'s `subtree-coherence` item and `P`'s `parent-consistency` item (context and origin `{Q}`, `blockedBy` `Q`'s item) enter, `unresolved`, and `P`'s `subtree-coherence` item remains. Decompositions (after a `split`): `blockedBy` is recomputed with each reference to a decomposed item replaced by all items of its decomposition; a decomposed kind and scope node is never re-added — its decomposition applies instead, recursively, as one `subtree-coherence` item per child subtree the scope node has at the re-derivation plus the scope node's `parent-consistency` item: in a path-blocks session, a child section added under a split item's scope root after the `split` gains its `subtree-coherence` item at the next re-derivation, `unresolved` — no generator but the decomposition producing it there — and the `parent-consistency` item's context and `blockedBy` gain the child and that item; decomposition matching compares canonical identities (T10.4-4's decomposition arm). Timing (10.5: re-derivation at an `updated` resolve and at the end of every `split`, at no other time): new items enter only at `create`, at a re-derivation, or through a `split`'s decomposition — resolving with `no-change` or `skipped` does not re-derive, so a concurrent workspace edit surfaces as invalidation, not new items, until the next `updated` resolve or `split` (T10.7-9(c)).
* **T10.5-6 Baseline recording.** The session records the resolved commit identity of `--base`; later `HEAD` movement or branch renames do not change what generators run against (re-derivation still diffs against the recorded commit).
* **T10.5-7 Embedding edges and a root-sourced embedding.** 10.5 derives `dependency-consistency` items over every dependency edge a requirement node bears, `embeds` as well as `depends` (5.2), and `code-impact` items over 9.2's impacted set, whose impact edges are `embeds` as well as `references`. Its "per requirement node" excludes no root. Fixture: configuration with a spec group over `specs/*.mdx` and a code group over `src/*.ts`. `specs/B.mdx` holds a top-level section `t`. `specs/A.mdx` imports `./B.xspec` as `B` and holds three constructs: a top-level `{text(B.t)}` outside every section, a root-sourced `embeds` edge as T8-5 stages one; a section `e` holding only `{text(B.t)}`, an `embeds` edge its sole dependency edge; and a section `f` spelled `d={B.t}` and holding `{text(B.t)}`, a `depends` and an `embeds` edge to one target. `src/show.ts` imports `../specs/B.xspec` with its `text` export and calls `text(B.t)` in a function `show`. `src/echo.ts` imports `../specs/A.xspec` likewise and calls `text(A.e)` in a function `echo`. No marker stands anywhere. With that state committed as the baseline and `t`'s text then edited, `review create --base` yields exactly six items, in this order. (1) `t`'s `subtree-coherence` item: context `specs/B.mdx`, origin `{specs/B.mdx#t}`. (2) and (3) `dependency-consistency` items scoped `specs/A.mdx#e` and then `specs/A.mdx#f`, each with context exactly `{specs/B.mdx#t}` and origin `{specs/B.mdx#t}`. `f` gets one item, not one per edge, and its target enters its context once though two edges reach it. (4) A `dependency-consistency` item scoped at `specs/A.mdx`'s root, context and origin likewise, at depth 0 after every depth-1 item (10.5: roots are 0). (5) The `code-impact` item of `src/echo.ts#echo`: transitively impacted through its `embeds` edge, since `e`'s effectiveHash changed and its subtreeHash did not (9.2); context `{specs/A.mdx#e}`; origin `{specs/B.mdx#t}`, the originating node of `e`'s change rather than the context node. (6) The `code-impact` item of `src/show.ts#show`: directly impacted, context and origin `{specs/B.mdx#t}`. Every `blockedBy` is empty. `e`, `f`, and `specs/A.mdx`'s root get no `subtree-coherence` item: they are `upstream-changed`, not `changed`, because an embedded target's edit leaves the embedder's own content unchanged (5.5). The root item's scope text in `show` and `export` is the root's own text (10.7): `t`'s current subtree text expanded where the top-level embedding stands, `e`'s and `f`'s contributions excised (1.6). It is byte-asserted, and is never the root's subtree text. A product deriving these items from `d` targets and markers alone omits items (2), (4), (5), and (6). One that excludes roots omits (4).
* **T10.5-8 A `changed` root.** An edit to a root's own content makes the root `changed` (5.5, 5.6), whether it edits the root's top-level text or adds, removes, or reorders a top-level section. The skipping rule then visits the root as N, so its single `subtree-coherence` item takes in every other `changed` node of its file. No `parent-consistency` item arises there, those going to non-root ancestors alone (10.5). Fixture: `specs/R.mdx` holds top-level text, a section `a` with a child `a.b`, and a section `c`. `specs/S.mdx` holds a section `x` with a child `x.y`. After the baseline commit, four edits: `a.b`'s text and `x.y`'s text are edited, a top-level section `d` is added to `specs/R.mdx`, and `specs/R.mdx`'s top-level text is edited. `review create --base` yields exactly three items, in this order. (1) `x.y`'s `subtree-coherence` item, at depth 2: context `specs/S.mdx`, `specs/S.mdx#x`; origin `{specs/S.mdx#x.y}`. (2) `x`'s `parent-consistency` item, at depth 1: context and origin `{specs/S.mdx#x.y}`, `blockedBy` item (1). (3) `specs/R.mdx`'s root's `subtree-coherence` item, at depth 0: scope the root and every section of the file (`a`, `a.b`, `c`, `d`); context empty; origin exactly `{specs/R.mdx, specs/R.mdx#a.b, specs/R.mdx#d}`; `blockedBy` empty. Item (3)'s `baseline`, fixed from the recorded baseline graph (10.2), holds for the root, `a`, `a.b`, and `c`, each present, the subtreeHash and metadataHash `query node` reports for each before the four edits — the root's, `a`'s, and `a.b`'s subtreeHashes differing from their current values — and for `d`, in the item's scope when it entered though the baseline graph lacks it, an entry presenting it absent, its hashes the explicit absent marker (10.4): never `d`'s current hashes, and never no entry, which 10.2 leaves to a node joining the item later. A product filling `baseline` from the current graph, or entering there only the nodes the baseline graph holds, fails. No item is scoped at `a.b`, `a`, or `d`. `specs/S.mdx`'s nodes keep their own items, since that file's root is no `changed` ancestor of theirs. `show` and `export` present item (3)'s scope text as the root's subtree text, `specs/R.mdx`'s entire compiled Markdown output (1.6, 10.7). A twin leaves `specs/R.mdx`'s top-level text unedited, so the added section alone makes the root `changed`, a structural edit originating at the parent (5.6). It yields the same three items. A product that never visits a root fails both stagings: it gives `a.b` and `d` items of their own and `a` a `parent-consistency` item.
* **T10.5-9 Deleted and moved nodes in baseline sessions.** 10.5: in a baseline session a node deleted since the baseline (5.6) is the child of its baseline parent, identities mapped through the journal (6.3), and a present node the child of its current parent; ancestors, descendants, children, and subtrees follow these links. Fixture: a top-level section `A` holding `A.B`, which holds `A.B.C`, committed as the baseline; `A.B.C`'s section then deleted, nothing else edited. `A.B` is `changed` — it lost a child (5.5, 5.6) — and visited; `A` is not `changed`. `review create --base` yields exactly two items: `A.B`'s `subtree-coherence` item — scope `{A.B, A.B.C}`, `A.B.C` presented absent under its baseline identity (10.4); context the root and `A`; origin exactly `{A.B, A.B.C}` — and `A`'s `parent-consistency` item — context exactly `{A.B}`, origin exactly `{A.B, A.B.C}`, `blockedBy` `A.B`'s item. A product linking deleted nodes nowhere gives `A.B`'s item the scope `{A.B}` and both items the origin `{A.B}`. Texts and hashes keep their definitions (10.5: the links bind node relations and node sets alone; 1.6, 5.5), `A.B.C`'s baseline text carrying a token no other node's own text spells. In `show` and `export`, and in `next --json` — which returns `A.B`'s item, then `A`'s once `A.B`'s item is resolved `no-change` — each text is byte-equal to the value `query node` reports: `A.B`'s item's scope text to `A.B`'s current subtree text, `A`'s item's scope text to `A`'s current own text, and `A`'s item's context text for `A.B` to `A.B`'s current subtree text; no scope or context text contains the token. The items' `current` entries — `A.B`'s subtreeHash and metadataHash in `A.B`'s item; `A`'s ownHash and metadataHash and `A.B`'s subtreeHash in `A`'s — equal the hashes `query node` reports after the deletion, and `A.B.C`'s entries in `A.B`'s item are the explicit absent marker (10.4). A product computing text or hashes over the linked tree splices `A.B.C`'s text into `A.B`'s subtree text or folds `A.B.C` into `A.B`'s subtreeHash. Journal arm: after the baseline, `xspec rename` renames `A.B` to `A.B2` (journaled; `A.B.C` becomes `A.B2.C` by prefix replacement), and `A.B2.C`'s section is then deleted by manual edit: the same two items, the first scoped at `A.B2` with scope `{A.B2, A.B2.C}` and origin `{A.B2, A.B2.C}`, the deleted node presented absent under `A.B2.C`, its baseline identity mapped forward through the journal — never under `A.B.C`, and never outside `A.B2`'s subtree — and `A`'s item context `{A.B2}`, origin `{A.B2, A.B2.C}`. Audit contrast (10.6: an audit item's scope is the node and all its current descendants): an audit session over the same baseline file, `A.B.C`'s section deleted by manual edit after `create`, then `A.B.C`'s item resolved `updated`: `A.B`'s re-derived item has scope exactly `{A.B}` and an empty `blockedBy`, while `A.B.C`'s item remains (10.5: items no longer generated remain). Present-node arm (10.5: a present node is the child of its current parent): `specs/R.mdx` holds top-level sections `P1`, holding `P1.M`, which holds `P1.M.c`, and then `P2`, each section holding a line of its own text, in T6.2-3's clean-boundary shape — every tag alone on its line, so `P2` is a flow-position parent — committed as the baseline. The journaled `xspec move specs/R.mdx#P1.M specs/R.mdx#P2.M` follows (`P1.M.c` becoming `P2.M.c` by prefix replacement, 6.5), leaving `changed` the two parents alone (6.2, T6.2-3); `P2.M.c`'s section is then deleted by manual edit, making `P2.M.c` `changed` as deleted and `P2.M` as having lost it, both beneath the `changed` `P2`. `review create --base` yields exactly two items, in this order: `P1`'s `subtree-coherence` item — scope exactly `{P1}`, origin exactly `{P1}` — and `P2`'s — scope exactly `{P2, P2.M, P2.M.c}`, `P2.M.c` presented absent under its baseline identity mapped forward through the journal (its baseline parent `P1.M` mapped to `P2.M`, a present node whose current parent is `P2`), never under `P1.M.c`, and origin exactly `{P2, P2.M, P2.M.c}` — each with context `specs/R.mdx` and an empty `blockedBy`. A twin stopping before the deletion yields the same two items, `P2`'s scope `{P2, P2.M, P2.M.c}` with every node present and its origin exactly `{P2}`. A product linking present nodes under their baseline parents gives `P1`'s item the moved subtree — scope `{P1, P2.M, P2.M.c}` — and `P2`'s the scope `{P2}`; one linking the deleted node under its unmapped baseline parent leaves `P2.M.c` out of `P2`'s scope.

### 10.6 audit

* **T10.6-1 Generation.** One `subtree-coherence` item per requirement node, roots included; context: ancestor chain (empty for roots); origin empty; scope: node plus descendants; no baseline required (works in a git-less workspace).
* **T10.6-2 Order and blocking.** Item order: scope-node file path bytes, then document order; each item's `blockedBy` is exactly its child sections' items, so leaves are unblocked and `next` walks bottom-up; after a `split`, blockers are the decomposition items (10.5/10.7). Absent-scope ordering (the 10.5 rule applies to this order too): after a scoped section is deleted by manual edit, its item orders after the same file's present-scope items, by scope-node identity then item `id`.
* **T10.6-3 Re-derivation on updated.** Audit sessions re-derive too (10.5: the resolve-time re-derivation holds for every strategy; `audit` records no creation parameters): author a new section, then resolve an unblocked item `updated` — the new node's `subtree-coherence` item appears in audit item order with status `unresolved` (10.2) and its `current` equal to the new node's subtreeHash and metadataHash captured via `query node` at the re-derivation (10.2: written at item creation; 10.5: added with current state) — still equal to them after a further edit to the new section's text, its item staying `unresolved` (T10.2-4's creation arm) — and its parent's item's `blockedBy` is recomputed to include it; existing items keep their `id`, status, note, and recorded state and take the generated scope (10.5): the new section's parent — a leaf at `create` — had its item resolved `no-change` with `--note` before the section was authored, and the `updated` resolve is another item's; the parent's item keeps its `id`, its note, and its `current` byte-for-byte, presents after the re-derivation the scope with the child (10.6: the node and all its current descendants) — before it, the scope as last assigned (10.4) — and reads `invalidated` (its subtreeHash changed, 10.4), never `unresolved`, its stored status kept. Control: after the same edit, resolving an item `no-change` adds no item — the new node's item enters only through a re-derivation, at an `updated` resolve or at the end of a `split` (10.5; T10.7-9(c)).

### 10.7 Commands

Where a test here names no form, its report assertions read the subcommand's `--json` document through its H-3 adapter, `export` being JSON-only; T12.0-15 asserts the same information of the human reports of `list`, `status`, `next`, and `show`, and of the refusals, on these fixtures — `next --json`'s self-contained payload, a requirement of the JSON form alone (10.7), excepted.

* **T10.7-1 create flags.** Exactly one of `--base`, `--strategy audit`, `--coverage` required: none, two, or `--strategy` with any other value → exit 2 — among those values `bogus`, and `path-blocks` and `coverage`, the strategies `--base` and `--coverage` alone select (10.5, 10.7), each a syntax-class error (T12.0-10: identically with the configuration file invalid or missing, `code` `null`). `create` with an existing name refused (exit 1, one code-less finding in T10.7-14's form, nothing modified); naming an existing corrupt session (T10.1-4's stagings), the corruption stands in the refusal's place — exactly one finding, condition 21 `corrupt-session`, no code-less refusal beside it, exit 1 (10.7); and so it does where the name matches the corrupt session's ignoring ASCII case alone (10.1: such a name is treated at `create` as an existing session's): `review create --strategy audit --name Foo` beside a corrupt `foo.json` (T10.1-4's garbage-bytes staging) reports exactly that finding, its concerned path `.xspec/reviews/foo.json`, exit 1, and writes no `Foo.json` — failing a product that matches names case-insensitively against readable sessions alone. A name matching several sessions ignoring ASCII case names each of them (10.1), the corruption reported as one finding per corrupt session it matches and the refusal not beside them (10.7). On the Linux leg, three arms, each with its own staging holding both `.xspec/reviews/foo.json` and `.xspec/reviews/FOO.json`, each file valid or corrupt as its arm states — a valid `foo.json` written by the product here, a valid `FOO.json` written by the product in an identical twin workspace and copied in (this workspace's own `create` refuses the second casing), and a corrupt file of either casing staged by T10.1-4's garbage bytes: (i) both corrupt — `review create --strategy audit --name Foo` exits 1 with exactly two condition-21 findings, concerning `.xspec/reviews/FOO.json` and `.xspec/reviews/foo.json` in that order (12.7: by concerned path bytes), and no code-less refusal; (ii) `foo.json` valid, `FOO.json` corrupt — `--name Foo`, and `--name foo`, whose exact match is the valid session, each exit 1 with exactly one condition-21 finding, concerning `.xspec/reviews/FOO.json`, and no refusal; (iii) both valid — `--name Foo` exits 1 with exactly one code-less refusal (10.7: each refusal one finding). In every arm nothing is written — no `Foo.json` created, both files byte-unchanged — failing a product that reports the first match alone, a refusal beside the corruption, or one refusal per match. Missing `--name` → exit 2. `--coverage` naming no configured profile → exit 2 (an unknown profile named in arguments, 12.0), nothing created — no session file exists and `list` reports no such session (10.7: `create` records the profile's resolved definition, so an unknown profile leaves nothing to record).
* **T10.7-2 Recorded parameters.** A `coverage` session records the profile definition with group names replaced by glob lists and kind: after `create`, renaming the profile or editing its group's globs in configuration does not change session behavior (recorded globs still matched against currently discovered sources); a file no longer in any configured group leaves the session's view (as if deleted). A baseline session records the commit (T10.5-6); an audit session records none.
* **T10.7-3 Unresolvable baseline.** `create --base` with an unresolvable ref fails per 6.3 as exit 2, modifying nothing. So does each later `review` subcommand that runs the session's generators against its recorded baseline — `status`, `next`, `show`, `export`, `resolve`, and `split` (10.4, 10.5, 10.7), each naming the session and, where it takes one, an item the operation would otherwise accept — once that baseline can no longer be reconstructed, the workspace otherwise passing `build`'s validations (staged, for instance, by removing the repository after `create`: the configuration file then lies inside no working tree, 6.3). `list`, which counts stored statuses without read-time invalidation (10.7), runs no generator: on the same state it reports the session, with its stored counts, and exits 0.
* **T10.7-4 coverage sessions.** One `uncovered-requirement` item per uncovered required node of the recorded profile — scope: the node; context: ancestor chain; origin and `blockedBy` empty; item order file path then document order, with absent-scope items after the same file's present ones by scope-node identity then item `id` (10.5 ordering rule; fixture deletes an uncovered node's section after `create`).
* **T10.7-5 list.** Reports every session, in byte order of session name — the fixture creates `a2`, then `a`, then `B`, and `list` reports `B`, `a`, `a2`: creation order and ASCII-case-folded order both differ from byte order (10.7, 12.0) — with name, strategy, item counts by stored status (no read-time invalidation applied — a stale-resolved item still counts under its stored status); corrupt sessions reported by name as corrupt; exit 1 iff any corrupt session exists, else 0.
* **T10.7-6 status.** Items in item order with id, kind, scope, status, blocked state, plus totals by status (read-time invalidation applied).
* **T10.7-7 next.** Returns the first needing-review unblocked item in item order; when all items are resolved (and for an empty session), exits 0 and reports fully resolved in human and `--json` forms with no item in the JSON payload; `--json` payload is self-contained: scope text, context text, origin before/after text, source ranges — every present node's, requirement node and present code location alike (1.7); none for absent nodes — baseline and current hashes, and a `metadata-consistency` item's metadata pair (T10.7-13). Full resolution concerns the session's items alone (10.7: a change that would generate a new item enters the session only at its next re-derivation): an audit session with every item resolved, then a new spec source holding one section added — touching no existing item's recorded state or context set, while a re-derivation would generate items for its root and section: `next` still exits 0 and reports the session fully resolved, no item in its JSON payload; once a resolved leaf item is re-resolved `updated`, `next` returns the new section's item, `unresolved` and the first unblocked item needing review.
* **T10.7-8 show/export.** `show <name> <item-id>` reports the full item (10.2 fields plus the `next --json` text payload); unknown item ID → exit 2. `export` emits one JSON document — with or without `--json` — containing name, strategy, recorded creation parameters, recorded decompositions, and every item in item order with fields, blocked state, payload, and read-time invalidation applied.
* **T10.7-9 split.** Splitting a `subtree-coherence` item whose scope root has children yields one `subtree-coherence` item per child subtree (context: the child's ancestor chain) plus one `parent-consistency` item for the scope root (context: exactly the scope root's children; `blockedBy`: the per-child items); newly created decomposition items enter `unresolved` (10.2) — asserted on a split of a resolved item, whose status must not propagate to them; every item outside the decomposition that was blocked by the original becomes blocked by all decomposition items; the original is removed and its `id` never reused (assert across subsequent re-derivations, exercised in a path-blocks session and in an audit session — the re-derivation and decomposition rules of 10.5 hold for every strategy); the decomposition is recorded durably — `export` presents it — and governs re-derivation (T10.5-5). Two fixtures carry the arms below. Path-blocks: top-level sections `N`, with children `N.a` and `N.b`, and `M`, with a child `M.k`; `N`'s own text and `N.a`'s text edited before `create --base` (`N` visited, its item's scope `{N, N.a, N.b}`); `N`'s item resolved `no-change`; `M.k`'s text then edited; `N`'s item split. Audit: a top-level section `N` with children `N.a`, holding `N.a.x`, and `N.b`, and a top-level leaf section `s`; after `create`, `N.b`'s item resolved `no-change` with `--note`; `N.a`'s item split; then, by manual edit, a child `N.c` holding `N.c.y` authored under `N` and a child `N.b.z` under `N.b`; `N`'s item split. (a) Per-child blocking (10.7: a per-child item's `blockedBy` is the one its session's strategy gives a `subtree-coherence` item — none in a baseline session, its child sections' items in an audit session): path-blocks — `N.a`'s and `N.b`'s items have empty `blockedBy`, and `N`'s `parent-consistency` item's is exactly those two; audit — `N.c`'s new item's `blockedBy` is exactly `N.c.y`'s item, never the original's blockers, which a product passing them on to new decomposition items assigns. (b) Origins (10.7: each decomposition item's origin is, as in 10.5, the `changed` nodes in its scope — for the `parent-consistency` item, in its scope node's subtree — and empty in an audit session): path-blocks — `N.a`'s item's origin exactly `{N.a}`, `N.b`'s exactly empty, and `N`'s `parent-consistency` item's exactly `{N, N.a}`, the scope node included — never `{N.a}`, the per-child origins' union; audit — every decomposition item's origin empty. (c) Re-derivation at the split's end (10.7, 10.5): path-blocks — once the split exits, `M.k`'s `subtree-coherence` item and `M`'s `parent-consistency` item (context and origin `{M.k}`, `blockedBy` `M.k`'s item) are in the session, created `unresolved`, in item order; audit — `N.c.y`'s and `N.b.z`'s items enter at `N`'s split likewise. A product that does not re-derive at a split adds none of them. (d) Recursion (10.7: a per-child item whose kind and scope node are recorded as decomposed is not created; its decomposition applies instead, recursively, its items standing in for the per-child item — in the `parent-consistency` item's `blockedBy` too): audit — `N`'s split creates no item scoped at `N.a`, nor does any later re-derivation (P-9); `N`'s `parent-consistency` item's context is exactly `{N.a, N.b, N.c}` and its `blockedBy` exactly `N.a.x`'s item, `N.a`'s `parent-consistency` item, `N.b`'s item, and `N.c`'s item; and the root's item, blocked by the original, is blocked by exactly those four items, `N`'s `parent-consistency` item, and `s`'s item. (e) Reuse (10.7: a decomposition item whose kind and scope node already exist in the session is not created; the existing item takes its place, keeping its `id`, status, note, and recorded state and taking the decomposition's scope, context, origin, reason, and `blockedBy`): audit — `N.b`'s item keeps its `id`, its note, its `current` byte-for-byte, and its stored status (`list` counts it `no-change`; reads present it `invalidated`, `N.b`'s subtreeHash having changed, never `unresolved`), and takes the scope `{N.b, N.b.z}` and the `blockedBy` `N.b.z`'s item; `N.a.x`'s item and `N.a`'s `parent-consistency` item keep their `id`s; `reason`, informational (10.2), is not compared. (f) Deleted children (10.7: the scope root's children — in a baseline session, deleted ones included, 10.5): a path-blocks session over a top-level section `P` whose only children `P.a` and `P.b` were deleted between the baseline and `create` — `P` `changed`, having lost them, and its item's scope `{P, P.a, P.b}` — splits `P`'s item into `subtree-coherence` items scoped at the absent `P.a` and `P.b`, each presented absent under its baseline identity (10.4), with origins `{P.a}` and `{P.b}` and context exactly the file root and `P` — each deleted child's ancestor chain over its baseline-parent link (10.5: ancestors follow these links, in decompositions too), never the empty chain the current graph gives an absent node — and `P`'s `parent-consistency` item, context exactly `{P.a, P.b}` and origin exactly `{P, P.a, P.b}`. Texts and hashes keep their definitions (10.5; as in T10.5-9), `P.a`'s and `P.b`'s baseline texts each carrying a token no other node's own text spells: before the split, `P`'s item's scope text is byte-equal to `P`'s current subtree text as `query node` reports it — its own text, `P` having no current child — and its `current` subtreeHash and metadataHash for `P` equal those `query node` reports; after it, `P`'s `parent-consistency` item's scope text is byte-equal to `P`'s current own text and its `current` ownHash and metadataHash for `P` equal those `query node` reports, its entries for `P.a` and `P.b` the explicit absent marker (10.4); neither of `P`'s scope texts contains either token. The absent children's texts come from the one graph state containing them, the items' baseline state (10.2: the recorded baseline): the per-child items' scope texts and `P`'s `parent-consistency` item's context texts for `P.a` and `P.b` are `P.a`'s and `P.b`'s baseline subtree texts (10.7). In an audit session the children are the current ones alone: an item whose scope root lost all its children by manual edit after `create` is the childless refusal below, though its scope as last assigned still lists them. (g) Blocker replacement under path-blocks' own blocking rules (10.7: every item outside the decomposition that was blocked by the original becomes blocked by all items of the decomposition; 10.5: each re-derivation recomputes `blockedBy` per the strategy's rules, any reference to a decomposed item replaced by all items of its decomposition): a twin of the path-blocks fixture, run through the same steps, nests its `N` under a top-level section `G`, its sections `G.N`, `G.N.a`, and `G.N.b` standing for the fixture's `N`, `N.a`, and `N.b`. `G`'s `parent-consistency` item (context `{G.N}`) is blocked by exactly `G.N`'s `subtree-coherence` item before the split, so it reads unblocked once that item is resolved `no-change`; once `G.N`'s item is split, by exactly `G.N.a`'s and `G.N.b`'s items and `G.N`'s `parent-consistency` item, `status` reporting it blocked; and still by exactly those three once `G.N.a`'s item is then resolved `updated` and the session re-derived — each `blockedBy` read from `export`. A product making the replacement in audit's child-section blocking alone, the audit fixture's (d), fails here. Refused (exit 1; T10.7-14's finding form; nothing modified), beside the successful splits above: `split` on an item of any other kind; on a `subtree-coherence` item whose scope root has no children — a leaf's item (audit `s`'s), and, in an audit session over a top-level section `Q` holding `Q.r`, `Q`'s item once `Q.r`'s section is deleted by manual edit after `create`.
* **T10.7-10 resolve.** Sets status and records current relevant state; works on any unblocked item regardless of status (re-resolving `invalidated` and flipping a resolved status both work); resolving a blocked item refused (exit 1, T10.7-14's finding form, nothing modified); unknown session or item → exit 2; `--note` stored and reported, and each resolve replaces the note (10.7: `resolve` sets the note to the `--note` text, removing any earlier note when `--note` is not given): a resolve with `--note n1`, then one with `--note n2`, leaves exactly `n2` in `show` and `export`; a further resolve without `--note` leaves the item no note in either.
* **T10.7-11 Coverage re-derivation.** Resolving an `uncovered-requirement` item `updated` re-derives with the session's recorded profile against the current workspace (10.5: every strategy; 10.7): a required node made newly uncovered since `create` (its covering edge removed) gains an `uncovered-requirement` item, created `unresolved` (10.2), in coverage item order; an item whose node was meanwhile covered is no longer generated and remains in the session with its status and recorded state (10.5); a generated item matching an existing kind and scope node keeps its `id`, status, note, and recorded state — one resolved with `--note` before the re-derivation presenting its note after it.
* **T10.7-12 Payload text contract.** A baseline fixture generating every built-in kind (a coverage session supplying `uncovered-requirement`), texts byte-asserted in `next --json` and identically via `show` and `export` (one payload rule, 10.7), with an embedding inside one asserted text to pin expansion (1.6). Scope text by kind: the scope root's subtree text for `subtree-coherence`; the scope node's subtree text for `uncovered-requirement`; the scope node's own text — not subtree text, the fixture making them differ — for `parent-consistency`, `dependency-consistency`, and `metadata-consistency`; a `code-impact` scope enters as identity, presence, and — when present — its source range, with no text (10.7; review payloads are one of the two range-presenting outputs for code locations, 1.7: the fixture's present impacted locations are named units whose ranges are byte-asserted per T1.7-2 — among them two property members, each holding a marker on a section edited since the baseline, `m = () => { SPEC.a };`, its range running through its `;`, and a decorated `@dec n = () => { SPEC.a };`, from its `@` through its `;` (1.7) — and a deleted location's entry carries none). Context text: own text where the context is an ancestor chain (`subtree-coherence`, `uncovered-requirement`); subtree text otherwise (the children of the scope node for `parent-consistency` — a generated item's changed-branch heads, every child of the scope root for a split-produced one; targets for `dependency-consistency`, `metadata-consistency`, and `code-impact`). Origin text: a before/after pair of the node's own text — before, its value in the item's baseline state (10.2: in a baseline session the recorded baseline graph), whether or not the node has an entry in `baseline`; after, its value in the current graph (the fixture edits an originating node again after `create`, so the sides differ and neither equals the create-time value). A node that joined the origin at a re-derivation has no entry in `baseline` (T10.2-2) and still takes its before side from the baseline state: on T10.4-7(a)'s fixture, `D2`'s text edited a second time once `X`'s item is resolved — its baseline text, its text when it joined, and its current text pairwise distinct — `X`'s payload presents `D2`'s pair as its baseline text before and its current text after, never the join-time text before, nor the before side absent; for a node added since the baseline the before side, and for a since-deleted node the after side, is the absent side of the pair — presented absent, with no text, like a node contained in no state. Absent-node provenance, each arm staged on a node in a role 10.7 assigns a text — a `subtree-coherence` item's scope root, the scope node of any other requirement-scoped kind, or a context node; 10.7 assigns none to a `subtree-coherence` item's other scope nodes, so no arm expects one there. (i) A node edited between the baseline and `create`, recorded into items at `create`, then deleted, presents the text of the most recent graph state containing it — the `create`-time derivation's, not the baseline's (the values differ) — and still does after a later `updated` resolve re-derives the session without it (a state not containing the node contributes nothing): with a top-level `A` holding `K1`, `K1`'s text edited before `create` and `K1`'s section deleted after it, `A`'s `parent-consistency` item presents its context node `K1`, and `K1`'s `subtree-coherence` item its scope root, absent with `K1`'s `create`-time subtree text; both still do once `K1`'s item is resolved `updated`, whose re-derivation, over a graph without `K1`, produces neither item (`A`, having lost `K1`, is now visited, 5.6, 10.5) and keeps both. (ii) A node deleted since the baseline and never seen by a mutating derivation with newer text presents its `baseline` value: a `metadata-consistency` item's removed `d` target, its section deleted with the reference before `create` (T10.4-2's context-arm shape), is presented absent with its baseline subtree text as its context text; T10.7-9(f) asserts the same of a `split`'s items, at a per-child scope root and at a `parent-consistency` context node. (iii) Resolve states count among those states (10.7: the states under which mutating subcommands derived or resolved the item with that node among its nodes): in a baseline session, a top-level `N` holding an unedited child `N.c`, `N`'s own text edited before `create` and again after it — its baseline, `create`-time, and resolve-time subtree texts pairwise distinct — `N`'s `subtree-coherence` item resolved `no-change`, then `N`'s section deleted: the item presents its scope root `N` absent with `N`'s resolve-time subtree text, `N.c`'s text included, never the `create`-time derivation's nor the baseline's.
* **T10.7-13 Metadata pair.** 10.7: a `metadata-consistency` item's payload — `next --json`, and identically `show` and `export` — carries its scope node's metadata pair, a before/after pair under the origin-text rule: before, the node's `d` targets, coverage attribute, and tags in the item's baseline state; after, in the current graph; each `d` target under its current identity (10.4); a side whose graph state lacks the node presented absent, carrying no metadata. The coverage attribute and the tags take 12.7's value forms (H-3, T12.7-1): `"required"` or `"none"`, and a tag set in byte order; `d` targets compare as a set, 10.7 fixing no order for them. Fixtures (path-blocks), the targets named below being unedited sections: (a) At the baseline, `M`'s `d` names `T1` and `T2`, and `M` spells no `coverage` attribute and spells `tags="b a"`; one edit changes `M` to name `T2` and `T3`, spell `coverage="none"`, and spell `tags="c b"`: `M`'s item's context is exactly `{T1, T3}`, and its pair is before — `d` `{T1, T2}`, coverage `"required"`, tags `["a", "b"]` — and after — `d` `{T2, T3}`, coverage `"none"`, tags `["b", "c"]`. (b) A tags-only edit on `G` (`tags="x"` to `tags="y"`) and, on `H`, a coverage-only edit (no attribute to `coverage="none"`): each item's context is empty, so its pair alone shows the change — `G`'s sides differing in tags alone, `H`'s in coverage alone. (c) `K`, whose `d` names `T4` at the baseline, its reference removed before `create`; after `create`, `xspec rename` renames `T4` to `T9` (journaled): `K`'s context and its pair's before-side `d` targets present `T9`, never `T4`. (d) After (a)'s assertions, `M`'s section is deleted by manual edit, nothing referencing it: `M`'s item presents its pair's before side as in (a) and its after side absent.
* **T10.7-14 Refusal finding form.** A review-operation refusal (10.7) carries no stable code and neither location nor concerned path (14: `locations` empty, `path` `null`). Each refusal the suite stages — `create` naming an existing session, exactly and matching it ignoring ASCII case alone (T10.7-1, T10.1-2); `resolve` of a blocked item (T10.3-2, T10.7-10); and `split` of an item of another kind and of a `subtree-coherence` item whose scope root has no children (T10.7-9) — run with `--json`, exits 1 with standard output exactly `{"findings": [f]}` (12.7: a refused operation's findings-only form), `f` exactly `{"code", "message", "locations", "path", "identities"}` with `code` `null`, `locations` `[]`, `path` `null`, and `identities` an array of strings whose content is informational (12.7) and not asserted; the session file stays byte-identical. A product carrying the session file as the refusal's concerned path, or locating the refusal, fails.
* **T10.7-15 Outcome order past the gate.** 10.7: a `review` subcommand meets its outcomes in one order, each step reached only once every earlier one has passed — ahead of the gate the errors 12.0 orders before it, then the gate (T10.1-5, T13.3-3; (f)); past it, the session read, meeting the named session's corruption — at `list` the listing of the session directory and the read of every session in it, each corruption met there part of its answer, and at `create` the examination of the session directory (T10.1-6, T10.7-1; (f) stages both behind the gate and behind the errors ahead of it) — then an unknown item ID ((g) stages it behind the gate), then the recorded baseline, then the refusals, `resolve`'s of a blocked item and `split`'s of an item of another kind or of one whose scope root has no children. Each of these outcomes but `list`'s corruptions ends the subcommand there, modifying nothing, so stale graph data stays stale; each judgement turning on the graph — an item's blocked state, a scope root's children — is made against the current workspace and, in a baseline session, the recorded baseline, never against stale graph data (13.3); and only past all of them come the writes, the refresh of 13.3 and then a mutating subcommand's session file, a read subcommand answering only then, `list` included — a refresh's write failure met after every check (12.0). T12.0-10 pins the corruption's place before the item ID, for every corrupt state of T10.1-4, and (d) its place before the recorded baseline. Fixtures: (A) a spec source `specs/A.mdx` holding top-level `P` with a child `P.c`, `Q` with a child `Q.r`, and leaves `s` and `u`; on the built workspace an audit session `a` created and `Q`'s item split — giving `a` `Q`'s `parent-consistency` item (T10.7-9) — and a second session `x` made corrupt (T10.1-4's garbage-bytes staging). (B) A workspace in a git repository holding top-level `G` with a child `G.N`, and a leaf `v`; `G.N`'s text edited after a commit `c1`, then `review create --base c1 --name b` — `G`'s `parent-consistency` item blocked by `G.N`'s `subtree-coherence` item (10.5) — and the recorded baseline then made irreconstructible by T10.7-3's staging, the repository removed, the workspace otherwise passing `build`'s validations. Stale graph data is staged by editing a section's text — `u`'s in (A), `v`'s in (B) — with no rebuild and no read run since graph data was last written; each invocation below runs on its own copy of its staged state, T13.3-2's operational path set compared around it. (a) Stale stays stale (10.7): on the stale states, `resolve a <P's item> --status no-change` (blocked by `P.c`'s unresolved item), `split a <Q's parent-consistency item>`, and `split a <s's item>` (no children) each exit 1 with one code-less refusal in T10.7-14's form; `resolve a <unknown-id> --status no-change`, `show a <unknown-id>`, and `split a <unknown-id>` each exit 2, `code` `null`; `status x` and `resolve x <any-id> --status updated` each exit 1 with exactly the condition-21 finding; and `status b` exits 2 with the baseline's plain usage error, `code` `null` — each leaving graph data byte-identical, `check` afterwards still reporting the graph-data unit form beside the edited source's per-file staleness. A product refreshing before it reaches the outcome leaves graph data refreshed and fails each arm. (b) The refresh write comes after them (12.0: a write failure is met only at the write it refuses, a refresh's too): (a)'s invocations on the same stale states with the graph-data area also staged unwritable by T14-9's graph-data staging (as for T13.5-7(f)'s refreshing reads) — for `resolve` and `split`, applied while each is held at the seam of 13.5, after acquisition (T14-9) — each report exactly their outcome in (a), exit 1 with its finding or exit 2 with `code` `null`, never `write-failure`, release's deletions the staging refuses changing no outcome (13.5, T13.5-11(d)). (c) Graph judgements against the current workspace (10.7, 13.3), each on a freshly built twin of (A) holding its own audit session `a`, unsplit, then edited by hand, no rebuild and no read between the edit and the invocation, so graph data is stale when the subcommand runs: (i) `P.c`'s item resolved `no-change`, unblocking `P`'s, then `P.c`'s text edited, invalidating that item (10.4) and so re-blocking `P`'s (10.3): `resolve a <P's item> --status no-change` is refused at once, exit 1, T10.7-14's form, nothing modified — where a product judging the blocked state from stale graph data, in which `P.c`'s hashes still equal its recorded state, resolves it (T10.3-2 observes re-blocking through `status`, a refreshing read); (ii) `Q.r`'s section deleted, `Q` left childless: `split a <Q's item>` is refused, exit 1, nothing modified — where a product taking children from stale graph data splits it (T10.7-9's childless arm leaves the staleness at its `split` unpinned); (iii) a child `s.t` authored under the leaf `s`: `split a <s's item>` succeeds, exit 0, the session gaining `s.t`'s `subtree-coherence` item and `s`'s `parent-consistency` item, context exactly `{s.t}` and `blockedBy` exactly `s.t`'s item (10.7), graph data refreshed before the session write (`check` afterwards reporting the edited source's per-file staleness and no unit-form finding) — where a product taking children from stale graph data refuses it. (d) The recorded baseline's place (10.7; 12.0: one of the two usage-class checks past the gate): on (B)'s state, no section edited since `create`, `resolve b <G's parent-consistency item> --status no-change` — a blocked item — and `split b <G's parent-consistency item>` — an item of another kind — each exit 2 with the baseline's plain usage error, `code` and `path` `null`, never a refusal's exit 1, nothing modified, failing a product that judges the refusal before resolving the recorded baseline; on that state with `b`'s file corrupted by T10.1-4's `blockedBy`-cycle staging — parseable, its recorded parameters well-formed — `status b`, `resolve b <G.N's subtree-coherence item> --status no-change`, and `split b <G's parent-consistency item>` each exit 1 with exactly the condition-21 finding, nothing modified, never the baseline's exit 2 (10.7: the session read, meeting the corruption, precedes the recorded baseline; 10.1: a session violating an invariant is corrupt), failing a product that reconstructs the recorded baseline before validating the session's invariants; and once a source of (B) is edited to fail `build`'s validations, each of `status`, `next`, `show`, `export`, `resolve`, and `split` naming `b` — with an item ID where it takes one — reports exactly the gate's findings, exit 1, nothing modified, never the baseline's exit 2 (T10.7-3 stages the passing side alone), failing a product that resolves the recorded baseline before the gate. One step admits no discriminating staging and is recorded so (as T6.5-6 records its unstageable clauses): the unknown item ID's place before the recorded baseline — both plain usage errors, exit 2, `code` and `path` `null`, nothing modified, so the two orders differ only in message wording, which no test asserts (H-3). (e) `list`'s place (10.7: past the gate it lists the session directory and reads every session, its corruptions part of its answer, and it answers only past the refresh — T13.3-2's sweep): on (A)'s stale state, `review list` reports `x` corrupt beside `a`, exit 1, graph data refreshed (`check` afterwards reporting no unit-form finding), failing a product that lets the corruption end `list`; with the graph-data area also staged unwritable (T14-9's graph-data staging), it exits 2, `code` `"write-failure"`, `path` `".xspec"`, standard output exactly that error document — the corruption ending nothing; and with `.xspec/reviews` staged unlistable instead (T14-10(h)'s staging), it exits 2, `code` `"read-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/reviews"`, graph data byte-identical — every session read before the refresh — failing a product that refreshes first. (f) The session directory's place behind the gate and the errors ahead of it (10.7: ahead of the gate come the errors 12.0 orders before it, `create`'s given baseline among them, then the gate, whose failure ends the subcommand, no session read; only past it come `create`'s examination of the session directory — the existing-name refusal, the corruption reported in its place, and the obstruction of 14.22 — and `list`'s listing; 14.21: corruption is reported only on a workspace passing `build`'s validations; 14.22: the session directory's obstruction is never the gate's). (i) Behind the gate: on twins of (A) with `specs/A.mdx` given a section with no `id` (14.1), so that the workspace fails `build`'s validations, each invocation below reports exactly the gate's findings — that one condition-1 finding — exit 1, nothing modified, no session file written or changed: `review create --strategy audit --name a` (an existing session), `--name x` (a corrupt one), and `--name A` (matching `a` ignoring ASCII case alone, 10.1), never the code-less refusal or a condition-21 finding; on such a twin with `.xspec/reviews` replaced by a plain file, `review create --strategy audit --name n`, never condition 22 concerning `.xspec/reviews`; and on such a twin with `.xspec/reviews` staged unlistable (T14-10(h)'s staging), `review list` and `review create --strategy audit --name n`, never `read-failure` — failing a product that examines the session directory, or lists it, ahead of the gate. (ii) Behind the errors ahead of the gate: on a twin of (A) whose workspace is a git repository holding a commit `c1`, passing `build`'s validations, `review create --coverage nope --name a` (an unknown profile) and `review create --base nope --name a` (an unresolvable ref) each exit 2, `code` and `path` `null`, and, with a garbage line appended to the current journal (T6.3-6(a)), `review create --base c1 --name a` exits 2, `code` `"journal-error"`, `path` `".xspec/journal"` — each never the existing-name refusal's exit 1, nothing modified; and on that twin's stale state, staged as (A)'s, `review create --base nope --name a` exits 2 likewise, graph data byte-identical and still stale, `check` afterwards still reporting the graph-data unit form (10.7: a given baseline that cannot be resolved fails modifying nothing, at its place in the order) — failing a product that examines the session directory before those errors, or refreshes before resolving the given baseline. (g) The item ID's place behind the gate (12.0: the gated reads' argument checks precede the gate, all but a `review` item ID's, which runs past it; 10.7: the item ID follows the session read): on (f)(i)'s failing twin of (A), whose session `a` is well-formed, `show a <unknown-id>`, `resolve a <unknown-id> --status no-change`, and `split a <unknown-id>` each report exactly the gate's findings — that one condition-1 finding — exit 1, nothing modified, never the unknown-item exit 2 (`code` `null`) the same invocations give on (A)'s stale state, which passes `build`'s validations ((a)) — failing a product that judges the item ID with the other argument checks whenever the session parses, which T10.1-5's unparseable session, admitting no item check, cannot expose.

## 11. Query Surfaces

SPEC.md 11's five commands are JSON-only: for `query`, `occurrences`, `view`, `at`, and `inventory`, a single JSON document is the only output form, with or without `--json` — `occurrences`, `view`, `at`, and `inventory` in the form-exact document forms of 12.7 (H-3, T12.7-2), `query` carrying its defining section's information through H-3 adapters — its document shape unpinned, but its value-form data (source ranges above all) form-exact per 12.7's universal value forms (H-3, T12.7-1). Each surface's flag-less and `--json` invocations are asserted to carry the same information — byte-identity between the two forms is not asserted (SPEC.md does not require it) — and an exit-2 error of any of them arrives as the 12.7 error document on stdout (12.0; T12.0-2, T12.7-3).

### 11.1 `xspec query`

* **T11-1 node.** Returns identity, source range, own and subtree text (expanded, 1.6), all four hashes, tags, coverage attribute (absent for roots), and incoming and outgoing edges by kind.
* **T11-2 nodes.** Filters `--group`, `--file <glob>`, `--tag`, `--coverage` combine conjunctively; `--coverage` matches no root; each row carries identity, source range, tags, coverage attribute (absent for roots); the rows are requirement nodes, one per node (11.1), never code locations, 5.1's other node kind: on a workspace configuring spec groups `p` (`specs/**/*.mdx`) and `q` (`specs/shared/*.mdx`) over `specs/x.mdx` and `specs/shared/S.mdx` — the latter in both groups (7.1) — and a code group `app` (`src/*.ts`) over `src/x.ts` and `src/y.ts`, whose markers and `text(...)` calls, at top level and inside named units (a function `f`, a class `C`'s method `m`; 4.6), reference sections of both spec sources, unfiltered `query nodes` returns exactly the two spec sources' roots and sections, each once — no code location (`src/x.ts`, `src/x.ts#f`, `src/x.ts#C.m`, …) among the rows, and no node of `specs/shared/S.mdx` repeated for its second group — `--group p` returns both spec sources' nodes and `--group q` those of `specs/shared/S.mdx` alone, each node once; `--file 'src/*.ts'`, matching code sources alone, → exit 0 with no rows; and `--file '**/x.*'`, matching `specs/x.mdx` and `src/x.ts`, returns the nodes of `specs/x.mdx` alone — failing a product that lists code locations as rows, unfiltered or for a `--file` glob reaching code sources, and one that lists a file's nodes once per group holding it; a `--file` pattern outside the workspace root by spelling — `../x/*.mdx`, `a/../../x`, `/specs/*.mdx`, and `**/../x/*.mdx`, outside though its lexical normalization `x/*.mdx` is not — → exit 2 (invalid flag value, decided as 7 decides a configured glob, T7-4), while an inside pattern matching nothing (`./specs/*.mdx`, `specs//*.mdx`, over spec sources under `specs/` that each pattern's normalization matches) → exit 0 with no rows; `--group` naming a code group → exit 2 (invalid flag value — the wrong-kind group reference of 14.14, 11). `--tag` acceptance is syntactic (11.1, as `occurrences --to`): a well-formed tag no node carries matches nothing — exit 0, an empty row set — while a spelling no tag can have exits 2 as a malformed value: the empty string, a whitespace-bearing spelling (`'a b'`), `#`, a forbidden name (`then`), a control character, `"`, `'`, `\`, `&` (one arm each), and U+FFFD (12.0's argument-value rule) — each of the syntax class, reported without loading configuration (T12.0-10).
* **T11-3 subtree/ancestors.** `subtree` returns the node plus descendants in document order (root query returns the whole file); `ancestors` returns proper ancestors nearest-first ending at the file root, excluding the queried node (empty for a root); rows carry the row fields of T11-2 — identity, source range, tags, coverage attribute (11: one row contract for `nodes`, `subtree`, and `ancestors`) — asserted on `subtree` and `ancestors` rows including a tagged `coverage="none"` node and a root (attribute absent), so a product omitting a row field from either subcommand fails.
* **T11-4 edges.** `--from`/`--to` accept requirement nodes and code locations; `--kinds` filters over all four kinds and defaults to no filter (contains edges included); comma-separated list form read as a set (11.1): an unknown kind value → exit 2; an empty element — `--kinds depends,`, `--kinds ,depends`, `--kinds depends,,embeds` — → exit 2 (invalid flag value); a repeated element collapses — `--kinds depends,depends` answers byte-identically to `--kinds depends` (T12.0-14).
* **T11-5 reachable.** Reports existence of a dependency path under the given kinds (default: all three dependency kinds, never `contains`) and one shortest witness path with the 12.0 tie-break (two-equal-paths fixture); equal `--from` and `--to` (a node bearing both incoming and outgoing dependency edges) report that no path exists — a zero-length path is not a path (11); `--kinds contains` → exit 2 (invalid flag value: `reachable` accepts only the three dependency kinds, 11 — `edges` accepts all four, T11-4).
* **T11-6 Identity resolution.** Bare `path` resolves to a root node for a spec-group file and to a code location for a code-group file; `path#unit` and `path#unit@N` address code locations; a path in no configured group → exit 2 (unknown, 12.0). Wrong-kind operands (12.0): `query node`, `query subtree`, `query ancestors` (11.1: `<node>` is a requirement-node identity for all three), and `show` (12.4) given a code-group `path` or `path#unit` — a code source named where a requirement-node identity is required — each exit 2. Unknown code units (12.0: an unknown node identity named in arguments, the check judged parse-local over the named file's named units, 4.6): on a discovered code source, `query edges --from <path>#<unspelled-unit>` → exit 2 — likewise `edges --to` and `reachable --from`/`--to` given the same identity — and an out-of-range disambiguator, `<path>#<unit>@2` where the chain occurs once in the file (4.6), is equally unknown, exit 2 — as is `<path>#<unit>@1` at every occurrence count: 4.6 suffixes only occurrences after the first, so no occurrence bears `@1` — the first occurrence's identity is the bare `path#unit`, and identities compare byte-wise (12.0) — staged where the chain occurs once and where it occurs twice, the two-occurrence arm discriminating a product that resolves `@1` to the first occurrence: never a bare edgeless graph node with an empty answer, exit 0, and never a resolved answer (the failing-workspace arm, and the unparseable named file masking the check: T12.0-10). Unit names 1.4 forbids as ID segments (4.6; 12.0: a code unit is judged over the file's named units, never by 1.4's segment rules): `query edges --from <path>#C.constructor` — a class `C` whose constructor holds a marker (T4.6-1) — and `--from <path>#C.then`, a method named `then`, each answer with that unit's edges, exit 0; a product pre-validating every `<graph-node>` spelling with 1.4's forbidden-name rule, as `occurrences --to` applies it to requirement identities (T11.3-3), fails.
* **T11-7 Ordering.** Every result list is deterministic: repeated runs byte-identical; content-identical workspaces in different directories produce identical output (H-6).

### 11.2 Availability on imperfect files

Tests here drive `occurrences`, `view`, and `at`; the per-surface contracts are 11.3–11.5's, the availability rules this section's.

* **T11.2-1 Parse-local structure, per-file masking, no writes.** Three spec files: A parseable with findings of both levels — an unresolved `d` reference and a self-cycle (resolution-level); a duplicate-ID pair, a malformed segment, an unknown prop, an invalid construct (per-file structural) — B unparseable, C finding-free. `view` over all three: A's full positional tree, construct ranges, raw attribute spellings, comment ranges, and occurrence positions are all served — structure survives A's own findings and B's invalidity; B contributes no view, its parse-failure finding accompanying; C's view is complete. The workspace fails `build`, so the gated reads report findings without answering (T13.3-3) while these surfaces answer per file — and modify nothing: graph data and derived files byte-identical around each invocation (11.2; the passing-workspace counterpart participates in refresh, T13.3-2).
* **T11.2-2 Spelled identities and interpreted data.** One file, each node's identity datum asserted via `view`: exactly one quoted static `id` → defined; a repeated `id` (values agreeing, and disagreeing — one arm each), a braced `id={"x"}`, a valueless `id` (`<S id>`, T2.7-3's arm), and no `id` → each spells none, identity explicitly unavailable, the accompanying finding 14.17 for the three invalid forms — never 14.1 for the valueless one — and 14.1 for the absent one (T2.7-3, T1.3-1); two sections both spelling `x` → both unavailable, no winner, while a uniquely spelled `x.y` beneath one of them keeps its defined identity (a defined identity without defined prefix identities); descendants of a no-identity or malformed-identity section are undefined by inheritance; structural validity is a chain condition too (11.2: each spelled identity in the chain satisfies 1.3) — a well-formed, uniquely spelled `m.n` nested inside `k`, with its structurally consistent child `m.n.o`, and separately a top-level `q.r` with its child `q.r.s`: `m.n` and `q.r` unavailable (structurally invalid), `m.n.o` and `q.r.s` unavailable by inheritance, `k` defined, beside exactly one 14.2 finding per invalid bearer (none for `m.n.o` or `q.r.s`, each conforming to its parent's spelling, T1.3-2), and same-file `d={"m.n"}` and `{text("m.n.o")}` report 14.5 and 14.6, recording no occurrence — failing a product that resolves through every well-formed, uniquely spelled ID and ignores 1.3; a section uniquely spelling `z` stays defined beside another section's invalid-form `id` attributes (uniqueness compares spelled identities only — an invalid form contests nothing). Interpreted tags and coverage: absent props define the defaults (no tags, coverage-required); a repeated, malformed, or invalid-valued `tags`/`coverage` leaves the interpreted value unavailable, its raw spelling still listed (T11.4-3).
* **T11.2-3 Invalid paths.** (Linux leg) A discovered spec source `a#b.mdx` and a non-UTF-8-named one (14.19): every node identity in each — root included — is explicitly unavailable while tree, ranges, and attributes stay on view; the condition-19 finding accompanies every answer whose domain includes the file; no identity over the invalid path is ever emitted, the non-UTF-8 path itself presented in the marked byte form (12.0, T12.7-1). A code source with `#` in its path defines no identity for its whole-file location or any unit: its spellings still record occurrences, each record's `source` explicitly unavailable (5.7, T11.3-1). Root identity is defined exactly when the file's path is valid. Target side (11.2: a spelling resolves only to a section whose node identity is defined, or, in module form, to a root whose identity is defined — its path valid; 1.5: no identity is resolved against over an invalid path): a discovered `specs/a'b.mdx` (7.1's bar, T7.1-1) holding a section `x` is imported by a spec source `specs/A.mdx` as `import B from "./a'b.xspec"` and by a code source as `import B, { text } from "../specs/a'b.xspec"` — each import valid, its target discovered (2.1, 4), `view specs/A.mdx` listing the import with target `specs/a'b.mdx`, a path and never an identity (T11.4-4). `specs/A.mdx` spells `d={B.x}`, `d={B}`, and `{text(B.x)}`, the code source the marker `B.x` and the call `text(B.x)`: `build` and `check` report 14.5 for each `d` reference, 14.6 for the embedding, and 14.7 for the marker and for the call, beside `specs/a'b.mdx`'s condition 19, exit 1; no edge and no occurrence — `occurrences` lists none for the five spellings, every one of those findings accompanying, and no record of any file carries a `target` over the invalid path — failing a product that resolves into the invalid-path file and emits `specs/a'b.mdx#x` or `specs/a'b.mdx` as an identity. On the same workspace `occurrences --to "specs/a'b.mdx#x"` and `occurrences --to "specs/a'b.mdx"` are accepted — well-formed spellings (11.3) — and each selects nothing, the domain's findings accompanying, exit 1, never exit 2 (T11.3-3).
* **T11.2-4 Resolution and expanded text.** Resolution turns on the referenced identity's own definedness: with duplicate spellings of `a` and a unique `a.b` beneath one bearer, a reference to `a.b` resolves and records its occurrence while a reference to `a` records none — ambiguous, every bearer undefined — reported by its finding's range, never as a record or an unavailable target. Source-side unavailability (5.7, 11.2): resolving spellings themselves live in undefined-identity sections — a `d` entry naming `a.b` on the other duplicate bearer of `a`, and a `{text("a.b")}` embedding inside a section spelling no identity (`id` absent) — and each still records its occurrence: the record carries `file`, its own `range`, `kind`, and `target` (`a.b`), with `source` exactly the unavailability marker — identity and range withheld together as one datum (12.7; enumerated so in T11.3-1), never a picked bearer's identity and never a dropped record — while the view still positions each enclosing construct, its identity unavailable (11.4, T11.2-2); the file's findings — the duplicate-`id` and missing-`id` conditions among them — accompany, exit 1. Expanded text via `view --text`: a chain A embeds B embeds C with an unresolved embedding in C → A's and B's own/subtree text unavailable (one unresolved spelling on the expansion path, or one embedding cycle — staged separately — poisons the whole value; partial expansion never occurs), sibling nodes with resolved expansions staying defined and byte-exact. Own text and subtree text are judged apart (11.2: own text consults the embeddings of the node's own contribution, subtree text those anywhere in its subtree, each recursively through every embedded target's subtree): a section `p` whose own contribution holds no embedding, with a child section `p.c` holding the unresolved `{text("missing")}` — and, staged separately, `p.c` holding `{text("p")}` instead, so that expanding `p`'s subtree re-enters `p` (an embedding cycle; 14.9, 5.3: a section embedding its own ancestor) — under `view --text`: `p`'s own text a plain value, byte-equal to its precomputed own text (`p.c`'s contribution excised, 1.6), and its subtree text explicitly unavailable; `p.c`'s own and subtree text unavailable; the file's findings accompanying, exit 1 — failing a product that marks both values unavailable whenever an embedding anywhere beneath the node fails. Removal classification is by syntactic form — after deleting an imported file, the importing file's text values are byte-identical to before (the import removed by form, its 14.15 finding notwithstanding), and a stray element (14.16) is content, preserved byte-for-byte in the enclosing text and located by its finding. Enclosure (11.2: a stray element is preserved by its own tags, the sections and embeddings it encloses classified by their own forms): `<div><S id="x">t</S>{text("y")}</div>` staged in flow position at the root level, `y` a section of the same file — exactly one condition-16 finding, `<div>` through `</div>`; node `x` in the view's tree as a child of the root (T11.4-1); the embedding's occurrence recorded with the root as its `source`; and under `view --text` the root's own text carrying `<div>` and `</div>` with `x`'s whole contribution excised (1.6) and the embedding expanded to `y`'s subtree text, its subtree text carrying `x`'s text `t` in place with `x`'s tags removed — each byte-asserted.
* **T11.2-5 Domain, findings, exits.** `view` naming only C (T11.2-1's finding-free file) → finding-free, exit 0, while A and B stay invalid — the domain is the requested files; naming A → A's findings of both levels accompany, exit 1, the full answer still emitted (the document complete and parseable, H-5); a two-file cycle accompanies whole when either participant is in the domain (14.9). Any finding or explicitly-unavailable datum → exit 1 with the full answer; complete and finding-free → exit 0; argument checks precede answering — unknown `<file>`, wrong-kind `<file>`, invalid glob, malformed `--to`, out-of-range offset each exit 2 whatever findings the named files carry (per-surface arms in T11.3-2/3, T11.4-2, T11.5-2, and T11.5-3 — the out-of-range offset on unparseable files).
* **T11.2-6 Never stale, gate findings never attach.** On a passing workspace, `occurrences`, `view`, and `at` participate in read-time refresh exactly as 13.3's reads (T13.3-2 covers them in its sweep); on a failing one they answer from current sources and write nothing (T11.2-1; T13.3-3 stages the journal-error and refused-write failures stale). A gate condition that is no domain file's finding accompanies no answer: with a garbage journal line (14.13) staged, and separately an obstructed write path (14.22), `view` of a finding-free file answers finding-free, exit 0 — those states surface through `build`, `check`, and the gated reads (13.3), never these answers. An obstructing component that is itself a discovered file attaches nothing (11.2): with `markdown: { emit: true, outDir: "specs/A.mdx" }` — every emit destination lying below the discovered spec source `specs/A.mdx`, a plain file — `build` and `check` report condition 22 concerning `specs/A.mdx`, exit 1, while `view specs/A.mdx` answers finding-free, exit 0. Findings of no domain file attach nothing on the passing side either (11.2: a finding is a domain file's only through a location in that file or a condition-19 concerned path): on a workspace passing `build`'s validations that violates a `forbidden` rule (T7.5-2's staging, the offending edge's `d` spelling in a spec source `specs/A.mdx`), and separately one holding a corrupt session (T10.1-4's garbage-bytes staging), `check` reports 14.12, respectively 14.21, exit 1, while `occurrences`, `view specs/A.mdx`, and `at specs/A.mdx 0` each answer finding-free, exit 0 — a policy finding is no file's finding and accompanies no answer of 11.3–11.5 (14.12), and a session file is no domain file (14.21) — failing a product that attaches every finding lacking an in-source location to these answers (the inventory's counterpart: T11.6-4). The judgement's reads (11.2: past their argument checks these surfaces judge `build`'s validations exactly as the gate of 13.3 does, making every read it makes whatever findings the sources carry — the journal's (14.13) and the kinds of `build`'s write-path directory components (14.22) included — and a read failure of that judgement stops them, a usage error before any answer; T14-10(l)(i) refuses the journal's read on both sides alike): under `markdown: { emit: true, outDir: "out" }`, no configured glob reaching a path under `out`, so that discovery never lists it (7), the directory `out`, holding a prior `build`'s emission, is staged with read and search permission both removed (mode `000`; the harness verifying on itself, as T14-10 does, that its own listing of `out` and its own examination of `out/specs` are refused), so the kind of `out/specs`, a directory component of every emit destination, can be read neither by examination nor from a listing. On a workspace whose sources also fail validation — `specs/A.mdx` holding a section with no `id` (14.1) — `view specs/A.mdx`, `occurrences`, and `at specs/A.mdx 0` each exit 2, `code` `"read-failure"`, `path` `out` or a path below it — which one turning on the product's read order (T14-10's last clause) — standard output exactly the error document and no answer, nothing modified; a product skipping the judgement on the failing side answers instead, exit 1, its answer carrying the condition-1 finding. The same holds on the passing side, and for `build`, `check`, and the gated reads (`ids` and `query nodes` as representatives) on either side, each exiting 2 with that error, nothing modified. The judgement's reads come past every argument check (11.2; 13.3: the gate follows the argument checks; 12.0: a read failure is met at the read it refuses, in the order the command makes its reads), and no argument check reads below `out`: on the same staging — its configuration holding, beside the spec group, a code group `app` (`src/**/*.ts`, reaching no path under `out`) over `src/x.ts` — on the passing and the failing side alike, each of `view specs/nope.mdx` (an undiscovered file, 11.4), `view src/x.ts` (a wrong-kind operand), `at specs/A.mdx <n>` with `<n>` the file's byte length plus one (11.5), `show specs/A.mdx#nope` (an `id` the file spells nowhere, 12.0), `query nodes --group app` (a code group's name, 11.1), `coverage <unknown-profile>`, and `review status <unknown-session>` exits 2 with its usage error, `code` and `path` `null`, never `read-failure`, nothing modified — failing a product that judges the workspace, write-path kinds included, before its argument checks. Once the permission is restored, `build` on the passing side exits 0 and `check` is clean.
* **T11.2-7 Removal by form.** 11.2 classifies the removals of expanded text by syntactic form, never by validity: every import declaration is removed whatever its binding shape, specifier validity, and target discovery; a section tag with every attribute it spells; and an export statement, matching no removal rule's form, is content (T11.2-4 stages the deleted-target import and the stray element, T2.3-3 the invalid containers).
