The gate is over every finding a `build` would report, source validity or not (13.3: source validation errors, journal errors, and refused writes alike): on an otherwise-valid workspace with a garbage journal line staged, and separately with an obstructed write path staged (T11.2-6's two fixtures, each holding an `audit` session created while the workspace was valid — no baseline to resolve, so the gate alone stands between the invocation and an answer; a baseline session yields the same exit 1 only by 13.3's rule that no session is read on a failing workspace, T10.1-5), each of `ids`, `show`, `coverage`, `review status`, and `query` — and, in the obstructed-write staging alone, `impact --base` against a commit taken before the obstruction was staged, its baseline resolving and 14.22 the operative gate finding — reports that finding — the journal error (14.13) naming the line, its `path` `".xspec/journal"` and `locations` `[]` (T6.1-3), the refused write (14.22) its offending component — exits 1, answers nothing, and modifies nothing (journal, sessions, derived files, and graph data byte-identical), discriminating a product that gates on source validity alone and answers `query` from a broken journal with exit 0 (refresh consumes the journal for canonical identities, 5.4); beside a source validation error the gate reports each of these findings with it (T14-1). `impact` is absent from the journal-error staging by necessity, not oversight: `impact` always takes `--base` (9), and a given baseline's resolution, which precedes the gate (12.0), reads the current journal before any other cause can fail it, a journal error there failing it as a usage error that carries condition 13's code and concerned path — exit 2, `journal-error`, never the gate's exit 1 (6.3; 12.0: a given baseline is never so masked; T6.3-6) — so no journal-error staging reaches this gate at `impact`, nor at `review create --base`. The never-gated contrast: on the same failing workspaces `occurrences`, `view`, and `at` answer per file under 11.2 (T11.2-1, T11.2-6) and `inventory` answers whatever the sources' validity (T11.6-4) — none of them modifying anything. Graph data being current on those two stagings, a refresh there would rewrite nothing, so each is also staged stale (13.3, 11.2: on a workspace failing `build`'s validations, journal errors and refused writes alike, nothing is modified — all graph data byte-for-byte as it was): on a twin of each — a section's text edited, validly, after graph data was last written, no read run since, and in the write-path twin the obstruction placed on a write path other than graph data's own: under `markdown: { emit: true, outDir: "out" }`, built, the directory `out` replaced by a plain file, a directory component of every emit destination — each read run above on that staging and each of `occurrences`, `view`, and `at` gives its outcome above and leaves T13.3-2's operational path set byte-identical around it, failing a product that decides whether to refresh by source validity alone. The mutating `review` subcommands meet the refused-write gate alike, past their acquisition (13.5, 10.7): on the write-path twin, its `audit` session in place, `review create --strategy audit` under a new name, `review resolve` naming an unblocked item, and `review split` naming an item whose scope root has children each exit 1 reporting exactly the condition-22 finding concerning `out` — no session created, the existing session, journal, derived files, and graph data byte-identical, the stale graph data unrefreshed — failing a product whose mutating subcommands gate on source validity alone and write the session (T6.4-6 stages `rename` and `move` on the same obstruction).
* **T13.3-4 Determinism.** Graph data files are byte-deterministic across rebuilds of an identical workspace (content otherwise unasserted, H-4).
* **T13.3-5 Never stale: tampered graph data, added sources, and a changed journal.** 13.3: on a workspace passing `build`'s validations, a refreshing read answers from graph data that is not stale — matching the graph data `build` would now write from the current sources, configuration, and journal, a comparison of content — refreshing it where it is stale, and `check`, which never refreshes, reports by the same comparison what does not match (12.2, 14.10). Every staging of T13.3-2 also changes or destroys whatever record of its inputs graph data might carry — the data deleted, an existing source edited, the whole set garbled — and so does T12.2-2's mismatch arm, its graph data refreshed for an edit then reverted, so a product answering from stored graph data, or a `check` judging it current, whenever a digest of its recorded inputs matches, passes them, as does one re-reading only the sources its graph data lists, which P-14, accepting the answer with its flipped source present or absent, cannot tell apart. Two stagings, (a) and (b), on T13.3-2's workspace, freshly built and valid, each read — T13.3-2's `ids`, `show`, `coverage`, `impact`, `review status`, `review list`, `query`, `occurrences`, `view`, and `at` — and `check` run under `--json` alone on its own copy of the staged state, since a refresh repairs the staging for every later read: (a) Partial tampering, shape-blind (H-3): each trial alters one file of T13.3-2's operational path set at a seeded offset (H-10; a fixed seed set in CI, E-5) — a single byte changed to a seeded other value, or the file truncated there — every nonempty file of the set altered in some trial; and, where the product writes several files there, further trials mix files from two product-written states, the workspace's own and a twin's built after one section's text was edited, each path of the two states' union taking its file, or its absence, from either state by seed, the mixes together taking every two paths at which the states differ one from each state, both ways round. Each read's exit code and standard output equal its run's on the untampered workspace, byte for byte. `check`'s verdict is predicted in every trial, single-file alterations and mixes alike, from what the staged state yields to two observations, never from the staging itself: the record's own files lie at unenumerated paths, which may differ between the two states, and a lagging or absent record is never staleness (13.3, 14.10, 14.23) — so a mix departing from the workspace's own state only where the record lies, leaving it absent or readable and the rest the workspace's own, has `check` clean, and a mix taking the twin's file at a path both states hold may go either way, as that file holds compared graph data or record alone (H-4). The observations, each on its own copy of the staged state: whether the reads rewrite anything in T13.3-2's operational path set — each read's run leaving that set byte-identical or not, every read alike, since each refreshes by 13.3's one comparison exactly when graph data does not match, writing what `build` would write and leaving the record unchanged — and `inventory`'s `recorded` (11.6: the record read without refresh). `check` then reports exactly one condition-10 finding in the unit form, `path` `".xspec"`, `locations` `[]`, when `recorded` is unavailable — the record unreadable (14.23), its form alone — or the reads rewrote the set — the comparison failed, `check` judging by the same comparison (14.10); beside it, where `recorded` is a list, one recorded-file-form finding concerning each listed path outside the `recorded` list the untampered workspace's `inventory` reports — the paths the current sources generate — whose occupant 13.4's removal would remove; and nothing else, the derived files being the workspace's own, matching — exit 1 when it reports any finding, and 0 otherwise. (b) A source added after the build, no existing file edited: a new valid spec source, and separately a new code source a configured code group matches, importing an existing spec source's module and holding a marker on one of its sections (4.5), so that its presence changes `occurrences`' answer — each read's exit code and standard output equal those of the same read on a twin on which `build` ran once the source was added, `view` and `at` also run, in the spec-source arm, naming the new source; and `check` reports the findings `check` reports on that twin and, beside them, exactly one condition-10 finding per derived path at which the staged workspace's occupant differs from the twin's file — the new spec source's module, each companion the twin's `recorded` lists for it, and its Markdown where emitted, all absent, among them — and one in the unit form, graph data omitting the added source, exit 1. (c) A journal changed alone, staged on a workspace of its own (13.3: a journal changed alone can be staleness, canonical identities and so hashes depending on it, 5.4, 5.5): on a freshly built, valid workspace, `xspec rename specs/A.mdx a b` renames a top-level section `a` to `b` — journaled, its finishing regeneration leaving the workspace current (6.4) — and `.xspec/journal` is then deleted by hand, sources and configuration untouched; the workspace stays valid, its journal empty (6.1), and `b`'s canonical identity becomes (`b`, the journal's start) where it was (`a`, the journal's start), so the root of `specs/A.mdx`, whose own content enters `b` by canonical identity, changes its ownHash (5.4, 5.5). `check` reports exactly one condition-10 finding in the unit form, `path` `".xspec"`, `locations` `[]`, and beside it exactly one per-file finding per derived path whose occupant differs from the one a `build` leaves on a copy of this state — the harness computing that set by comparison, as T13.5-7(b) does, never assuming whether generated content depends on the journal (13.4: derived files are reproducible from sources, configuration, and the journal) — and nothing else, exit 1; a product comparing graph data with the sources and configuration alone reports no unit form. On another copy of the state, a refreshing read answers from refreshed data: `query node specs/A.mdx` reports the ownHash that `query node specs/A.mdx` reports on a twin holding the same sources and no journal, built there — an ownHash differing from the one it reported before the deletion — and `check` afterwards reports no unit-form finding, its per-file findings as before.

### 13.4 Derived, durable, and transient files

* **T13.4-1 Plain committable files.** Every file xspec writes is a plain file; writing the workspace into git and back (commit, clean checkout) round-trips builds and reads. Sorted keys (13.4): every JSON object in a product-written session file — read after `create` and again after a `resolve` rewrites it (10.4) — has its keys in byte-sorted order — 13.4 leaves "sorted" unqualified; the assertion pins it, recorded as an interpretive pin, to byte order as the sole string order SPEC.md defines (12.0), so a product sorting keys under any other deterministic collation fails by intent (a pin non-discriminating over ASCII keys, where byte, UTF-16 code-unit, and code-point orders coincide: it adds no requirement in practice unless a product keys an object by non-ASCII strings — recorded so the interpretive label is not read as a new requirement) — asserted shape- and value-blind over whatever objects and keys are present (H-3: no shape or values pinned). The session file is the one written class where the clause is discriminable: journal entry content (6.1) and graph data (13.3) are opaque, and generated TypeScript and Markdown carry no keyed serialization; the sibling stable-ordering clause is covered by the determinism protocol (H-6, T10.1-1, T12.0-7, T13.3-4).
* **T13.4-2 Derived reproducibility.** Delete, truncate, and garbage-overwrite each class of derived file (module, companion, Markdown, graph data): `build` restores all byte-exactly.
* **T13.4-3 Orphan knowledge boundary.** Build so a derived file exists and is recorded; delete all graph data (T13.3-2's operational definition), taking the recorded derived-file paths with it; change the configuration so that file is no longer generated; `build`: the orphaned file is outside xspec's knowledge and is not removed; it may be deleted manually (asserted: subsequent builds leave the stray file alone). The unreadable half of the same boundary (13.4: a record missing or unreadable, 14.23): with the record instead corrupted shape-blind (T6.6-6's staging) before the configuration change, `build` replaces the record (14.23, 13.3) and leaves the now-orphaned file in place, subsequent builds leaving it alone likewise.
* **T13.4-4 Derived paths belong to xspec.** A user-created file at a derived path is replaced by `build`; a symbolic link at a derived file's own path is replaced as the occupant — nothing is written through it (link target byte-identical after build, link gone, plain file present; not an error); and a directory at a derived path holding nothing xspec discovers or generates — empty, and separately holding a file no group matches — is replaced by the derived file, `build` exiting 0 with a plain file there and `check` clean (13.4: writing a derived file replaces whatever exists at its path, 14.22's refusal reaching only a path that is a directory component of a discovered source's path or of another derived path, T13.4-9).
* **T13.4-5 Durable protection.** `build` and read commands never modify or delete the journal or session files (byte-compare); durable files are never regenerated (deleting a session file: xspec does not recreate it; review naming it → exit 2 unknown session). `rename` and `move` never write a session file either (13.4: a durable file is modified only by its owning commands, a session's being the mutating `review` subcommands, 10.4; a rename's or move's writes are its source edits, its journal append, and its finishing regeneration, 6.4, 13.5). Fixture: a git repository whose baseline commit holds `specs/A.mdx`, with top-level sections `a`, holding `a.x`, and `s`, and `specs/B.mdx`, with a top-level section `t`; `a.x`'s text is then edited. Three sessions reference, through their items, recorded presences, and decomposition, the nodes the operations below re-identify: a path-blocks session (`--base` the baseline commit) with `a.x`'s `subtree-coherence` item resolved `no-change`; an audit session with `a`'s item split (a decomposition recorded at `a`) and `s`'s item resolved `no-change`; and a `coverage` session whose profile's required set, the spec group's leaves `a.x`, `s`, and `t`, is uncovered, `a.x`'s item resolved `no-change`. `xspec rename specs/A.mdx a b`, then the file-form `xspec move specs/A.mdx specs/C.mdx`, then the section-form `xspec move specs/C.mdx#s specs/B.mdx#t.s` — each exiting 0 and journaled (T6.1-1) — leave every session file byte-identical to its bytes just before the operation, while reads afterwards present the recorded nodes under their current identities (10.4, T10.4-4): `a.x`'s as `specs/C.mdx#b.x`, `s`'s as `specs/B.mdx#t.s`, and the audit session's recorded decomposition at `specs/C.mdx#b`. A product writing the mapped identities into session files at a `rename` or `move`, rather than mapping recorded identities forward when a session is read, presents the same identities and fails here.
* **T13.4-6 Obstructed write paths.** A write whose path has a symbolic link at a workspace-relative directory component is refused before anything is modified (14.22; command exits 1 with the report, workspace byte-identical); `check` reports the same without writing; occupant kinds (14.22: a plain file, a symbolic link whatever it targets, or any other non-directory occupant) — a plain file occupying a directory component of a `build` write path, a first emission's `outDir` component with no move operand involved (a plain-file or symbolic-link component under a move's destination or its derived paths reports `refused-invalid-destination` instead, T6.5-4, T14-7), is refused identically: `build` exits 1 with the condition-22 finding, concerned path that component, modifying nothing, and `check` reports it without writing; finding cardinality (14.22: one finding per distinct offending component, whatever write paths it refuses) — one non-directory occupant at a component under which two derived files would be written yields one finding, concerned path that component, and two distinct offending components yield two findings (asserted via `check`); a durable path occupied by a symlink or non-plain file → journal error (14.13) / corrupt session (14.21) — never read, appended, or replaced (link and target byte-identical after the attempt). Positive counterpart: path components above the workspace root are unrestricted (13.4) — a workspace whose absolute path traverses a symbolic link above its root builds, mutates, and `check`s normally. The graph-data area's own path and the session directory occupied by a non-directory are T10.1-6's arms (14.22 concerning `.xspec`; `review create`'s finding concerning `.xspec/reviews`).
* **T13.4-7 Source exclusion.** T7-6 covers `.xspec.`/`.xspec/`/emit-destination exclusion from groups.
* **T13.4-8 Writes create missing directories.** A missing intermediate directory never refuses or fails a write: the nonexistent workspace-relative directory components of a written path come into existence as directories (13.4), each named case staged with its directories absent beforehand and asserted present as real directories afterward — a file-form move to `new/deep/b.mdx` (destination in a configured spec group, `new/` absent) succeeds, the moved file and its regenerated derived files under the fresh directories; a section-form move whose created target file (6.5) lies under an absent directory succeeds likewise; a first emission under a nested nonexistent `markdown.outDir` (7.3) writes every destination, creating the chain.
* **T13.4-9 Derived paths above sources and other derived paths.** 13.4 and 14.22 refuse, before any write, a module, companion, or emitted Markdown path that is a directory component of a discovered source's path or of another such path xspec writes, occupied or not — the plain file written there would leave the other write no directory, or replace a directory holding a source. Six stagings — (e) a pair per companion path — each on a workspace otherwise passing `build`'s validations, the first five staged before any build, so nothing but a directory occupies the offending path (T13.4-6's occupied-component relation kept apart), and (f) where that relation and this one meet at one component: (a) emission next to sources, `specs/a.mdx` beside `specs/a.md/b.mdx` — `a.mdx`'s emit path `specs/a.md` a directory component of a discovered source's path (and of `b.mdx`'s derived paths); (b) emission under `markdown.outDir: "out"`, sources `a.mdx` and `a.md/b.mdx` at the workspace root — the emit path `out/a.md` a directory component of the emit path `out/a.md/b.md`; (c) `specs/A.mdx` beside a discovered `specs/A.xspec.ts/B.mdx` (its file name holding no `.xspec.`, so no exclusion applies, 13.4) — the module path `specs/A.xspec.ts` a directory component of a source's path; (d) a code source the only file beneath — emission next to sources, `specs/a.mdx` beside a discovered code source `specs/a.md/x.ts` (a code group globbing `specs/**/*.ts`, the file holding `export const v = 1`) — the emit path `specs/a.md` a directory component of that source's path and of no derived path, a code source generating none, whereas (a) and (c) each place a spec source, and so its derived paths, beneath the offending path, where the relation between derived paths refuses it by itself; (e) the companion leg, one pair of stagings per companion path a build of `specs/A.mdx` records — none for a product writing no companions — the paths read from `inventory`'s `recorded` set after a build of a scratch twin holding, under the staging's configuration, `specs/A.mdx`'s bytes at that path alone (11.6; T11.6-3: the recorded entries `specs/A.xspec.<suffix>` other than the module `specs/A.xspec.ts`, each companion attributable to its source through 13.1's naming; the suffixes are the product's own, as T6.6-5 composes them): `specs/A.mdx` beside a discovered `specs/A.xspec.<suffix>/B.mdx`, as (c) stages the module path, and, separately, beside a discovered code source `specs/A.xspec.<suffix>/c.ts`, the only file beneath, as (d) stages the emit path (a code group globbing `specs/**/*.ts`, the file holding `export const v = 1`) — neither file name holding `.xspec.`, so no exclusion applies (13.4), and the companion path a directory component of a source's path, of `B.mdx`'s derived paths as well in the first; (f) the staging of (b) after a `build` of `a.mdx` alone, `a.md/b.mdx` added after it — `out/a.md` is then the plain file that build emitted, occupying a directory component of the write path `out/a.md/b.md` (T13.4-6's relation) while being the derived path the relation between derived paths names, one offending component under both (14.22). In each, `build` exits 1 with exactly one condition-22 finding concerning the offending derived path — `specs/a.md`, `out/a.md`, `specs/A.xspec.ts`, `specs/a.md`, (e)'s companion path, and `out/a.md`: one finding per distinct offending path, whatever write paths it refuses and whichever relations it meets — `locations` `[]`, and writes and removes nothing (workspace byte-compare); `check` reports the same finding without writing, and a gated read (`ids`, T13.3-3) reports it, exit 1, answering nothing — failing a product that vets occupied components alone and writes the file over the directory's path; by (d), one that vets derived paths against one another and against sources for equality alone, writing `specs/a.md` over the directory and so deleting `x.ts` behind a reported success (13.4: writing a derived file replaces whatever exists at its path); by (e), one whose relations take module and Markdown paths but leave out companions, writing the companion over the directory and so deleting `B.mdx` or `c.ts` alike; and, by (f), one vetting the two relations in separate passes, which reports a finding from each.
* **T13.4-10 Rebuild-obstructing orphans.** An orphan, recorded or not, occupying a workspace-relative directory component of a path the rebuild writes obstructs that write (13.4, 14.22): the rebuild is refused before any write or removal, and the orphan stays until it is deleted manually (13.5: one of the two kinds of orphan 13.4 leaves for manual deletion, which a rerun `build` does not resolve — the other the unrecorded orphan of T13.4-3). Staging: `build` with `markdown: { emit: true, outDir: "out" }` and source `specs/A.mdx`, recording the emitted `out/specs/A.md`; then `outDir` reconfigured to `"out/specs/A.md"`, so the new emit path `out/specs/A.md/specs/A.md` lies below the recorded, no-longer-generated `out/specs/A.md`. `build` exits 1 with exactly one condition-22 finding concerning `out/specs/A.md` and modifies nothing — the orphan, every other derived file, and graph data byte-identical; `check` exits 1 reporting that finding and, beside it, exactly one condition-10 finding in the recorded-file form concerning `out/specs/A.md` whose correction is the file's manual deletion, never a rebuild (14.10; the correction asserted by H-3's robust matching), and no mismatch form — undetectable on a workspace failing `build`'s validations (14.10, T12.2-4); once the file is deleted by hand, `build` exits 0, writing `out/specs/A.md/specs/A.md`, and `check` is clean. The unrecorded twin: the same staging with graph data deleted before the reconfiguration (T13.3-2's operational definition), the orphan now outside xspec's knowledge (T13.4-3), obstructs identically — `build` exits 1 with the one condition-22 finding, modifying nothing — and `check` reports that finding alone: no record lists the path, so no recorded-file finding, and the missing graph data is a mismatch form, undetectable there (14.10).
* **T13.4-11 Removing recorded paths no longer generated.** 13.4 removes a recorded derived path the current sources and configuration no longer generate only where its occupant is neither a directory nor a discovered source — a symbolic link as the link itself — and leaves a path holding a directory, a discovered source, or nothing as it is, making no write; 14.10's recorded-file form reports exactly the occupants that removal would remove. Each arm builds with emission next to sources, stages a change leaving the recorded `specs/A.md` no longer generated together with its occupant, then runs `check`, `build`, and `check` again: (a) a directory — `specs/A.md` replaced by a directory holding a file, then emission disabled: the first `check` reports no condition-10 finding concerning `specs/A.md` — whether the graph-data unit form accompanies it is not asserted: the comparison of 13.3 excludes the record, and graph data's content beyond its enumeration is opaque, so whether disabling emission leaves graph data stale is unpinned (13.3, 14.10) — `build` exits 0 leaving the directory and its content byte-identical, and the last `check` is clean; (b) a discovered source — `specs/A.md` overwritten with well-formed TypeScript (`export const n = 1`) as emission is disabled and a code group globbing `specs/*.md` is added, so the path is a discovered code source once it is no emit destination: no condition-10 finding concerning it, `build` exits 0 with the file byte-identical (a source is never derived), the last `check` clean; (c) a symbolic link — `specs/A.md` replaced by a link to a file outside the workspace, then emission disabled: the first `check` reports the condition-10 recorded-file finding concerning `specs/A.md`, `build` exits 0 with the link itself gone and its target byte-identical, the last `check` clean; (d) nothing to remove — built instead under `outDir: "out"`, recording `out/specs/A.md`, then `out/specs` replaced by a plain file and `outDir` changed to `"md"`: the recorded path lies below a non-directory component and holds nothing (13.4: nothing is read there), so no condition-10 finding concerns it, and `build` exits 0 — no 14.22, the path being no write path's component, and no 14.24, the removal making no write — `out/specs` byte-identical, the last `check` clean; (e) nothing to remove below a symbolic link — (d)'s staging with `out/specs` replaced instead by a symbolic link to a real directory holding a foreign plain file named `A.md`, staged once with that directory inside the workspace, under no group's globs, and once outside the workspace root: the recorded path lies below a component a symbolic link occupies, where nothing is read whatever the link targets (13.4), so the first `check` reports no condition-10 finding concerning `out/specs/A.md`, `build` exits 0 — no 14.22, the link being no write path's component, and no 14.24 — the link, its target directory, and the target's `A.md` byte-identical afterward, and the last `check` clean; a product resolving the recorded path's parent through the link reports the foreign file as a stale recorded file and deletes it, inside or outside the workspace, behind a reported success; (f) no occupant — `specs/A.md` deleted, then emission disabled: the recorded path holds nothing (13.4), so the first `check` reports no condition-10 finding concerning `specs/A.md` (whether the graph-data unit form accompanies it unasserted, as in (a)), `build` exits 0 — no 14.24, the removal making no write — leaving nothing at `specs/A.md`, and the last `check` is clean; a product whose removal fails on the vacant path, reporting 14.24, or whose recorded-file form reports a recorded path holding nothing, fails. Order independence (13.4: a completed regeneration's outcome does not depend on the order of its writes and removals): `build` with a source `specs/B.md/C.mdx`, recording `specs/B.md/C.md` and `C.mdx`'s module and companions; then `specs/B.md/C.mdx` deleted and `specs/B.mdx` added, whose emit path `specs/B.md` is the directory holding those recorded orphans: `build` exits 0 — the write replacing the directory, each orphan's removal finding nothing below the replaced path or removing its file first alike — and the workspace is exactly the regenerated one, `specs/B.md` a plain file holding `B.mdx`'s Markdown and nothing under it, `check` clean; a product failing a removal whose path's component a write replaced (14.24, or 14.22 on a removal) fails.
* **T13.4-12 Transient lock path.** The lock path is transient (13.4): it bears on no comparison of graph data and on no record, no write but acquisition and release touches it, and graph data lies beside it (7.3), so it is no write path of `build`'s either (14.22). On a freshly built valid workspace, with each readable leftover or obstruction staged in turn — a dead run's entry (a held `rename` killed under T13.5-3's discipline), a plain file at `.xspec/lock` holding garbage, a symbolic link at it to a directory outside the workspace holding a file, a FIFO at it, and a lock directory holding a symbolic link, a FIFO, and a directory holding a file: `check` is clean, exit 0 — neither condition 10 nor condition 22; `inventory` reports `recorded` available and equal to a leftover-free twin's, finding-free, exit 0; and `build` exits 0. With the workspace then made stale (a section's text edited), `check` reports exactly the leftover-free stale twin's findings, and a refreshing read (`ids`) answers exit 0 with the twin's answer. Each command completes promptly, and the staged leftover or obstruction stays byte-untouched throughout — kind, name, content, and a link's target alike — the lock path holding after each command exactly what was staged, nothing added beside it: a lock directory exactly its staged occupants, and its staged directory exactly the file staged in it (H-6). Clean path: `build` on an unbuilt valid workspace exits 0 and leaves nothing at `.xspec/lock`; once an `audit` session `s` is created there (nothing at `.xspec/lock` afterwards, T13.5-11), each of `build`, `check`, `inventory`, a `rename --preview` of an operation succeeding there, `ids`, `review status s`, and `version` exits 0 and leaves nothing at `.xspec/lock`, as do `ids` and `review status s` once a section's text is then edited, each refreshing graph data before answering (13.3). These fail a product reading the lock path as graph data or as part of the record, judging it among `build`'s write paths, or writing or removing anything there outside acquisition and release — among them one laying out `.xspec/lock` with the graph-data area on every run, and one whose read commands add an entry or a directory there and leave it, wholly or but for the directory. Unreadable leftovers (14.25: the lock path with everything under it is no part of condition 23's state, a refused read there being a read failure wherever a command makes it): on a freshly built valid workspace holding a dead run's entry staged as above, with the lock directory made unlistable (`--x`) and, separately, the entry made unreadable (`-w-------`) — T14-10's disciplines, each verified on the harness itself — `inventory`, `check`, and `ids` each either answer exactly as on the leftover-free twin — `recorded` available and equal, `check` clean, `ids` the twin's answer, each exit 0 — or exit 2 with the error document, `code` `"read-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, nothing modified, SPEC.md stating no read of the lock path by these commands yet making any refused read there that read failure (14.25); a `move --preview` of an operation succeeding on the twin answers byte-identically to its run on the leftover-free twin, its `delta` available, exit 0 — never the read failure, 6.6 fixing a preview's answer as the real operation's once holding exclusivity, which no state of the lock path decides (T6.6-3(a)), so failing a product whose preview reads the lock path and reports the refusal; never `recorded` or `delta` unavailable, a condition-23 finding, or `check`'s unit form (14.23, 14.10), which fail a product reading the lock path as part of the record and taking its refused read for unreadable recorded state. The entry stays byte-identical, name and content, and the lock directory's only occupant, the harness restoring its permissions to inspect it. Manual remedy (13.4): each obstruction of T13.5-10(a) deleted by hand while no mutating command runs — the occupant of `.xspec/lock`, and a non-directory at `.xspec` — the next `rename` proceeds, exit 0; the remedy for a leftover that blocks, an entry in 13.5's gap, is T13.5-10(g)'s.
* **T13.4-13 Any other occupant: FIFOs.** SPEC.md's catch-all occupant clauses — at the configuration path found or named, anything but a plain file (7, 14.14); at a durable file's path, anything other than a plain file (13.4: such a journal a journal error, 14.13; such a session corrupt, 10.1, 14.21); at the session directory's and the graph-data area's own paths, anything but a directory (10.1, 11.6, 13.4); at a derived file's path, any other non-plain-file occupant, stale to `check` (14.10) and replaced by `build`'s write (13.4); at a write path's directory component, any other non-directory occupant (14.22); at a move's destination, whatever kind of filesystem object — for the section form, any occupant discovery does not yield as a spec source — and at a directory component of it or of a derived path it would generate, anything other than a directory (6.5); at a recorded derived path no longer generated, anything but a directory or a discovered source, reported by `check` (14.10) and removed by `build` (13.4); and at the hold path, anything (13.5) — are staged elsewhere with directories, symbolic links, and plain files alone (T7-1, T6.1-3, T11.6-3, T10.1-4, T10.1-6, T12.2-2, T13.4-4, T13.4-6, T6.5-4, T13.4-11, T13.5-1), which a product judging kinds by a deny-list of those three passes, though at any other occupant it opens the object and blocks, reports a read or write failure (14.25, 14.24) in the condition's place, or misses the condition — finding the destination free, or leaving in place what 13.4 removes. On the Linux leg, each arm staged on a freshly built valid workspace holding an `audit` session `s` created while it was valid, a FIFO at one path — a path no group's globs match, SPEC.md leaving open whether discovery yields a FIFO a glob matches (7) — every invocation run under `--json`: each completes promptly (S-3), no FIFO opened, the FIFO still in place afterward unless a `build` replaces or removes it ((e), (h)), and nothing else modified but by a `build` that succeeds. (a) The configuration path, the root's `xspec.config.ts` a FIFO: `build`, `check`, `ids`, `view`, `inventory`, and a `rename` of an operation succeeding on a FIFO-free twin each exit 2 with the error document, `code` `"configuration-error"`, `path` `"xspec.config.ts"` (T12.7-3); `version` exits 0. And the path `--config` names, the root's configuration valid and `alt.config.ts` beside it a FIFO: each of those commands given `--config alt.config.ts` exits 2 likewise, `path` `"alt.config.ts"`, and `version` so given exits 0. (b) The journal path, `.xspec/journal` a FIFO: `build`, `check`, and `ids` each report condition 13 concerning `.xspec/journal`, exit 1, `ids` answering nothing (13.3), and such a `rename` is refused with that finding alone (6.4), exit 1; `inventory` reports `journal` exactly `{"path": ".xspec/journal", "occupied": true}`, finding-free, exit 0 (11.6); and `occurrences`, and `view` and `at … 0` of a discovered spec source, each answer finding-free, exit 0 (11.2: their judgement of `build`'s validations makes every read the gate makes, the journal's included — that it is made, T14-10(l)(i)'s arm — and the journal's condition accompanies no answer). (c) A session file, `.xspec/reviews/s.json` a FIFO: `check`, `review status s`, and `review list` each report `s` corrupt (condition 21), exit 1; `inventory` lists `.xspec/reviews/s.json`, finding-free, exit 0. (d) The session directory, `.xspec/reviews` a FIFO, and separately the area's own path, `.xspec` a FIFO: every command T10.1-6's plain-file arm for that path runs on its freshly built workspace answers exactly as there — its stale-twin runs aside. (e) A derived file's path, a generated module's path a FIFO: `check` reports exactly one finding, condition 10 in the per-file form concerning that path, exit 1; `build` exits 0, replacing the FIFO with the generated plain file, byte-equal to a FIFO-free twin's; and `check` is then clean. (f) A write path's directory component, on the arm's workspace configured with `markdown: { emit: true, outDir: "out" }` and built, the directory `out` with all it holds replaced by a FIFO: `build` exits 1 with the condition-22 finding concerning `out`, modifying nothing, `check` reports it without writing (T13.4-6), and the 11.2 surfaces, run as in (b), each answer finding-free, exit 0 (11.2: the kinds of `build`'s write-path directory components among their judgement's reads, condition 22 accompanying no answer). (g) A move's destination and its components, under the spec glob `specs/**/*.mdx` with `specs/A.mdx` holding a section `x`: `move specs/A.mdx other/B.mdx` and `move specs/A.mdx#x other/B.mdx#x`, `other/B.mdx` a FIFO in no spec group, each exit 1 with exactly two findings concerning `other/B.mdx`, `refused-destination-exists` and `refused-invalid-destination` (T6.5-4's two-reason arm); and `move specs/A.mdx specs/sub/B.mdx`, `specs/sub` a FIFO, and, on (f)'s configuration, `move specs/A.mdx specs/new/B.mdx`, the component `out/specs/new` of its emit path a FIFO, each exit 1 with exactly one finding, `refused-invalid-destination` concerning the destination — nothing modified throughout. (h) A recorded derived path no longer generated, on the arm's workspace built with emission next to sources: `specs/A.md` replaced by a FIFO, then emission disabled — `check` reports the condition-10 recorded-file finding concerning `specs/A.md` and no other finding but, unasserted, the unit form (T13.4-11(a)); `build` exits 0, removing the FIFO; and `check` is then clean. (i) The hold path, outside the workspace: a `rename` of an operation succeeding on a FIFO-free twin, given `--test-hold` naming a FIFO, exits 2 with the error document, `code` and `path` `null` (13.5's usage error, a plain one: 12.7, 14.24), modifying nothing, `.xspec/lock` absent afterwards (T13.5-11).

### 13.5 Concurrency and isolation

All mutual-exclusion tests use the `--test-hold <path>` seam for determinism — T13.5-3's kills past the hold and T13.5-13's deliberately unsynchronized starts excepted, whose assertions hold under every timing and schedule, T13.5-13's unheld arm using no seam at all — their hold paths lying outside the workspace. A hold is *lifted* when the harness deletes the held run's hold file, the run then proceeding (13.5); *release* is throughout 13.5's alone — a run's own deletion, as it ends, of its entry and then of the emptied directories (T13.5-11). The held-state comparison, wherever a test inspects a workspace while a run is held: every workspace path but the lock path `.xspec/lock` and everything under it — and `.xspec` itself where the invocation found none, acquisition then creating it (13.5) — is byte-identical to its pre-invocation state as the harness's own stagings during the hold, and the writes of another run completing meanwhile, have changed it (T13.5-8, T13.5-10(c), (e), T13.5-12), and `.xspec/lock` is a directory holding, besides whatever the harness staged there (T13.5-10(c), (e)), exactly one entry, a plain file, per held run whose entry stands: the held run's, standing until its release (13.5) unless the harness deletes or replaces it, or a run on another machine takes it over (T13.5-10(e)) — the harness's alteration of its content in place leaving it standing (T13.5-10(c)) — its name, content, and permissions opaque (13.4, H-4).

* **T13.5-1 Hold seam basics.** A mutating command (`rename`, `move`, `review create/resolve/split`) with `--test-hold`: creates an empty file at the path after acquiring exclusivity and before modifying anything (the held-state comparison above holding while held — staged on a freshly built workspace, as T10.1-1, so no refresh is pending), proceeds only once the file is deleted, then completes normally, `.xspec/lock` absent afterwards (T13.5-11). Stale-workspace arm (13.5: the hold precedes every modification, the 13.3 refresh included): on a workspace whose graph data is stale — a section's text edited after `build`, the workspace still valid — `review create --strategy audit --name n --test-hold <path>`: while held, the held-state comparison holds — graph data, and every other workspace file but the lock path, byte-identical to the pre-invocation state; once the hold is lifted, the session is created and the graph data refreshed (T10.1-1) — discriminating a product that refreshes before acquiring exclusivity. Seam neutrality (13.5: the seam changes no other behavior): the final workspace state of a run held and its hold then lifted — sources, journal, sessions, derived files, and graph data — is byte-identical to the same operation run without `--test-hold` on an identical twin workspace (the hold path outside the workspace; a product-to-itself comparison under H-4, well-defined across directories per H-6). If anything exists at the hold path — a file, a directory, a symbolic link to a file, and a dangling symbolic link whose target lies in a writable directory outside the workspace — the command fails exit 2 without modifying anything, the occupant unchanged, `.xspec/lock` absent afterwards (T13.5-11); after the dangling link's arm nothing exists at the link's target either (13.5: creation fails if anything, a symbolic link included, already exists at the path), failing a product that tests the path through the link, finds nothing there, and creates the hold file through it — a write through a planted link, which only a dangling link exposes. A non-mutating command (`build` and `query` as representatives) given `--test-hold` fails exit 2 as an unknown flag: 12.0 enumerates the flags a command accepts — its synopsis's, the global `--json` and `--config`, and, for a mutating command alone, `--test-hold` — so on every other command the token names no accepted flag, an unknown flag of the syntax class (12.0, T12.0-14), and a product accepting and ignoring it fails this arm; the flag being value-taking by name on every command (12.0), `build --test-hold --json` consumes `--json` as the path and leaves JSON out of effect (stdout empty, exit 2).
* **T13.5-2 Mutual exclusion.** While command 1 is held, each other mutating command — `rename`, `move`, `review create`, `resolve`, and `split`, each invoked as an operation succeeding on an unheld twin and composing with command 1, succeeding there too once command 1 has run (as in T13.5-10(e)) — fails promptly, exiting while command 1 still holds, with exit 2, and modifies nothing (journal, sessions, sources byte-identical); each is run once under `--json`, its stdout exactly the error document (12.7) with `code` `"workspace-busy"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, and `locations` `[]` (14.26; `message` and `identities` informational, 12.7), and once without, stdout empty (12.0). Command 1's entry is byte-identical, name and content, after each refusal, and again the lock directory's only entry (13.5: no acquisition or release removes or alters a live run's entry, and the refused run's release deletes its own). After command 1 completes, the second command succeeds. *Determinism* (12.0; 12.7: `message` and `identities` deterministic, informational though they are): each refused command's `--json` run is repeated twice, each time on a fresh content-identical workspace held by a separate holder process, whose entry therefore records another process identifier (cyclic allocation, T13.5-3) — once at the same absolute path, the workspace recreated there, and once at a different one — its standard output and standard error byte-identical across the three runs (H-6), failing a product whose diagnostic names a process identifier, an entry, or the workspace's absolute path.
* **T13.5-3 Exclusivity ends with the process.** Kill a held mutating command — each in turn — and a subsequent mutating command succeeds, exit 0. Exit-collection discipline, since 13.5 lets an entry whose recorded identifier, not the acquirer's own, is still listed in the acquirer's process list refuse acquisition (its run's process terminated but still listed, or the identifier reused): the harness starts the held command in a process group of its own, kills the whole group with an uncatchable termination (SIGKILL) — a catchable signal would let a product release on it, as 13.5 allows an interrupted command, and might not reach the first process of a fresh process-identifier namespace (T13.5-10(f)), to which a process outside the namespace delivers only the signals that first process handles, SIGKILL and SIGSTOP aside — collects the exit of the process it started, and confirms that no process of the group remains listed in the process list before starting the later command. Between the kill and that command, `.xspec/lock` holds exactly one entry, a plain file — the dead run's, a leftover (13.5: abnormal termination leaves its entry) — which the later command takes over: `.xspec/lock` is absent once it ends. Identifier reuse cannot be prevented, only made negligible and detected: the Linux leg allocates process identifiers cyclically, a freed one recurring only once allocation wraps; and should the later command nonetheless be refused (`workspace-busy`), the harness checks the process list for the killed group's identifiers — any listed again voids the trial, rerun on a fresh workspace a bounded number of times, exhaustion a harness error (H-11) — while a refusal with none listed fails the test. Every test that kills a mutating run (T11.6-3, T13.4-12, T13.5-7's kill arm, T13.5-10, P-10, and E-6's exclusion probe, whose Windows leg terminates as E-6 states) or takes over a dead run's entry (T13.5-11(d)) applies this discipline — T13.5-7's kill arm without the reuse check, no mutating command following its kills, each run's group confirmed gone before `check` runs; T13.5-10(f) and (g) without it, the identifier listed being the acquirer's own in (f) and the staging itself in (g). *Past the hold* (13.5: a run's entry stands until its release, which comes as the command ends, after its last write, so its exclusion spans every write it makes; an abnormal termination leaves the entry): each mutating command in turn — on a freshly built valid workspace holding an `audit` session `s` created while it was valid, as an operation succeeding there — is held, its hold lifted, and the run then killed under this discipline, its group confirmed gone before the lock path is inspected and the reuse check aside, no later command running: each kill lands on a fresh copy of that workspace, at delays spread across the span from the lift to the exit of the command's twin — the same operation held and lifted alike on an identical workspace, that span measured on it — by a fixed rule, fixed fractions of that span, a fixed number of kills per command in CI (E-5). Whenever the kill, not a normal exit, ended the run while some path the twin's run changed — a source, the journal, a session file, a derived file, or graph data — did not yet hold the twin's final state, the run had not made its last write, so had not released, and `.xspec/lock` holds exactly one entry, a plain file — the dead run's, a leftover; a run that exited before the kill landed ends exactly as its twin — exit code, output, and workspace byte-equal (H-6) — with `.xspec/lock` absent (T13.5-11). A product that releases when its hold is lifted — and so, unheld, straight after acquiring — or anywhere short of its last write fails, its writes then excluding no other run; T13.5-13's unheld arm meets the same fault through the journal appends and resolutions it loses.
* **T13.5-4 Readers during mutation.** While a mutating command is held, read commands still run and observe the prior state — `check` clean on a clean workspace, reporting nothing for the lock path, and no read command, `check`, `inventory`, or preview reporting the busy condition, which the refused mutating command alone reports (14.26, T14-4) — and once each has exited, the held-state comparison holds, the holder's entry byte-identical, name and content, nothing added beside it (H-6: a command that acquires nothing writes nothing at the lock path); `build` runs during a hold too (13.5: every command but the mutating ones may run concurrently) — on a freshly built valid workspace while a `rename` is held, `build` exits 0 while the hold stands, neither refused (no `workspace-busy`) nor waiting out the hold, its regeneration reproducing the workspace's bytes, so the held-state comparison still holds once it exits, the holder's entry byte-identical, name and content; and the `rename`, its hold lifted, ends exactly as on a twin with no concurrent `build` — exit code, output, and final workspace byte-equal (H-6); non-mutating commands run concurrently with each other (parallel `build`/`query` storm on one workspace terminates, and any derived-file inconsistency is resolved by one final `build` — byte-equal to a clean build).
* **T13.5-5 Atomic visibility.** A concurrent reader polling a derived file during repeated builds only ever observes prior content, complete new content, or absence-before-first-write — never a partial file (property-style loop, 16).
* **T13.5-6 Workspace isolation.** Two workspaces driven concurrently by parallel harness instances never interfere: results equal serial runs (H-1). Exclusion is per workspace (13.5), for sibling workspaces and for one root inside another alike — an inner root `R/in` within the outer root `R`, each configuration globbing `specs/**/*.mdx` under its own root, so neither discovers the other's sources and neither reads or writes a path the other writes: a mutating command held in either workspace leaves each mutating command in the other prompt and unrefused, exit 0 while the hold stands; and concurrent mutations in the two workspaces leave each exactly as serial runs do. Workspaces sharing a file carry no guarantee (13.5) and are not staged.
* **T13.5-7 Interrupted or write-refused mutation: the pinned write order.** 13.5 fixes the state a mutating command leaves when it stops early — the writes already made, each complete, and no later one — and 14.24 makes stopping stageable on demand: a write the environment refuses stops the command at that write, exit 2 with the error document (`write-failure`, the concerned path; 12.0, 12.7). Refusals are staged by permission removal on the Linux leg under T14-9's discipline — the directory holding the path made read-only and its occupant, where one exists, unwritable, so creation, replacement in place or by renaming, appending, and removal are all refused whatever the product's write strategy (E-1) — and, for a mutating command, applied while the command is held at the seam of 13.5 (T13.5-1), after acquisition and before any modification, so acquisition never meets the staging (seam neutrality, T13.5-1) — release does wherever a staging covers the lock directory's parent: (b)'s read-only `.xspec` refuses release's deletion of the emptied lock directory, which remains, empty, no outcome changed and no write failure reported (13.5, 14.24), until a mutating command run once the permissions are restored proceeds unrefused and removes it; every other arm ends with `.xspec/lock` absent (T13.5-11). Every arm starts from a freshly built, valid, journal-bearing workspace (no refresh pending) and byte-compares every file the operation would touch; every rewritten-byte expectation is read from an identical twin workspace on which the same operation runs unrefused (H-6). (a) Source edits first, one write per file, in preview `files` order: a rename whose section lives in `specs/b/B.mdx`, referenced from `specs/a/A.mdx` (a `d` reference) and `specs/c/C.mdx` (an embedding) — `files` order by path bytes A, B, C (6.6, 12.7) — with `specs/b` staged unwritable: A is rewritten (byte-equal to the twin's A), B and C are byte-untouched, the journal is byte-unchanged (no entry: the append is the commit point, reached only once every source edit is in place), derived files and graph data are byte-unchanged, the error document concerns `specs/b/B.mdx`, and `check` reports exactly one finding, condition 5 for A's now-unresolved spelling (13.5: a partly applied rewrite manifests as 14.5–14.7; 14.10's mismatch forms are undetectable on a failing workspace, T12.2-4). (b) The journal append as the commit point: the same rename with `.xspec/journal` staged unwritable (`.xspec` read-only, graph data untouched) — every source edit made (A, B, C byte-equal to the twin's), no journal entry (the file byte-unchanged), the error document concerning `.xspec/journal`, derived files and graph data byte-unchanged; `check` then reports condition 10 alone, the workspace being valid and consistently rewritten: the graph-data unit form (13.3: graph data no longer matches the rewritten sources) and one per-file finding for each derived path whose occupant differs from the one a `build` leaves on a copy of this state, permissions restored — the harness computes that set by comparison, B's module certainly in it (4.1: its properties are named by ID segment), never from the twin's post-operation derived files, the twin's journal holding the entry this state lacks (13.4: derived files are reproducible from sources, configuration, and the journal, 5.4); and once the permissions are restored, `impact --base <pre-rename ref>` reports the old identity deleted and the new one added — manual restructuring (6.7), no entry having been journaled — and the next `build` leaves `check` clean. (c) Stopped after the append, the identity effect complete: a file-form move `specs/A.mdx` → `specs/sub/B.mdx` under `markdown: { emit: true, outDir: "out" }` — `out/specs` holding `A.md` alone once built, so no other spec source emits there or beneath it, and `specs/A.mdx`'s importers code sources under `src/` — with `out/specs` staged unwritable — every source edit made, the relocation complete (origin absent, destination present, the importers rewritten), the journal holding exactly one new entry byte-equal to the twin's, and the error document concerning one of the two Markdown writes the regeneration owes under `out/specs` — `out/specs/sub/B.md`'s creation (13.4: the directory it needs is part of that write) or `out/specs/A.md`'s removal, the order among a regeneration's derived-file writes being unpinned (13.5) — with stdout exactly that document: the write failure is reported alone, no `mapping` (6.4); `check` reports condition 10 alone (the stale remainder), and after the permissions are restored `impact --base <pre-move ref>` reports no change categories (6.2: the identity effect is observable through the journal's effects alone) and the next `build` leaves `check` clean. A rename stopped there alike (6.4: it reports the write failure alone, its journaled mapping observable thereafter only through the journal's effects): (a)'s rename on a variant of (a)'s workspace whose configuration adds `markdown: { emit: true, outDir: "out" }`, built, `out/specs/b/B.md` then deleted — `out/specs/b` holding nothing else — and `out/specs/b` staged unwritable, so that the regeneration owes a write there, that file's creation, however it treats the derived files whose content the rename leaves unchanged (a rename changes no Markdown, 3) — A, B, and C byte-equal to the twin's, the journal holding exactly one new entry byte-equal to the twin's, and stdout under `--json` exactly the error document concerning `out/specs/b/B.md`, no `mapping` beside or instead of it; `check` reports condition-10 findings alone, `out/specs/b/B.md`'s per-file finding among them; and after the permissions are restored `impact --base <pre-rename ref>` reports no change categories (6.2) and the next `build` leaves `check` clean. (d) A relocation is two writes, the destination produced and then the origin removed (13.5, 14.24): the same move with `specs/sub` present and writable and `specs` staged unwritable — the destination `specs/sub/B.mdx` present with the moved file's rewritten bytes (the twin's), the origin `specs/A.mdx` still present and byte-unchanged, the error document concerning `specs/A.mdx` (the removal, the relocation's second write), no journal entry, no importer rewritten (the relocation's entry precedes `src/` in `files` order), and `check` reporting condition 10 alone — both files valid, the destination's derived files missing. (e) `review` mutators write the session file once, last: on a stale, valid workspace (a source edited after `build`) with `.xspec/reviews` and the session file staged unwritable and `.xspec` itself writable, `review resolve s <item> --status no-change` exits 2 with the error document concerning `.xspec/reviews/s.json`, the session file byte-unchanged (`status`, once restored, reports the item still `unresolved`), and the refresh already made: `check` afterwards reports the edited source's per-file staleness and no unit-form finding — graph data matching the current sources — where a product writing the session before refreshing, or refusing the refresh, fails; `review create --strategy audit --name n` and `split` on the same staging behave alike, no `n.json` created and no decomposition recorded. (f) `build` and a refresh, each file complete, the order unpinned: a built workspace whose `specs/b/B.mdx` is edited (the workspace valid) before `specs/b` is staged unwritable — `build` exits 2 with the error document concerning a derived path under `specs/b/` (`specs/b/B.xspec.ts` or a companion, the first `specs/b/` write in the product's order); every derived file present afterwards is complete — byte-equal either to its bytes before the invocation or to the twin's file at that path, none partial (12.1, 13.5) — and `check` reports exactly the derived paths whose occupant differs from the twin's derived state — the harness computes that set by comparison, B's module certainly in it, its companions as the comparison finds them (13.1: companions are the product's own), the graph-data unit form exactly when graph data differs — the next `build`, permissions restored, exiting 0 with `check` clean (12.1, 13.4). A refreshing read on the same edited-but-not-rebuilt workspace with `.xspec` staged unwritable by T14-9's graph-data staging, which covers the area and every path of T13.3-2's operational path set: `query nodes` and `view specs/a/A.mdx` each exit 2 with the error document concerning `.xspec` (14.24: a graph-data write concerns the area), stdout exactly that document and no answer (13.3, 11.2), while `check` on the same state exits 1 reporting the staleness (never a 14.24 reporter) and a `move --preview` exits 0 writing nothing (6.6). (g) A created target file is a source edit, made in its `files` place (13.5: one write per file the operation rewrites or creates, in the order the preview's `files` lists them — a created file at the path its creation would occupy, 12.7): `move specs/a/A.mdx#x specs/n/N.mdx#x` — `x` a top-level section of `specs/a/A.mdx` that nothing references, and `specs/n` an empty directory, so the target file is created (6.5) and `files` lists the origin `specs/a/A.mdx` before the created `specs/n/N.mdx` — with `specs/n` staged unwritable: `specs/a/A.mdx` rewritten, byte-equal to the twin's; nothing at `specs/n/N.mdx`, `specs/n` still empty; the journal byte-unchanged (no entry: the append follows every source edit, the creation included); derived files and graph data byte-unchanged; exit 2, stdout under `--json` exactly the error document concerning `specs/n/N.mdx` (14.24: the file the write would have produced), no `mapping` beside or instead of it; and `check` reporting condition 10 alone, the workspace valid with `x` deleted as by manual restructuring (6.7): the graph-data unit form and one per-file finding for each derived path whose occupant differs from the one a `build` leaves on a copy of this state, permissions restored — the harness computing that set by comparison, as in (b), A's module certainly in it (4.1). With the two paths' byte order reversed — the same staging on a workspace whose unreferenced top-level section `x` lies in `specs/z/Z.mdx` instead, moved by `move specs/z/Z.mdx#x specs/n/N.mdx#x`, so `files` lists the creation first — the refused creation is the run's first write: `specs/z/Z.mdx` byte-untouched, nothing at `specs/n/N.mdx`, the journal, derived files, and graph data byte-unchanged, the same error document, and `check` clean, exit 0. A product creating the target file after the journal append, as part of the finishing regeneration, fails both, leaving an entry; one making a section move's writes in any order but `files`' fails one or the other. (h) A removal owed alone (14.24: a removal is a write the environment can refuse; 12.1, 13.4: removing the recorded derived files the current sources and configuration no longer generate is part of every regeneration): (c)'s workspace, built under `markdown: { emit: true, outDir: "out" }` — `out/specs` holding `A.md` alone — with emission then disabled (`markdown` removed from the configuration), so that a regeneration owes nothing under `out/specs` but `out/specs/A.md`'s removal, and `out/specs` staged unwritable: `build` exits 2, stdout under `--json` exactly the error document, `code` `"write-failure"`, `path` `"out/specs/A.md"`, the file byte-identical in place; and a rename of a section of `specs/A.mdx` on the same state, the staging applied at its hold, exits 2 with that error document alone, no `mapping` beside or instead of it (6.4), its source edits and journal append made — every rewritten source byte-equal to the twin's, the journal holding exactly one new entry byte-equal to the twin's — and `out/specs/A.md` byte-identical in place — failing a product whose orphan removal is best-effort, which meets no other refused write on either staging and exits 0. Kill arm, a robustness check only — a kill lands nondeterministically, the refused write above being the deterministic seam: across a spread of kill timings on (a)'s rename once its hold is lifted, each kill under T13.5-3's discipline, `check` never crashes and reports exactly a state 13.5 admits — clean, or condition 5–7 findings alone, or condition 10 findings alone — and the journal is byte-equal either to its prior bytes or to the twin's post-operation bytes (13.5: each write complete) — the lock path's state after such a kill being T13.5-3's past-the-hold arm's.
* **T13.5-8 Acquisition's place in the order.** 13.5 and 12.0 pin it — once configuration is loaded and sources discovered, before the argument checks of 12.0, baseline resolution (6.3), the gate and refresh of 13.3, the operation's own validation, and the hold-file seam — so acquisition's outcomes, the busy refusal (14.26) and acquisition's write and read failures (14.24, 14.25), precede every later check's, the syntax class, the configuration error, and discovery's read failures precede acquisition, and the hold seam engages on an invocation a later check refuses or the gate turns back. The acquisition-first and acquisition-after arms each run under `--json` twice, each run exiting 2 and modifying nothing: once while a `review resolve` is held (`--test-hold`) on the arm's workspace — holding an `audit` session `s` created while it was valid — and once with no holder and `.xspec/lock` a symbolic link to a directory outside the workspace (T13.5-10(a)), the link and its target left untouched. Acquisition first — the held run's `code` `"workspace-busy"` and the obstructed run's `"write-failure"`, each with `path` `".xspec/lock"`: `rename specs/A.mdx nope x` (a nonexistent old ID, 12.0), `move specs/A.mdx#nope specs/B.mdx#x` (a nonexistent origin ID, 6.5), and `review create --base <unresolvable-ref> --name n` (6.3), never the unknown-ID usage error (`code` `null`) or the baseline error; the same `review create` again with a garbage line appended to the current journal (T6.3-6(a)'s staging), never `journal-error` — 6.3's journal read, first among the causes that can fail the baseline (T6.3-6(b)), is one of baseline resolution's reads, all of which follow acquisition (13.5; 12.0: the acquisition errors precede a given baseline) — which a product reading the journal as it loads the workspace, ahead of acquisition, returns; `review create --coverage nope --name n` (an unknown profile) and, `<code>` a code source the arm's workspace discovers, `rename <code> a b` and `move <code> specs/C.mdx` (a wrong-kind origin, 6.4, 6.5), never the unknown-profile or wrong-kind usage error (`code` `null`) — checks whose inputs exist before acquisition, the profile judged against the loaded configuration and the origin's kind against the first discovery (12.0), which a product making them as soon as those inputs exist returns (12.0: for a mutating command, the acquisition errors precede the unknown and wrong-kind names and files of the usage class); `review resolve s <unknown-item> --status skipped`, and `review resolve c <item> --status skipped` naming a session `c` made corrupt for that run (unparseable bytes, T10.1-4), never the unknown-item usage error (`code` `null`) or the corruption's exit 1 (14.21), which a product reading the session before acquiring returns — the item check, consulting the session's content, follows acquisition (12.0); on a workspace failing `build`'s validations — by a source validation error, and separately by that garbage journal line alone (14.13: a journal error is among `build`'s findings, 13.3) — `review create --strategy audit --name n`, `review resolve s <item> --status skipped`, `rename specs/A.mdx a b`, and `move specs/A.mdx specs/C.mdx`, never the gate's or the precondition's exit 1 (6.5: `move`'s validation mirrors `rename`'s, the valid-workspace precondition included) or a `journal-error`, which a product judging the gate, the precondition, or the journal before acquiring returns; and an invocation whose `--test-hold` path is already occupied, never the hold-file usage error (`code` `null`). Acquisition after — never acquisition's error: a surplus operand (`rename specs/A.mdx a b c`) → the syntax-class error, `code` `null`; a configuration file made invalid (in the held arm, during the hold) → `code` `"configuration-error"`; a configuration error the first discovery meets — on a workspace configured as T13.5-12(d)'s, whose spec and code groups both match one path, a file created at that path just before the run (in the held arm, during the hold, the holder having started with the path unoccupied) → `code` `"configuration-error"` with the configuration file's path (T12.7-3), failing a product that checks for a file both groups match only once it has acquired (12.0: of the configuration errors, only one the rediscovery alone meets follows acquisition's errors, T13.5-12(d)); and a discovered directory made unlistable (T14-10(g)'s staging) → `code` `"read-failure"` at that directory — each staged change undone before the holder's hold is lifted. Seam ordering, each under `--test-hold` with no other holder and no obstruction: `rename specs/A.mdx nope x` (a nonexistent old ID, 12.0), `review create --base <unresolvable-ref> --name n` (6.3), that `review create` again with the garbage journal line above (6.3's journal read), `review create --coverage nope --name n` (an unknown profile), `move <code> specs/C.mdx` (a wrong-kind origin), and, on a failing workspace, `review create --strategy audit --name n` (13.3's gate) each create the hold file first — the held-state comparison holding while held — and only after its deletion exit 2, 2, 2, 2, 2, and 1 respectively with their own errors or findings, the journal-staged baseline's error `code` `"journal-error"` with `path` `".xspec/journal"` (T6.3-6(b): the journal read before the ref's resolution), the unknown profile and the wrong-kind origin each a usage error with `code` `null`, the workspace then byte-identical to its pre-invocation state, `.xspec/lock` gone; a product judging arguments (a profile and an origin's kind included), the baseline — its journal read included — or the gate before acquiring exclusivity reports them without ever creating the hold file. The non-mutating boundary: `rename specs/A.mdx nope x --preview` creates no hold file and exits 2 at once (a preview acquires nothing, 6.6; `--test-hold` beside `--preview` is itself exit 2, T6.6-3). The gate arm is 13.3's statement that, for a mutating `review` subcommand, exclusivity acquisition precedes the gate's report.
* **T13.5-9 Reach of the exclusion.** 13.5 excludes, while one run holds exclusivity, every other run on the machine against the same workspace that reaches acquisition — by any user who can write it, in any environment, through any path to its root directory, a symbolic link above the root or another mount of it included. While a `review resolve` started by the harness's own identity, from the workspace root and in the harness's environment, is held (`--test-hold`), each mutating command in turn — `rename`, `move`, `review create`, `resolve`, and `split`, each an operation succeeding on an unheld twin — runs as a second run in each situation below, under `--json`, and fails promptly, exiting while the hold stands, with exit 2 and nothing modified, the holder's entry byte-identical, name and content, and again the lock directory's only entry once the second run has exited (13.5: no acquisition or release removes or alters a live run's entry): (a) a different environment — `TMPDIR`, `HOME`, and `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` each set, for the holder and the second run, to different existing directories, and unrelated variables added to the second run's; (b) a different working directory — a subdirectory of the root, and a directory outside it, the configuration named through `--config`; (c) a different path to the root — through a symbolic link above the root, and through a second mount of the root; each of (a)–(c) with `code` `"workspace-busy"` and `path` `".xspec/lock"` (14.26), the environment refusing none of acquisition's reads and writes there; and (d) a different user who can write the workspace — `code` `"workspace-busy"`, or `"write-failure"` or `"read-failure"` with `path` `".xspec/lock"` where the environment refuses that user a read or write acquisition makes (13.5; the modes of the lock directory and its entries are the product's own), never success; and, since a lock directory the holder makes under its own modes may refuse that user's entry and so leave the liveness judgment unreached, (d) runs again with `.xspec/lock` made by the harness before the holder starts — an empty directory the shared group may list, write, and search, which acquisition keeps (13.5) — and there exactly `code` `"workspace-busy"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, failing a product that judges a live holder of another user dead because its liveness probe is refused. The harness verifies, before the holder starts and again once it is held, that the second identity can create and remove a file in that directory: a failure before is an ineffective staging (H-11); one after, the holder's acquisition having withdrawn that access, leaves the lenient expectation above. A product keeping its exclusion state outside the workspace — under a temporary, home, or runtime directory, or per user — fails (a) or (d) where that state follows the environment or the user, and fails the held-state comparison wherever it lies (T13.5's preamble, T13.5-1), `.xspec/lock` not holding the held run's entry: that comparison alone catches such state kept under one fixed directory for every user and keyed by the root's identity on the filesystem, which (a)–(d) all pass; one keying the workspace by the spelling of its root's path fails (b) or (c). Staging discipline, Linux leg (E-1): the symbolic link is the harness's own; the second mount — a bind mount of the root at a second path — and the second user — an unprivileged identity distinct from the harness's, sharing a group with it, the workspace's directories and files group-readable and group-writable, its directories group-searchable — are made with E-1's administrative access, outside every product invocation, the second user's runs started as that identity and unprivileged like every product invocation. The harness verifies each staging on itself before use, as T14-9 does: a file it creates through either path to the root appears through the other, the two paths being distinct mount points; and the second identity's user identifier differs from the harness's, that identity reads every file and lists every directory in the workspace, the configuration and every discovered source among them, and creates and removes a file in the workspace root and in `.xspec`, and each identity's process list lists the other's processes (13.5's one machine). An ineffective staging is a harness error (H-11), never a pass or a skip (H-9).
* **T13.5-10 Acquisition.** 13.5's acquisition, each arm staged on a freshly built valid workspace holding an `audit` session `s` created while it was valid — an unbuilt workspace where an arm says so — and run with each mutating command in turn, `rename`, `move`, `review create`, `resolve`, and `split`, each an operation succeeding on an unstaged twin, under `--json` — every arm staged afresh for each command, a run's takeover or release clearing what the arm staged; every run completes promptly, no FIFO opened. (a) Obstruction — acquisition removes nothing and fails with the write failure, leaving the occupant for manual deletion (T13.4-12): `.xspec` a plain file, a symbolic link to a directory holding a journal and sessions, a dangling symbolic link, and a FIFO → exit 2, `code` `"write-failure"`, `path` `".xspec"` (14.24; 10.1's area so occupied failing every mutating command at acquisition first, T10.1-6); `.xspec/lock` a symbolic link to a directory, a symbolic link to a file, a dangling symbolic link, and a FIFO → exit 2, `code` `"write-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`; in each, nothing modified and the occupant byte-identical in place, a link's target untouched and nothing written through a link. (b) Leftover takeover — the command proceeds, exit 0, its result byte-equal to the twin's, `.xspec/lock` absent once it ends: a plain file at `.xspec/lock`, and one with every permission removed; in a lock directory, a directory holding a file; a directory tree two levels deep — a directory holding a directory that holds a plain file, a symbolic link to a directory outside the workspace holding a file, a dangling symbolic link, and a FIFO — removed whole (13.5: a directory with all it holds); a symbolic link to a directory outside the workspace holding a file, removed itself; and a FIFO, removed unopened — every link's target, the directory outside the workspace and the file it holds, byte-identical afterward, nothing removed or written through a link (13.5: a symbolic link itself, never its target; 13.4: writes never traverse symbolic links), as T13.4-11(e) requires of derived-file removal, failing a product whose removal follows a link into its target, deleting foreign content behind a reported success, or reaches only one level into a directory; a dead run's entry (a held `rename` killed under T13.5-3's discipline), and one with every permission removed (acquisition reads no file's content); and a dead run's entry carried into another workspace by a copy of the whole workspace made after the kill — 13.5's entry created for another workspace, as a copy or version control carries it in. No arm stages a plain file of the harness's own naming inside the lock directory: the entry form is the product's own (13.5), so such a file may read as an entry whose recorded identifier lies in 13.5's gap, which MAY refuse. P-15 searches further leftover trees as a property. (c) A busy refusal removes nothing and lists no directory but the lock directory, in whatever order acquisition meets the lock directory's contents: during a held `review resolve`'s hold the harness adds, beside the holder's entry, dead runs' entries — each taken from a twin workspace in which a held `rename` is killed under T13.5-3's discipline, and copied in, name and content: 13.5's entry created for another workspace, as a copy of the workspace carries it in — and leftovers of its own naming, each a directory holding a file or a FIFO (never a plain file: (b)'s caution), made unlistable once filled (mode `-wx`, T14-10's discipline, the harness's own listing of each refused, verified first). Each kind brackets the holder's entry: at least one dead entry and at least one leftover of the harness's naming precede that entry, and at least one of each follows it, both in byte order of name and in the order the filesystem lists the lock directory to the harness — the entry's name observed, never pinned (H-4). The harness names leftovers below and above that name in byte order, then further ones until its listing brackets the entry too; and, the dead entries' names being the product's own, it copies in further dead entries until they bracket the entry in both orders, its twins killed both before the holder starts and while it holds, so that names ordered by their runs' start fall on both sides — each a bounded number, exhaustion voiding the trial, rerun with a fresh holder a bounded number of times, exhaustion of the reruns a harness error (H-11) — certain on a filesystem listing in creation order, where every staged name follows, or every one precedes, the earlier-made entry, so the arm is staged on a filesystem whose listing order is not creation order. Each mutating command is then refused, `code` `"workspace-busy"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"` — never `"read-failure"` — leaving every staged leftover, each dead entry included, byte-identical in place, and the holder's entry byte-identical, name and content (13.5: an entry of a run alive on this machine refuses acquisition, no leftover then removed, and acquisition lists only the lock directory and each directory it removes), the harness restoring the leftovers' permissions before comparing them; once the holder completes, `.xspec/lock` holds exactly the staged leftovers — release deleting its entry, the lock directory still holding something — and the next mutating command removes them all, `.xspec/lock` absent once it ends. A product removing leftovers as its listing meets them and refusing at the first live entry fails, as does one removing every dead entry while judging liveness and sweeping the rest only once it acquires, and one judging entries alone in listing or byte order — removing each dead one it meets, refusing at the first live one — a dead entry preceding the live one in either order; so does one listing leftover directories before it judges liveness — to plan the removal it makes only once it acquires — which meets a refused listing and reports `"read-failure"` (14.25) where 13.5 gives the busy refusal (14.26).
