A busy refusal judges the live entry by its name alone (13.5: the name records the identifier, and acquisition reads no file's content): with no leftover staged, the harness removes every permission from the held `review resolve`'s entry during its hold — its name and content recorded first, and the harness's own read of it then refused, as T14-10 verifies its stagings — and each mutating command is refused alike, `code` `"workspace-busy"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, modifying nothing, the entry still the lock directory's only one under the same name; the harness then restores the entry's permissions, its content byte-identical, and once the holder completes `.xspec/lock` is absent. A product reading an entry's content to judge liveness — as a single-file lock keeps the identifier — fails, whether it reports a read failure here or judges the unreadable live entry dead, removes it, and acquires, though it passes every arm whose live entry stays readable. Whether the mode change counts as interference (13.5) changes no expectation: each run fares as the lock directory decides, which holds an entry whose name records a live run's identifier. Nor does a live entry's content bear on the judgment, an alteration of it being 13.5's interference, which voids the hold's exclusion and leaves each run to fare as the lock directory then decides: with no leftover staged, the harness alters the held `review resolve`'s entry during its hold, in place, its name and kind unchanged, in three stagings, each separate — truncated to zero length; overwritten with garbage, bytes of the harness's choosing, ill-formed UTF-8, of a length other than the entry's; and overwritten with the content of a dead run's entry, taken from a twin workspace in which a held `rename` is killed under T13.5-3's discipline, as above — recording the entry's name before the alteration and its altered content after (H-4), a staging that leaves the content as it was (the product's entries empty, or the dead run's content the holder's own) altering nothing, its expectation the same. Each mutating command is then refused alike — exit 2, `code` `"workspace-busy"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, modifying nothing — the altered entry byte-identical as altered, name and content, and still the lock directory's only entry (13.5: an entry whose name records a live run's identifier refuses acquisition, no leftover then removed); and once the holder, its hold lifted, completes, exit 0, `.xspec/lock` is absent, release deleting its entry and reading nothing (13.5). A product reading entries' content to judge liveness where it can — taking the identifier from the content, or judging an empty or unparseable entry a crashed holder's, as a common lockfile rule does — removes the altered entry and acquires, and fails, though it can pass every arm whose entries hold their own runs' content and, its refused read falling back on the name, the permission arm above; so does a product whose release reads its entry before deleting it and, finding it altered, leaves it behind or errs. (d) Refused reads and writes — T14-9's and T14-10's disciplines, each staging verified on the harness itself — each exit 2, nothing modified, and release following the failure (13.5: a command failed at acquisition releases, T13.5-11), leaving exactly the residue stated: an empty leftover lock directory unlistable but writable (mode `-wx`) → `code` `"read-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, no `.xspec/lock` afterwards — release deletes the run's entry and then the emptied directory, which needs only `.xspec`'s write permission; a lock directory holding a subdirectory that holds a file, the subdirectory unlistable (`-wx`) → `"read-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, never the nested path (14.25: anything under the lock path concerns the lock path); an empty leftover lock directory refusing writes (`r-x`) → `"write-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, no `.xspec/lock` afterwards — no entry added, release deletes the empty directory all the same; a lock directory holding a subdirectory that holds a file, the subdirectory refusing writes (`r-x`), so the leftover's removal is refused → `"write-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`; after either subdirectory arm, `.xspec/lock` holding exactly one occupant, a directory holding exactly the staged file, byte-identical (the harness restoring its permissions to inspect it), and no entry — release deletes the run's entry, the lock directory still holding something — that directory's name unasserted: the lock directory stays writable, unlike under T14-9's full discipline, so a removal may first rename the leftover within it, which Linux permits without permission on the leftover itself, and 14.24 keeps every write already made; a read-only `.xspec` holding no `lock` → `"write-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, no `.xspec/lock` afterwards; a plain file at `.xspec/lock`, a leftover acquisition removes (13.5), with `.xspec` read-only and the file unwritable — T14-9's full discipline — so its removal is refused → `"write-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, the file byte-identical in place afterwards, the command stopping at the refused removal (14.24) — failing a product that ignores the refusal and mishandles the still-occupied path when it goes on to create the lock directory there or an entry under it; `.xspec` unsearchable (mode `rw-`), holding no `lock` and, separately, an empty lock directory, under a configuration whose globs reach nothing under `.xspec` (as (h)'s), the harness's own examination of `.xspec/lock` and its own creation of a file in `.xspec` each refused, verified first → `code` `"read-failure"` or `"write-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"` either way, `.xspec` with all it holds byte-identical once the harness restores its mode: no conforming acquisition takes the lock path's kind from a listing ((h): it lists no directory holding that path), so it meets the refusal at its examination of the path, a read failure, or at a write it attempts there first, a write failure (14.25, 14.24), and release's deletions are refused alike, which changes no outcome (13.5) — failing a product that handles only nonexistence when it examines the lock path and, on any other refusal, crashes, exits outside 12.0's codes, or proceeds without exclusivity; a lock directory unsearchable (`rw-`) holding a directory that holds a file, the harness's own examination of that directory's kind refused, verified first → the same codes and path, `.xspec/lock` holding exactly the staged directory and its file afterwards, and no entry (the harness restoring modes to inspect them) — its occupants' kinds refused, as is every write and listing below it; and, on an unbuilt workspace, its root read-only → `"write-failure"`, `path` `".xspec"` (14.24: a write producing the area's directory), no `.xspec` afterwards, `resolve` and `split` there naming the absent session `s`, acquisition preceding that check (12.0). A product skipping release after a failure at acquisition fails these residues, its entry or the emptied lock directory left behind. The two empty leftover lock directories' arms — a write failure at the entry's addition, and a read failure past it — are also *determinism* arms: each run twice, and once more on a content-identical workspace at a different absolute path, each run on its own staging, standard output and standard error byte-identical (H-6; 12.0: no diagnostic naming an entry or an absolute path). (e) Interference (13.5): with a held `rename`'s entry deleted by hand during its hold — or instead replaced by hand, under its name (H-4), with an occupant that is no entry, each staged separately: a directory holding a file, a FIFO, and a symbolic link to a plain file outside the workspace — each mutating command, as an operation composing with that `rename`, acquires — creating its own hold file while the first still holds, never refused `workspace-busy` — and the two, their holds lifted in turn, the `rename`'s first, each exit 0 (an interfered hold excludes no run, each faring as the lock directory then decides). A replacing occupant is gone once the second run has created its hold file — the directory with all it holds, the link itself, its target byte-identical in place, the FIFO unopened — and the lock directory then holds that run's entry alone, a plain file named apart from the replaced entry (H-4): anything in the lock directory but a plain file is no entry and records no identifier, whatever its name — a leftover outside the gap, which acquisition removes (13.5) — so a product judging liveness by names alone, or learning an occupant's kind through a link, refuses here and fails, though it passes every arm whose non-entries bear names of the harness's choosing ((b), (c), P-15). Once the `rename` completes, the second run's held-state comparison is taken against the state the completed `rename` leaves, and its entry is byte-identical, name and content, to its state when that run created its hold file, and the lock directory's only entry (13.5: no release removes or alters a live run's entry), and a further mutating command started then is refused, `code` `"workspace-busy"`, modifying nothing — the second hold's exclusion intact; once both complete, the workspace equals a twin's on which the two operations ran in turn, and `.xspec/lock` is absent. With the held `rename` met instead by a run on another machine (13.5: a command on another machine removing its entry as a leftover interferes), each mutating command — an operation composing with that `rename`, as above — is started under `--test-hold` in a fresh process-identifier namespace with its own process list, made as (f)'s are but with a process of the harness's own as its first, outliving the command; the harness verifies on itself, through that process, while the `rename` is held and before the command starts, that the namespace's process list lists no identifier the `rename`'s processes bear, a listed one an ineffective staging (H-11). The holder is then no run of the acquirer's machine, and its entry, recording an identifier the acquirer's process list does not list, is a leftover outside the gap (13.5, 14.26): the command takes it over — creating its hold file while the holder still holds, never refused `workspace-busy` — and the lock directory then holds its entry alone, a plain file named apart from the holder's (H-4). The holds are lifted in turn, the holder's first, each run exiting 0, the holder's release finding its entry gone, which changes no outcome; the assertions above then follow but for the further mutating command, which, started in the harness's namespace, would meet an entry recorded on another machine whose identifier that namespace may list, in the gap. Should the command nonetheless be refused `workspace-busy`, the harness checks, through the namespace's first process, whether the namespace has by then allocated an identifier as large as the least the `rename`'s processes bear (allocation being cyclic, T13.5-3) — one such voiding the trial, rerun on a fresh workspace in a fresh namespace a bounded number of times, exhaustion a harness error (H-11) — and a refusal otherwise fails the test: a product judging liveness beyond the acquiring process's own process list, through anything its namespace shares with the host, refuses here, though it passes every arm meeting another namespace's holder only once that holder is dead ((f), (g)). With the lock directory itself — `.xspec/lock` and all it holds — deleted by hand during the hold of each mutating command in turn, the held run, its hold lifted, ends exactly as its unstaged twin does — exit 0, output and final workspace byte-equal (H-6), no `write-failure` — and leaves nothing at `.xspec/lock`: its release finds neither its entry nor the lock directory, which changes no outcome (13.5: interference voids the hold's exclusion alone, and a deletion the environment refuses at release changes no outcome and is no write failure, 14.24), failing a product whose release errs or crashes when both are gone, or that brings either back. (f) The acquirer's own identifier: a held `rename` started as the first process of a fresh process-identifier namespace with its own process list is killed under T13.5-3's discipline, and each mutating command, started the same way in another fresh namespace, takes that dead run's entry over — exit 0, `.xspec/lock` absent afterwards — though the identifier the entry records is listed there, being the acquirer's own (13.5: an entry recorded on another machine is a leftover, and one recording the acquiring process's own identifier lies outside the gap); a product refusing on its own identifier fails — any refusal here failing the test, T13.5-3's reuse check not applying where the identifier listed is the acquirer's own. The namespaces are made as T13.5-9's machine-wide stagings are (E-1, E-3), and the harness verifies on itself, while each of the two runs is held at the seam, that their processes bear the same in-namespace identifiers — a mismatch voiding the trial, rerun on a fresh workspace in fresh namespaces a bounded number of times, exhaustion a harness error (H-11). The later run's entry is named apart all the same (13.5: an entry's name distinguishes its run, no other run's entry ever bearing it): the harness records the dead entry's name after the kill — the lock directory's one entry (T13.5-3) — and, while the later run is held at the seam, that run's entry, the lock directory's one entry by the held-state comparison, bears a byte-different name (H-4's one comparison across runs). A product naming an entry by its identifier alone fails this assertion: the two runs' acquiring processes bearing one identifier here, it overwrites the leftover with its own entry or adopts it as its own, and still exits 0 with `.xspec/lock` absent afterwards, passing the rest of the arm — (f) being the one staging that forces two runs to share an identifier. Residual, recorded: SPEC.md fixes no process structure, and the arm presumes that the structure the seam shows repeats between runs — a refused run reaching no seam — so a product whose in-namespace identifiers vary between runs cannot be judged here. (g) The gap's bound and its manual remedy (13.5; 13.4): a held `rename` started as the first process of a fresh process-identifier namespace, as in (f), is killed under T13.5-3's discipline, the harness verifying on itself — while that run is held, and again before each later command — that every in-namespace identifier its processes bear or bore is listed in the harness's own process list, a mismatch an ineffective staging (H-11): its entry then records an identifier, not the acquirer's own, that the acquirer's process list lists — an entry in 13.5's gap. Each mutating command, started in the harness's own namespace, completes promptly and either proceeds — exit 0, its result byte-equal to the twin's, `.xspec/lock` absent once it ends — or is refused — exit 2, `code` `"workspace-busy"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, nothing modified, the entry byte-identical, name and content (13.5, 14.26: such an entry MAY refuse as a live run's entry does) — never another outcome, T13.5-3's reuse check not applying; where it is refused, `.xspec/lock` is deleted by hand while no mutating command runs (13.4: the remedy for a leftover that blocks), and the same command then proceeds, exit 0, its result byte-equal to the twin's, `.xspec/lock` absent afterwards (13.5: such an entry refuses only until it is deleted manually). (h) Acquisition lists no directory but those 13.5 names — it examines occupants' kinds and lists the lock directory and each directory it removes: with `.xspec`, holding no `lock`, made unlistable but searchable and writable before the invocation (`-wx`, T14-10's discipline — the harness's own listing of it refused, and its own creation and removal of a file in it permitted, verified first), under a configuration whose globs reach nothing under `.xspec` (as T13.5-11(b)'s and (c)'s), each mutating command whose operation leaves no orphan — `rename`, a section-form `move`, `review create`, `resolve`, and `split` — proceeds: exit 0, standard output byte-equal to its unstaged twin's, every path outside `.xspec` and the journal and session files byte-equal to the twin's, `.xspec/lock` absent once it ends; and once the harness restores `.xspec`'s permissions, `build` exits 0 and `check` is clean. A conforming acquisition learns the lock path's kind by examining that path, which needs only `.xspec`'s search permission, brings the lock directory and its entry into existence, which needs its write permission, and lists the lock directory alone; and a refused listing of `.xspec` anywhere but at acquisition is condition 23's state, never a read failure (14.25): no real operation is refused in that state (6.6), and a refresh regenerates graph data in it (13.3). A product whose acquisition lists `.xspec` — to learn the lock path's kind from that listing — meets a refused read there, at acquisition a read failure (14.25), and fails; T14-10's recorded residual does not reach it, no listing of `.xspec` succeeding before acquisition under this staging. Graph data is compared nowhere here, and the file form of `move` is not run: removing its orphans relies on the record (13.4), whose readability without a listing of `.xspec` rests on a graph-data layout SPEC.md leaves unenumerated (13.3).
* **T13.5-11 Release.** 13.5: a command ending normally — completed, refused, or failed, at acquisition or at a refused write or read included — deletes its entry, then the lock directory when that holds nothing else, then `.xspec` when its acquisition created it and it then holds nothing else; release reads nothing, and a deletion the environment refuses changes no outcome and is no write failure (14.24). (a) `.xspec/lock` is absent after each mutating command's success (exit 0) and after its usage error past acquisition (exit 2 — T13.5-1's occupied hold path, and an unknown name the command takes: an old ID, an origin file, a profile, a session, an item); after a refusal of each (exit 1 — a `rename` refused by validation, T6.4-3; a `move` refused for one of 6.5's reasons, T6.5-4; a `review create` the gate turns back on a failing workspace, and one naming an existing session, T10.7-1; a `resolve` of a blocked item, T10.7-10; a `split` of an item of another kind, T10.7-9; and a `resolve` and a `split` naming a corrupt session, T10.1-4 — each met past acquisition, release following it alike, 13.5, the lock path lying outside those tests' own modifies-nothing comparisons, H-6); after a read failure past acquisition (T13.5-12(e)) and a configuration error the rediscovery meets (T13.5-12(d)); after each write failure of T13.5-7 but (b)'s, whose residue T13.5-7 states; and after acquisition's own read and write failures, save where its staging leaves an occupant acquisition could not remove or release could not delete — an obstruction, a leftover whose listing, emptying, or removal was refused, or a lock directory inside an unsearchable `.xspec` (T13.5-10(a), (d), which state each residue); after a busy refusal it holds exactly the holder's entry (T13.5-2). (b) The area's directory: on a workspace holding no `.xspec`, a `rename` failing its argument check (an unknown old ID, exit 2) leaves no `.xspec`, its acquisition having created it — and does so with `.xspec`'s read permission removed during its hold (`-wx`), exiting 2 with its unstaged twin's output, release reading nothing (13.5: a directory's deletion alone decides whether it holds anything else), so failing a product that lists the area's directory before deleting it; with an empty `.xspec` present beforehand, the same run leaves it present and empty; with the workspace root alone made read-only during the same run's hold (`r-x`, `.xspec` left writable; the harness's own attempt to create a file in the root refused first, as T14-9 verifies its stagings), it exits 2 with its unstaged twin's output — no `write-failure` — and leaves `.xspec` present and empty, release's third deletion refused, as (d) refuses its first and T13.5-7(b) its second (13.5: a deletion the environment refuses changes no outcome and is no write failure; 14.24); and with an empty `.xspec/lock` present beforehand, no `.xspec/lock`. With each mutating command in turn: (c) release reads nothing — with the lock directory's read permission removed during the hold (mode `-wx`, T14-10's discipline), the command ends either with its unstaged twin's outcome, exit code and output byte-equal, or with exit 2, `code` `"read-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"` (12.7, H-5), and in both leaves no `.xspec/lock`, release deleting its entry and then the emptied directory, neither deletion needing the directory's read permission — failing a product whose release lists the directory before deleting it and, refused, skips a deletion, leaving its entry or the emptied directory behind; the second outcome stands because 13.5 bounds acquisition's reads and gives release none while SPEC.md neither states nor excludes a read of the lock path between the hold and release, 14.25 making a refused read of the lock path a read failure wherever it is made, the command then attempting nothing further but release — the reading T13.4-12 applies to `inventory`, `check`, and `ids`, which acquire nothing, though not to a preview, whose answer 6.6 fixes as the real operation's once holding exclusivity, which no state of the lock path decides (T6.6-3(a)); this arm and (b)'s unlistable `.xspec` run under a configuration whose globs reach nothing under `.xspec`, (b) admitting its twin's outcome alone — a refused listing of the area's own occupant past acquisition being condition 23's state, never a read failure (14.25), which a `rename` failing its argument check never reports (13.3, 6.6); (d) a refused deletion — with the lock directory made unwritable during the hold (`r-x`, T14-9's discipline), the command ends with its unstaged twin's outcome — exit 0, output byte-equal, no `write-failure` — and its entry remains, the lock directory's only one; once the permissions are restored, the next mutating command takes it over (T13.5-3's discipline) and succeeds, `.xspec/lock` absent afterwards.
* **T13.5-12 Rediscovery under exclusivity.** 13.5: a mutating command holding exclusivity discovers its sources again, and that discovery fixes the sources every later check and read consults, a configuration error it meets reported there — after acquisition's and the hold file's errors, before every other (12.0). The hold engages before the second discovery, so each change below, made during a hold before it is lifted, is deterministic, each arm run under `--json` on a freshly built valid workspace: (a) a spec source holding a `d` reference to the section a held `rename` renames, created during the hold, is rewritten too, the result byte-equal to a twin's on which that file was created before the invocation — and so, separately, is a code source a configured code group matches, importing that section's module and holding a marker on it (4.5; 6.4: a rename rewrites TypeScript markers), created during the hold; and a spec source created during the hold of `review create --strategy audit --name n` lies within the session's scope, the session file byte-equal to such a twin's; (b) such a referencing source — the spec source and, separately, the code source — present at invocation and deleted during the `rename`'s hold is absent from the operation — exit 0, no `read-failure`, the result byte-equal to a twin's on which it was deleted before the invocation; (c) on a workspace built without `specs/A.mdx`, that file created during the hold of `rename specs/A.mdx a b`: the `rename` proceeds, exit 0, as on a twin on which the file was created before the invocation — while, separately, `specs/A.mdx` present at invocation and deleted during the hold makes the `rename` exit 2 with the unknown-file usage error (`code` `null`), nothing modified; (d) under a configuration whose spec and code groups both match a path nothing occupies at invocation, a file created at that path during the hold of `rename specs/A.mdx nope x` (an unknown old ID) makes it exit 2 with `code` `"configuration-error"` and the configuration file's path (T12.7-3) — never the unknown-ID usage error, `code` `null` — nothing modified, `.xspec/lock` absent afterwards (T13.5-11); (e) a directory discovery lists, made unlistable during the hold (`--x`, T14-10's discipline), makes the held command exit 2 with `code` `"read-failure"`, `path` that directory, nothing modified, `.xspec/lock` absent afterwards (T13.5-11); (d) and (e) run likewise with each other mutating command, as an operation otherwise succeeding. A product consulting its first discovery fails (a) — its code-source arm where it does so for code groups alone — (c)'s creation arm, (d), and (e) — not necessarily (b) or (c)'s deletion arm, which it passes by honouring 14.25's further clause (below) for the source deleted during the hold, those two arms pinning the outcome whichever discovery yields it; (b)'s code-source arm fails such a product whose code-source reading takes a vanished file for an unreadable one (14.20, 14.25). A source found absent when its content is read — 14.25's further clause, reading as undiscovered — admits no deterministic staging: no seam separates any command's discovery from its content reads, a mutating command's hold preceding its second discovery; P-14 exercises it as a property.
* **T13.5-13 Overlapping acquisitions** (property-style loop, 16; unsynchronized by design). 13.5: of runs whose acquisitions overlap, at most one acquires — possibly none, the others refused (14.26). Repeatedly — a fixed trial count in CI (E-5) — on a freshly built workspace and on an unbuilt one, several mutating commands, operations each succeeding alone and composing in any order (renames of distinct sections, `review create` under distinct names), each with its own hold path and under `--json`, are started together, unsynchronized; every hold file stands until each run has either exited or created its hold file, and only then is deleted. At that point at most one hold file exists, and each run that created none has exited 2 with `code` `"workspace-busy"`, `path` `".xspec/lock"`, modifying nothing; the run that created one, its hold lifted, exits 0 — a trial with no success at all acceptable. Afterwards the journal's lines and the session files are exactly the successful operation's, the workspace equals a twin's on which that operation alone ran (or the starting workspace, absent a success), and `.xspec/lock` is absent — save that on the unbuilt workspace without a success an empty `.xspec` may remain, 13.5's one stated residue of overlapping acquisitions, and nothing else may. A product letting two overlapping acquisitions both acquire, or refusing with another error, fails. *Unheld arm* (13.5: exclusivity lasts from acquisition to release, which comes as the command ends, after its last write, so concurrency on one machine never loses a journal append or a resolution): repeatedly, a fixed trial count in CI, on a freshly built valid workspace, several mutating commands of one series — renames, each of a section in a file of its own, no file referencing a section another holds, so that the operations share only the journal, derived files, and graph data; and, separately, `review resolve` of distinct items of one `audit` session `s` created while the workspace was valid, each item unblocked from the start (a leaf section's, 10.6) — are started with no `--test-hold`, each under `--json`, unsynchronized, at offsets ranging from simultaneous to staggered across the span one of them takes alone. Each run exits 0, or exits 2 with `code` `"workspace-busy"` and `path` `".xspec/lock"`, never another outcome; once all have exited, `.xspec/lock` is absent and the workspace equals a twin's on which the successful runs' operations ran one after another, unheld, in one of their orders — the harness trying each — every success's rewritten source, journal line, and resolution present and no refused run's effect, a trial with a single success or none included. A product whose run releases short of its last write, or which excludes no unheld run, lets a second run acquire while the first still writes, and fails wherever their writes interleave into a state no serial order yields — an append or a resolution lost to a run that rewrote the journal or the session from its reading before that write, or derived files and graph data left behind the final sources.

## 14. Validation Errors

Sections 1–13 exercise each numbered condition in its home context; this section adds the reporting-contract tests. Primary tests per condition (not exhaustive; the H-7 map is the complete record): 14.1 (T1.3-1), 14.2 (T1.3-2/3/4/6), 14.3 (T1.3-5), 14.4 (T1.4-1/4), 14.5/14.6/14.7 (T14-2, T4-5), 14.8 (T2.4-2/3, T4.3-2, T4.5-3, T14-11), 14.9 (T2.1-5, T5.3-1/2), 14.10 (T12.2-2, T13.4-10, T13.4-11), 14.11 (T4.4-1, T4.5-9), 14.12 (T7.5-2/6), 14.13 (T6.1-3, T6.3-6, T13.4-6), 14.14 (T7-1..T7.5-1), 14.15 (T2.1-2/3, T4-2, T4-5, T4-6, T4.5-8 through T4.5-11), 14.16 (T2.7-1, T2.3-3, T2.7-4, T11.2-4), 14.17 (T2.5-3, T2.7-3), 14.18 (T4.5-5, T4.5-9), 14.19 (T1.5-2, T7.1-1), 14.20 (T1.6-5, T14-3, T14-5, T14-12, T2.3-3, T2.4-2, T2.7-4, T14-11), 14.21 (T10.1-4, T10.1-5), 14.22 (T13.4-6, T13.4-9, T13.4-10, T10.1-6), 14.23 (T6.6-6, T11.6-4, T12.2-2, T13.3-2), 14.24 (T13.5-7, T13.5-10, T14-9), 14.25 (T6.3-6, T7-8, T11.2-6, T13.5-10, T13.5-12, T14-10, P-14), 14.26 (T13.5-2, T13.5-8, T13.5-9, T13.5-10); the refusal reasons and their codes (T14-7; staged at T6.4-3, T6.5-4, T6.5-6, T6.5-16, T6.5-17, T6.5-20, T6.5-21, T6.6-3); the stable-code, location-cardinality, and range contracts (T14-6, T14-8, T14-11); the well-formedness contract (T14-12).

* **T14-1 Actionable and complete reporting.** A workspace seeded with several independent error conditions across files: `build` and `check` report each of them (not only the first), and every report identifies file and location and states a correction-oriented message (information presence, not wording). Families a source validation error leaves detectable are reported beside it (14: a condition goes unreported only where another error makes it undetectable; 13.3: the gate reports exactly the findings a `build` would now report, source validation errors, journal errors, and refused writes alike): a freshly built valid workspace's `specs/A.mdx` is given a section with no `id` (14.1), and beside that error a garbage line is appended to the journal (H-4's shape-independent staging, T6.1-3) — and, separately, on a twin built under `markdown: { emit: true, outDir: "out" }`, the directory `out` is replaced by a plain file, a directory component of every emit destination (T13.3-3's write-path obstruction), in the garbage line's place: `build` and `ids` each exit 1 reporting exactly two findings — the condition-1 finding and, beside it, the condition-13 finding (`path` `".xspec/journal"`, `locations` `[]`, T6.1-3), respectively the condition-22 finding concerning `out` (T13.4-6) — `ids` answering nothing, and `check` reports both, exit 1, nothing modified throughout, failing a product that judges the journal or the write paths only once the sources pass, as at graph construction, and so drops that finding from a failing workspace's report (the session family's counterpart: T10.1-5; staleness's and policy's: T12.2-4). That the judgement makes those reads on the failing side is shown by T14-10(l)(i)'s failing twin for the journal and by T11.2-6's unreadable emit directory for the write paths.
* **T14-2 Unresolved references.** A `d` reference, a `text(...)` target, and a TypeScript marker/`text` call that do not resolve → 14.5, 14.6, 14.7 respectively; the TS case is also a type error against the generated module (asserted when a prior valid generation exists). Escape-spelled forms are unresolved likewise (2.4: read verbatim): `d={"lo\u0067in"}` → 14.5 and a TS marker `SPEC.lo\u0067in` → 14.7, the latter no type error — 14.7's type-error clause holds only for a spelling free of escape sequences (T2.4-5).
* **T14-3 Masking.** An unparseable file (14.20 — malformed MDX, `d={]}`, its zero-length range at the offset of the `]` (T14-11, T14-12); malformed TS under the grammar its name selects: a TSX-only construct in a `.ts` file; invalid UTF-8; BOM) masks conditions inside itself, and every reference into it from other files reports as unresolved (14.5–14.7); the parse-failure location is reported. A configuration error suppresses all source analysis: only 14.14 is reported (exit 2) even with invalid sources present.
* **T14-4 Reporter matrix.** 14.10 and 14.12 reported by `check` only (a stale workspace `build`s successfully by regenerating; a policy-violating workspace `build`s successfully); 14.21 reported by `check`, by `review` subcommands naming the session, and by `review list` — not by `build`, and on a workspace failing `build`'s validations by `check` alone, beside the gate's findings (T10.1-5); 14.23 reported by `inventory` and `rename`/`move` previews only — `check` reports the state as 14.10's unit form, and `build` and the refreshing reads never do (the rebuild replaces the record; the reads leave it unconsulted, T13.3-2); 14.14 delivered as an exit-2 error by every command that loads configuration — never `version` (T12.6-2); 14.13 and 14.22 reported by both `build` and `check` and by the gated reads (T13.3-3), yet accompanying no `occurrences`/`view`/`at` answer — each is the finding of no domain file, the journal and a write-path component never being domain files (11.2, T11.2-6) — and 14.13 delivered besides as an exit-2 error, never a finding, by a given baseline's resolution, at `impact --base` and `review create --base` (6.3, 14.13; T6.3-6); 14.12 and 14.21 likewise accompanying none of those answers, nor `inventory`'s, on a passing workspace (T11.2-6, T11.6-4); 14.24 delivered as an exit-2 error — never a finding — by `build`, `rename`/`move`, every refreshing read of 13.3, and the mutating `review` subcommands, never by `check`, `inventory`, `version`, or a preview (T14-9); 14.25 delivered as an exit-2 error by every command making the refused read, at the read, except where 14.25 assigns the object its own condition (T14-10's table); 14.26 delivered as an exit-2 error — never a finding — by the refused mutating command alone (T13.5-2), never by `check`, `inventory`, `build`, a preview, or a read run during a hold (T13.5-4, T11.6-3, T6.6-3); every other condition reported by both `build` and `check`, and as a domain file's finding accompanying the answers of each of `occurrences`/`view`/`at` whose domain can hold its staged file: all three for a spec-source staging, `occurrences` alone for a code-source one — the only staging for 14.7, 14.11, and 14.18, which locate in code sources alone — `view`'s and `at`'s domains holding spec sources only (11.2, 11.3–11.5, T11.2-5). Among the matrix's stagings: the condition-11 arms of T4.4-1 (`occurrences` carrying the finding beside the call's record), the 14.16 and 14.20 arms of T2.3-3, T2.7-1, T2.7-4, and T14-12 (all three surfaces for their spec-source stagings), and the 14.15 and 14.18 arms of T4-5 and T4.5-9 (`occurrences` alone).
* **T14-5 Grammar selection.** A file matched by a code group and named `.tsx`, containing TSX-only syntax (not parseable as plain TypeScript — T14-3's construct) inside a named unit that also holds a dependency marker and a `text(...)` call: `build` succeeds — `.tsx` parses as TSX (14.20) — and the marker's `references` edge and the call's `embeds` edge are recorded and attributed to that unit per 4.6. The negative direction, the same TSX-only construct in a `.ts` file failing 14.20, is T14-3's; further arms stage it in code-group files of other names, each failing 14.20 identically — any name but `.tsx` selects plain TypeScript (14.20): one ending `.mts`, discriminating against products keying specifically on `.ts`, and `src/x.TSX` (its group globbing `src/*.TSX`), whose ending differs from `.tsx` in case alone (12.0: byte-wise and case-sensitive) — a single-casing arm failing a product that folds the name's case before selecting the grammar.
* **T14-6 Stable codes.** For each of the 26 conditions, staged via its primary test's fixture and read from its stated reporter (T14-4): conditions 1–13 and 15–23 carry the exact token 14 lists (`missing-id` … `unreadable-record`) as the `code` of their finding in the JSON report form, where 12.7 pins it, and conditions 14 and 24–26 — usage errors, never findings (14.14, 14.24–14.26) — carry `configuration-error`, `write-failure`, `read-failure`, and `workspace-busy` as the `code` of the exit-2 error document (12.7, T12.7-3; T14-9, T14-10, T13.5-2), appearing in no findings array, while condition 13 carries `journal-error` as that document's `code` too where a given baseline's resolution reports it as a usage error (6.3, 14.13; T6.3-6) — the value is the token string alone, the ordinal numeral no part of it — so a product omitting or misspelling a code fails even where exit class and located information are right. A plain usage error and a review-operation refusal carry no stable code — `code` `null` (14, T12.7-1/3) — the refusal's finding carrying `locations` `[]` and `path` `null` beside it (14; T10.7-14).
* **T14-7 Refusal reasons.** Staged refusals asserting each stable code with its concerned file, range, or identity (14), form-exact (12.7): a reason concerning an identity carries it as the sole element of `identities`, in 1.5's form over the operation's destination file — `<file>#id` for a rename, `<target-file>#id` for a section move, and the bare `<new-file>`, its root identity, for a file move — whether or not the ID is valid and whether or not the path is a valid source path (14: every entry of a refusal's `identities` is a spelling in 1.5's form over the would-be operation, carried whatever its path's validity and defining no node — the invalid-path arms below), and a reason concerning a path carries it as the finding's `path` with `locations` `[]`: `refused-invalid-id` (concerning the new identity alone — `identities` exactly `["specs/A.mdx#a.then"]` for `rename specs/A.mdx a a.then` and `["specs/B.mdx#x y"]` for `move specs/A.mdx#x 'specs/B.mdx#x y'`, the invalid ID spelled verbatim; intrinsic form only — a structurally misplaced but intrinsically valid new ID reports `refused-structural-parent` alone, never both); `refused-identity-unchanged`, reported alone by an identity-unchanged rename (`["specs/A.mdx#a"]`) and by the exact self-move of either form (`["specs/A.mdx#x"]`; the bare `["specs/A.mdx"]` for `move specs/A.mdx specs/A.mdx`) — no collision reason beside it (6.4: the after-removal check collides with nothing); `refused-id-collision`, locating every colliding bearer (two in T6.4-3's prefix-replacement arm, `b` and `b.c`) with `identities` exactly the located bearers' identities in location order (`["specs/A.mdx#b", "specs/A.mdx#b.c"]`); `refused-structural-parent` (the violated identity); `refused-cycle`, locating the would-be cycle's full path in pre-operation coordinates — for a dependency cycle every reference spelling recording a participating edge; for a spec import cycle each participating import declaration existing before the operation and, for an import the move would add, every reference spelling the operation roots at its binding, whether or not the spelling's characters change (14) — the harness computes the located set as the spellings rooted at the added binding, independent of whether each appears as a `reference-rewrite`: a section move carrying `x` from `specs/A.mdx` into `specs/B.mdx`, where `B` imports `A` and a section of `A` outside the moved subtree references `x` in local form — that reference, rooted after the move at a binding of `B`'s module that `A` lacks, requires adding `B`'s import to `A`, closing the cycle — locates `B`'s existing import declaration and that local reference's spelling (its occurrence span, 5.7), never a range for the import that does not yet exist — and, with that section referencing `x` twice in local form (`d={["x", "x"]}`), both spellings beside the declaration, each rooted at the added binding (14: no representative chosen); and a sibling arm in which the characters need not change — `A` imports a third module `specs/C.mdx` as `C`, the moved text carries `d={C.foo}`, and `C` imports `B` — where the move roots that chain at a binding of `C`'s module `B` lacks, the addition closing the cycle `B` → `C` → `B`: the finding locates `C`'s existing import of `B` and the chain's spelling in `A` (pre-operation coordinates, inside the moved text), though a product choosing `C` as the fresh identifier would rewrite nothing there; and, for a dependency cycle, a move making a section the child of a node it depends on (5.3) — `move specs/A.mdx#x specs/A.mdx#p.x`, `p` and `x` top-level flow-form sections, `x` spelling its dependency on `p` twice (`d={["p", "p"]}`, one edge, 5.2), no other reason applicable — locates both entries and nothing for the containment the move would create (14: a `contains` edge is located by no spelling), failing a product that keeps one spelling per edge; `refused-destination-exists` (the occupied path; the section form's non-spec-source occupant included), judged at a path in discovered-path form alone (14): a file-form destination spelled `./a.mdx` for the origin `a.mdx`, `specs//b.mdx`, or `specs/../specs/b.mdx` — each naming an occupied path were it normalized — is refused `refused-invalid-destination` alone (6.5: a spelling carrying a `.`, `..`, or empty segment is no discovered path), never reported occupied, and a section-form target path so spelled likewise; `refused-missing-target-parent` (the target-parent identity); `refused-invalid-destination` (the destination path as spelled; the destination-side directory-component cases of 6.5 report this code, never 14.22 — a plain file staged as a destination directory component, in T6.5-4's derived-path arm as a directory component of the destination's `outDir` emit destination, and in T6.5-4's symbolic-link arms a link to a directory at a component of the destination path, of a created target file's path, and of the `outDir` emit destination — the link and its target byte-identical after each refusal — and so do T6.5-4's barred path characters and T6.5-20's derived-path relations and module-linking designation); `refused-exposed-derived-file` (`path` the origin's emit destination, `locations` `[]`, `identities` `[]`: T6.5-21); `refused-invalid-rewrite` (locating the moved construct and, for an addition no offset admits, the spellings rooted at its binding, `identities` the concerned files' paths in byte order, `path` `null`: T6.5-16); `refused-moved-import` (locating each moved declaration by the import range of 11.4, `identities` `[]`: T6.5-17). Identities over invalid paths (1.5, 14): `move specs/A.mdx#x 'specs/new.txt#x y'` — an absent path lacking `.mdx`, an invalid ID — reports `refused-invalid-destination` (`path` `specs/new.txt`) beside `refused-invalid-id` with `identities` exactly `["specs/new.txt#x y"]`; `move specs/A.mdx#x specs/new.txt#p.y` reports `refused-invalid-destination` beside `refused-missing-target-parent` with `identities` exactly `["specs/new.txt#p"]`, the absent file bearing no `p`; and `refused-invalid-rewrite`'s created-target identity over such a path is T6.5-16's — in each the identity is a plain string over the path as spelled (12.7), defining no node: `query node` on it is a usage error, exit 2 — 12.0's unknown-identity error, `specs/new.txt` a path in no configured group (T11-6). No reason exists for a rewritten reference failing to resolve: every rewritten reference resolves by construction (6.4, 6.5), asserted on the success side of every successful operation (T6.4-1, T6.5-3), and a code 14 does not list never appears in any report. Every applicable reason reports together, one finding per reason: a section move staged to both collide (`<new-id>` present in the target file) and create a dependency cycle reports both findings, never only the first. The invalid-workspace refusal reports the workspace's numbered findings alone — on a workspace failing validation, a rename staged to also collide reports the validation findings only, exit 1, no refusal reason evaluated or reported beside them (6.4, 14).
* **T14-8 Location cardinality.** A condition several constructs jointly violate is one finding locating every participant, each in its containing file: a triple-duplicated ID → one condition-3 finding with three locations (one per bearer, no representative chosen); an import-binding collision → one condition-15 finding locating every colliding declaration; a cross-file dependency cycle → one condition-9 finding locating its full path, every participating reference spelling, and a spec import cycle every participating import declaration — no representative chosen where one edge or one import hop has several spellings (5.2: duplicate references collapse to one edge, each spelling still a distinct occurrence, 5.7; 2.1: several imports MAY bind one module): on the cross-file `depends` cycle `specs/A.mdx#a` → `specs/B.mdx#x` → `specs/A.mdx#a`, its first edge spelled twice in one array (`<S id="a" d={[B.x, B.x]}>`) and `specs/A.mdx` holding `import B2 from "./B.xspec"` beside `import B from "./B.xspec"`, `B2` unused, the dependency cycle's finding locates both array entries and `specs/B.mdx`'s spelling of the edge back, and the import cycle's finding both of `specs/A.mdx`'s declarations and `specs/B.mdx`'s; on a twin whose first edge is instead an `embeds` edge spelled by two `{text(B.x)}` containers in `a`, the dependency cycle's finding locates both containers, each brace through brace — each finding's `locations` exactly its participants, in 12.7 order, failing a product that detects cycles over the edge or import set and keeps one spelling per edge, or one declaration per hop; a no-occurrence MDX embedding spelling → its condition-6 finding's range the full braced container — the span its occurrence would occupy (5.7), keeping T11.4-6's byte classification exact; a policy finding → `locations` `[]`, `path` `null`, its context identities alone (T12.7-1). Location order within a finding is file bytes, then start, then end (12.7); the ranges themselves, per condition, are T14-11's.
* **T14-9 Write failures (14.24).** Staging discipline, Linux leg (E-1): an environment refusal is staged by permission removal alone — the directory holding the path made read-only and, where the path is occupied, its occupant made unwritable — so that creation, replacement in place or by renaming, appending, and removal are all refused whatever write strategy the product uses (14.24 pins the effect, never the mechanism); for a mutating command the staging is applied while the command is held at the seam of 13.5 (T13.5-1, T13.5-7), after acquisition and before any modification — acquisition's own stagings excepted, applied before the invocation (T13.5-10(d)); the harness first verifies each staging on itself — its own attempt at the staged path is refused — and reports an ineffective staging (a privileged runner) as a harness error (H-11), never a pass or a skip (H-9); symbolic links and non-directory components are never involved (those are 14.22, or the refusal of 6.5: T13.4-6, T6.5-4) — but at acquisition, whose obstructions are this condition (T13.5-10(a)). Graph-data staging: graph data's paths are unenumerated (13.3) — a product may keep it in files directly in the area or in directories of its own below it — so wherever a test refuses a graph-data write by staging `.xspec` unwritable (T13.5-7(f)'s refreshing reads; the concerned-path, reporter-set, and precedence arms below; T10.7-15(b) and (e)), the discipline covers the area and every path of T13.3-2's operational path set, as T14-10(f) stages graph data's refused reads: `.xspec` itself and every directory in that set made read-only and every file in that set unwritable, the journal, the session directory, and the lock path keeping their modes, so every graph-data write is refused whatever layout graph data takes, where `.xspec` alone made read-only would leave a file in a directory below it replaceable; the harness verifies the staging on itself across that set, its own attempts to create a file in `.xspec` and in each directory of the set, and to write each file of the set, all refused. Contract: a refused write is a usage error, exit 2, never a finding — stdout the error document under `--json` and on JSON-only surfaces, `code` `"write-failure"`, `path` the concerned path (12.0, 12.7, T12.7-3), stderr the diagnostic — and the command stops at that write, every earlier write complete and no later one attempted but release's (14.24, 13.5; T13.5-7 asserts the per-command states, T13.5-11 release). Concerned paths, one arm each (T13.5-7's stagings): a generated module or companion (the derived path under `specs/b/`, (f)); an emitted Markdown file's creation or removal (`out/specs/sub/B.md`, `out/specs/A.md`, and the rename's `out/specs/b/B.md`, (c)), and its removal owed alone (`out/specs/A.md`, from `build` and from a rename's finishing regeneration, (h)); a rewritten source (`specs/b/B.mdx`, (a)); a section-form move's created target file (`specs/n/N.mdx`, (g), on either side of the origin's rewrite in `files` order); the journal (`.xspec/journal`, (b)); a session file (`.xspec/reviews/<name>.json`, (e)); a relocation's two writes, each concerning its own path — the origin's removal `specs/A.mdx` in (d), the destination's production `specs/sub/B.mdx` when `specs/sub` is the directory staged unwritable instead (the origin then still present, nothing relocated); graph data, concerning the graph-data area `.xspec` — never a path inside it (14.24, 11.6; (f)'s refreshing read, under the graph-data staging above); and acquisition's writes, staged before the invocation (T13.5-10(a), (d)) — the entry's addition, a leftover's removal, and the lock directory's creation concerning the lock path `.xspec/lock`, no entry ever named, and the area directory's creation and an occupant of the area's own path concerning `.xspec` (14.24). Reporter set (14.24, T14-4): `build`, `rename` and `move`, every refreshing read of 13.3 — `ids`, `show`, `coverage`, `impact --base`, `review status`, `query`, `occurrences`, `view`, and `at` on a stale workspace with `.xspec` staged unwritable (the graph-data staging above) each exit 2 with the error document — and the mutating `review` subcommands; never `check` (exit 1 with the staleness on the same state), `inventory` (exit 0), `version`, or a `--preview` (exit 0, writing nothing). Precedence (12.0: met only at the write it refuses, after every check and validation): on (f)'s stale staging, `query nodes --group <code-group>` exits 2 with the invalid-flag-value error (`code` `null`) and `query node specs/a/A.mdx#missing` with the unknown-node error; `impact --base nope`, and `impact --base` naming a commit the repository holds with the repository's object store refused (T6.3-6(e)'s staging), each exit 2 with the baseline's plain usage error, `code` and `path` `null`, never `write-failure` (12.0, 13.3: a given baseline's resolution precedes the gate, and so the refresh), and on a writable copy of the stale state each leaves T13.3-2's operational path set byte-identical, `check` afterwards still reporting the graph-data unit form (as T10.7-15(f)(ii) pins for `review create --base`); and on a twin whose sources also fail validation `ids` exits 1 with the findings (13.3: nothing is written on a failing workspace, so no write is refused) — a product attempting the refresh first fails each, the object-store arm also one that resolves the ref's name before the refresh and reads the baseline's git data after it; a rename refused by validation (T6.4-3) under (a)'s staging exits 1 with its refusal, no write attempted; and a hold file that cannot be created is 13.5's usage error, never this condition — `--test-hold` naming a path in a read-only directory: exit 2, `code` `null` (T13.5-1); nor is a deletion the environment refuses at release, which changes no outcome (T13.5-7(b), T13.5-11(d)). Recovery (12.1, 13.4): after every arm, restoring the permissions and running `build` exits 0 and `check` is clean.
* **T14-10 Read failures (14.25).** Staging discipline, Linux leg (E-1; the harness verifies each staging on itself as T14-9 does): a refused content read is staged as the file's read permission removed with its write permission kept (mode `-w-------`), so a regeneration that replaces or rewrites the object is never itself refused whatever the product's write strategy; a refused directory listing as the directory's read permission removed with search permission kept (`--x`), so its entries stay reachable by name; nonexistence is never staged as a refusal (14.25: an absent object reads as its own section states — T6.3-2, T11.6-3, T10.1-1, T10.1-6, T7-1, and T7-6 cover the absent cases). The object read decides the outcome, one arm per row of 14.25: (a) a discovered source's content — a spec source `specs/B.mdx` referenced from `specs/A.mdx` and, separately, a code source: condition 20 at `build` and `check`, exit 1, its one location the zero-length range `{"start": 0, "end": 0}` (14), the file masked exactly as an unparseable one (T14-3: A's reference into it reports 14.5, nothing inside it reports), the surfaces of 11.2 still answering per file — `view specs/A.mdx specs/B.mdx` serves A's view, B contributing none, B's condition-20 finding accompanying, exit 1; `occurrences` lists no record for B's spellings; and `at specs/B.mdx 0`, `at specs/B.mdx 7`, and `at specs/B.mdx 999999` each report the resolution explicitly unavailable beside the condition-20 finding, exit 1 — never the out-of-range usage error, the offset bound being judged only where the content was read (11.5); (b) the journal's content — condition 13 concerning `.xspec/journal` from `build`, `check`, and the gated reads (`ids` exits 1 answering nothing, 13.3), a `rename` refused with that finding alone (6.4), and a given baseline's resolution reporting it as its usage error, exit 2, `code` `"journal-error"` (6.3; T6.3-6(c)), and the surfaces of 11.2, whose judgement makes the same read ((l)(i) shows it made), meeting it as that condition, a gate condition that accompanies none of their answers (11.2) — on that otherwise valid workspace `occurrences`, `view` of a spec source, and `at` on it each answer finding-free, exit 0, nothing modified, never 14.25's exit 2 — while `inventory` reports `journal` exactly `{"path": ".xspec/journal", "occupied": true}`, finding-free, exit 0 (11.6: the kind read, permitted, is the only read it makes there); (c) a session file's content — condition 21 from `check`, from `review status <name>` (exit 1, exactly one finding, `corrupt-session`, nothing modified), and from `review list` (the session reported corrupt, exit 1), `inventory` listing the session, exit 0; (d) the configuration file's content — condition 14 from every command but `version` (`build`, `ids`, `inventory`, and `view` as representatives): exit 2, the error document's `code` `"configuration-error"` and `path` the configuration path in the anchoring form (`xspec.config.ts` from the root), `version` answering exit 0 (12.6); (e) a derived file's content — a generated module staged unreadable: `check` reports exactly one condition-10 finding, the per-file form concerning that path, and `build` exits 0 (it reads no derived file; its write replaces the occupant, 13.4); (f) graph data — every path under `.xspec/` other than the journal, the session directory, and the lock path staged unreadable, a directory among them by its read permission alone removed (`-wx`: write and search permission kept, not the discipline's `--x`) and a file as above (`-w-------`), so only reads are refused and every regeneration stays possible whatever layout graph data takes, which SPEC.md leaves unenumerated (13.3): `inventory` reports `recorded` unavailable with the condition-23 finding (concerned path `.xspec`, T11.6-4's outcome), exit 1; a `move --preview` reports its `delta` unavailable likewise (T6.6-6); `check` reports one condition-10 finding in the unit form alone (14.10: the unreadable-record form, never the mismatch form beside it), exit 1; a refreshing read (`ids`) exits 0 with its answer, regenerating the data rather than failing (14.25, 13.3); and `build` exits 0, no later read of the record it wrote asserted: what that read meets turns on the modes `build`'s writes leave, which SPEC.md fixes nowhere (a successful `build`'s replacement of an unreadable record is T12.2-2's, staged shape-blind); and, separately, on a freshly built, valid workspace, the area's own occupant (14.25: a refused read of it but at acquisition is condition 23's state to every surface consulting the record; 11.6) — `.xspec` itself staged unlistable but searchable and writable (`-wx`, T7-8's carve-out staging, verified on the harness itself first), every path under it readable: `inventory` and a `move --preview` of a valid move each answer either as on the unstaged twin — exit 0, `recorded`, respectively `delta`, available — or with that datum unavailable beside the condition-23 finding concerning `.xspec`, exit 1, every other member of the answer equal to the twin's either way, never `read-failure`: whether a product's record read lists the area is its own (13.3), and a product meeting that refused listing as 14.25's usage error fails; (g) a directory discovery lists — `specs/sub` staged unlistable under the glob `specs/**/*.mdx`: every command that loads the configuration (`build`, `check`, `ids`, `view`, `inventory`, `review list`, and a `rename`, each representative) exits 2 with the error document, `code` `"read-failure"`, `path` `specs/sub`, nothing modified; precedence (12.0: at the read, in read order — the configuration search and discovery of 7 before every error consulting them): with `specs/a/A.mdx` also failing validation, `build` still exits 2 with the read failure, never the findings; `coverage <unknown-profile>` reports the read failure (discovery precedes every error consulting configuration); a syntax-class error — `ids extra`, `ids --file '../x'`, a malformed value — is reported without loading configuration, `code` `null` (T12.0-10); and with the configuration file itself invalid the configuration error precedes the read (14.14); its other side, a directory no glob reaches below never listed and its refusal never met (7), is T7-8's; (h) the session directory's listing — `.xspec/reviews` staged unlistable on a valid workspace holding a session: `review list`, `inventory`, and `check` each exit 2 with the error document concerning `.xspec/reviews`, while `build` (reading no session) and `ids` exit 0; `review status <name>`, which may find its session by name without listing, is asserted nowhere here; and on a workspace failing `build`'s validations `review list` reports exactly the gate's findings instead, the listing never reached (T10.7-15(f)); (i) at acquisition, the lock path's occupant and everything under it — the lock directory unlistable, and a subdirectory under it unlistable — `read-failure`, `path` `.xspec/lock`, the nested path never named, nothing modified (T13.5-10(d)); `.xspec` unsearchable, refusing the lock path's kind and every write there, and, separately, the lock directory unsearchable, refusing its occupants' kinds and every route below it — `read-failure`, or `write-failure` where the product meets the refusal first at a write (14.24), `path` `.xspec/lock` either way, nothing modified (T13.5-10(d)); and a directory the second discovery of 13.5 lists — `read-failure` at that directory (T13.5-12(e)); (j) git data — every read a given or recorded baseline's resolution makes but the current journal's (6.3) — no condition: the baseline unreadable, a plain usage error, exit 2, `code` and `path` `null`, never `read-failure` (T6.3-6(e): the repository's stored data and, separately, its discovery); (k) the directories the upward search examines (7) — examined before the root is known, so concerned in their anchoring form (14.25, 11.6) — staged where both routes are refused alike: a working directory `R/tools` beneath a valid workspace root `R`, no glob reaching below it, entered and only then stripped of its read and search permission both (mode `000`) — a process keeps a working directory it entered before the permissions went, as a wrapper may arrange between changing directory and running the product, and a lookup of any entry there and the directory's listing are then each refused, whether spelled relative to it or walked absolutely from the filesystem root — the harness verifying on itself that its own lookup of `xspec.config.ts` there and its own listing of the directory are both refused: `build`, `ids`, `inventory`, and `coverage <unknown-profile>` each exit 2 with the error document, `code` `"read-failure"`, `path` `"."` (the working directory's anchoring form), nothing modified — none loads `R`'s configuration and answers, and none reports `configuration-error`; the same staging with no configuration in `R` or in any ancestor (H-1) likewise, `path` `"."`, never T7-1's missing configuration; `ids extra` there is the syntax-class error, `code` `null`, reported without loading configuration (12.0, T12.0-10); and `version`, which loads none, exits 0 (12.6) — (g)'s precedence on the search's side (12.0: the configuration search before every error consulting it) — failing a product whose search takes a refused examination for absence, as an existence probe answering "absent" on a permission error does, and walks on upward; (l) the kind halves of (b)–(d) (14.25: the journal's, a session file's, and the configuration path's refused kind read is this condition where their refused content read is the file's own; 11.6), each staged where both routes are refused alike, the directory holding the path stripped of its read and search permission both (mode `000`, verified on the harness itself), so no route learns the occupant's kind and no content read begins: (i) the journal — on a freshly built, valid, journal-bearing workspace whose repository holds a commit `c1`, its spec sources including `specs/A.mdx`, `.xspec` so staged: `build`, `ids`, `impact --base c1` (6.3: a given baseline's resolution reads the current journal before any other cause can fail it), and the surfaces of 11.2 — `occurrences`, `view specs/A.mdx`, and `at specs/A.mdx 0`, whose judgement makes every read the gate makes, the journal's included, a read failure there stopping them before any answer (11.2) — each exit 2 with the error document, `code` `"read-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/journal"`, nothing modified — a refused listing of the area's own occupant being condition 23's state, never a read failure (14.25), the journal's path is the one concerned path open to them — and `inventory` exits 2 likewise, `path` `".xspec/journal"` or `".xspec/reviews"` as its read order decides, never the condition-23 answer alone; on a twin whose sources also fail validation, `specs/A.mdx` given a section with no `id` (14.1) after the build and before the staging, `build`, `ids`, and the three surfaces each exit 2 exactly so, and `check` likewise, `path` `".xspec/journal"` or `".xspec/reviews"` (its session read, 14.21) as its read order decides, never exit 1 with the condition-1 finding (13.3, 14: the gate judges the journal beside the sources, T14-1; 11.2: the judgement's reads are made whatever findings the sources carry) — failing there a product that reads the journal only once the sources pass, which answers or reports that finding alone, and on the valid workspace one whose surfaces of 11.2 skip the read, which meets the area's refused write at its refresh (`write-failure`) or answers; none ever reports `journal-error` ((b), T6.3-6(c)), nor a `write-failure` from reading the journal as absent and then meeting the area's refused write; (ii) a session file — on a freshly built, valid workspace holding a valid session `s`, `.xspec/reviews` so staged: `review status s` exits 2, `code` `"read-failure"`, `path` `".xspec/reviews"` or `".xspec/reviews/s.json"` as its route decides, nothing modified — never `corrupt-session` ((c)) and never the unknown-session error; (iii) the configuration path — `--config xspec.config.ts` naming a valid workspace's configuration from its root `R`, `R` entered and then so staged as (k) stages its working directory, so that the path's anchoring form and its workspace-relative path coincide (14, 14.25): `build`, `ids`, and `inventory` each exit 2, `code` `"read-failure"`, `path` `"xspec.config.ts"` — or `"."`, the root's own, for a product learning the kind from the root's listing — nothing modified, never `configuration-error`, whether (d)'s content refusal or missing configuration (T7-1); each of (i)–(iii) failing a product that folds every refused access into the file's own condition, or probes a path with a call answering "absent" on a permission error. Two clauses admit no product-independent staging and are recorded so (as T6.5-6 records its unstageable clauses): a refused read of a path occupant's kind — the area's own occupant's at acquisition included (13.5), though not the lock path's there, which (i) stages, a conforming acquisition taking that kind from no listing (T13.5-10(h)) — since whether a product learns a kind by a separate examination the environment can refuse or from a listing it already made is its own (7, 13.4) — save where both routes are refused alike: a directory staged with read and search permission both removed refuses its listing and the examination of every kind below it, so a product judging a kind there meets a refusal whatever its route, only the concerned path, the directory's or a path below it, turning on the product's read order (T11.2-6's unreadable emit directory; (k)'s working directory; (l)'s three paths); and a refused read of a directory above the workspace root, or any the anchoring resolution makes (11.6) — since whether a product makes such a read at all is its own: it may spell a physical path by examining its components or take it from the working directory the process holds (11.6), and reach the directories the upward search examines by a walk from that working directory or by an absolute one from the filesystem root (7), so a directory above the root made unsearchable once the working directory is entered refuses only the absolute walk, the search stopping at the root (7) — the working directory itself, which every route examines, being (k)'s.
* **T14-11 Per-condition ranges.** Byte-precise fixtures against precomputed offsets, one arm per range rule of 14 beyond T14-8's: a `d` value's offending expression, first token through last — an unresolved array entry alone (no brackets, commas, or whitespace), a non-array braced expression `d={foo}` (14.8) located as the expression its braces enclose, the braces excluded, `d={(BASE.a)}` (14.8) locating `(BASE.a)`, the enclosing parentheses included, `d={BASE.a, BASE.b}` (14.8) locating the whole comma sequence, and `d={ /* c */ BASE.missing }` (14.5) locating `BASE.missing` — the braces and the whitespace and comment between them and the expression excluded, as are U+00A0, U+FEFF, U+1680, and U+3000 spelled there (ECMAScript whitespace, 1.4, its space separators Unicode 15.1's, 14.20; T5.7-2); a spread entry `d={[...BASE.a]}` (14.8) located as `...BASE.a`, the `...` included; the elisions of one array literal as one finding (14.8) located at the whole array literal, brackets included, however many holes — `d={[BASE.a, , , BASE.b]}` one finding, and two sections each spelling an elided array two findings, one per literal — while a trailing comma spells no hole (`[BASE.a,]` is ECMAScript's one-element array literal): `d={[BASE.a,]}` is valid, one `depends` edge and one occurrence spanning `BASE.a`, no finding, failing a product that splits the array on commas and reports 14.8; `d={}` and `d={ /* c */ }` are instead not well-formed MDX (2.7, 14.20) — condition 20, never 14.8, the zero-length range at the offset of the closing brace (the prefix before `}` begins some well-formed file); the other kinds' spans, under every condition locating a reference spelling (14: per kind, 5.7) — an MDX embedding at its full braced container, opening brace through closing brace, under 14.8 as under 14.6 (T14-8): `{text()}` and `{ /* c */ text(BASE?.a) }`, each at the whole container, its inner whitespace and comment included, never at the call or its argument alone; a TypeScript `text(...)` call callee through closing parenthesis, argument included, under 14.7 as under 14.8 (T4.3-2) and 14.11 (T4.4-1): an unresolved `text(SPEC.missing)` and, through an aliased binding (`{ text as t }`, T5.7-2), `t(SPEC.missing)`, each at the whole call, from `text`, respectively `t`, never at the argument chain alone; and a marker's bare reference chain, exclusive of any terminator: an unresolved marker `SPEC.missing;` (14.7) at `SPEC.missing` alone, the `;` excluded, never the whole statement; a non-static bare reference in expression-statement position (`SPEC?.a;`, 14.8) located as the statement's expression exclusive of the `;`, and `(SPEC.a);` (14.8, T4.5-3) located as `(SPEC.a)`, the parentheses included; attribute conditions at the attribute's own characters — each bearer's `id` attribute for 14.2 and 14.3, the `id` or `tags` attribute for 14.4 with one finding per violating attribute (a section `id="a b"` with a child `id="a b.c"` reports two findings, the child's at its own `id`), and 14.17's — a repeated prop locating every attribute spelling the name (both `id` attributes of a twice-`id`ed tag), an unknown prop, a spread attribute (its whole braced construct), an invalid `coverage` value; 14.1 at the section's opening-tag range; 14.15 per form — an import declaration, an export declaration (`export * from "./A.xspec"`), and `import X = require("./A.xspec")` by their own characters, `export import X = require("./A.xspec")` from `import`, the leading `export` and what separates it excluded (as 1.7 excludes one), each of the three otherwise with its decorators and modifiers (14: TypeScript's parser admits them on all three, 14.20) — `@dec export * from "./A.xspec"` and `@dec import X = require("./A.xspec")` each from its `@`, `@dec export import X = require("./A.xspec")` whole from its `@` as well, its `export` inside the range (1.7 excludes only what leads; a product stripping an import-equals declaration's `export` wherever it stands failing this arm), and the invalid import declarations `@dec import { a } from "./A.xspec"` from its `@` and `export import { a } from "./A.xspec"` from `export`, each specifier designating a discovered spec source so that the named binding alone is invalid — the 1.7 exclusion reaching a leading `export` of an import-equals declaration alone, so that a product excluding one from every declaration fails the last arm — and the nested forms of T4-6 located alike, a dynamic `import("./A.xspec")` by its call expression, an import type from `import` through the closing parenthesis of its argument list — `typeof import("./A.xspec").default` and `import("./A.xspec").T<number>` in type positions each locating `import("./A.xspec")`, a `typeof` before it and a qualifier or type arguments after it excluded — a string-named module declaration by its own characters, its decorators and modifiers included and a leading `export` excluded — `declare module "./A.xspec" { }` whole, `declare` included, `export declare module "./A.xspec" { }` from `declare`, and `@dec declare module "./A.xspec" { }` from its `@` — and a colliding non-import declaration by the construct binding the name — `let SPEC;` at `SPEC` alone, `const { SPEC } = o` at `{ SPEC } = o`, `@dec class SPEC {}` from its `@`, `export class SPEC {}` from `class`, and, carrying modifiers other than a leading `export`, `async function SPEC() {}` and `export async function SPEC() {}` from `async`, `abstract class SPEC {}` from `abstract`, `const enum SPEC {}` from `const`, and `declare namespace SPEC { export const v: number }` from `declare` (T4.5-8), a product starting the range at the declaration's keyword failing these — and a colliding parameter by its own characters, its decorators and modifiers included: `SPEC: number` whole, `SPEC = 1` and `{ SPEC }` whole, initializer and binding pattern included, `...SPEC: number[]` from its `...`, and the constructor parameter property `@dec private readonly SPEC: number` from its `@` (T4.5-11(a)); 14.16 per form — an element from its opening tag through its closing tag (a self-closing one its own tag), a fragment from `<>` through `</>` (T2.7-1), an expression container brace through brace, an export statement whole; 14.18 as the binding's identifier extended by the longest static chain it roots at the use (`const n = SPEC.a.b` → the range of `SPEC.a.b`; `f(text)` → `text`); and 14.20's zero-length range — `{"start": 0, "end": 0}` for a refused read (T14-10) and for a byte-order mark, the byte length of the longest well-formed UTF-8 prefix for an encoding failure — the offset of the first byte of the first ill-formed sequence, never a later byte at which a decoder notices it — staged in a spec source and in a code source alike, each `{"start": n, "end": n}` with code `unparseable-source`: a valid 5-byte prefix then `0xFF` → 5; `41 E2 82 41` → 1 (a three-byte sequence cut short, the `41` where a decoder notices); `C0 80` (overlong) → 0; `ED A0 80` (a surrogate code point) → 0; and `41 E2 82` truncated by the end of the file → 1 — a decoder accepting overlong or surrogate encodings, or reporting where it resynchronizes, failing these arms; and for a syntax failure the byte length of the longest whole-character prefix with which some well-formed file begins — an MDX file ending inside an unclosed section (the whole file such a prefix → its byte length) and a TypeScript file `let x = ;` (the prefix `let x = ` → `{"start": 8, "end": 8}`) — never a line/column pair and never past the file's length, the syntax-failure offsets of T2.3-3, T2.4-2, T2.7-3, T2.7-4, and T14-12 asserted the same way. Per-spelling resolution inside a repeated `d` (11.2): a section spelling `d` twice, one entry resolving and one not, reports the 14.17 repetition (both attributes located), one occurrence for the resolving entry (`occurrences`), and one 14.5 finding at the unresolved entry's expression.
* **T14-12 Well-formedness contract.** 14.20 decides well-formedness by derivability alone under the input languages' grammars — every rule beyond derivability excluded, whether the language's own text calls its violation a syntax error or its tools report it after parsing. Positive arms — each file well-formed, proceeding to its ordinary outcome, never 14.20: in a spec source, ECMAScript's early errors — two imports binding one identifier within one ESM block (14.15, T2.1-3); an export naming no declaration — `export { nope }`, `nope` a binding no declaration introduces, on the line after a valid, used import in one ESM block: exactly one condition-16 finding located at the export statement whole (T14-11), exit 1, no 14.20 and no 14.15 (the statement holds no declaration, which 2.1's collision clause needs), the import beside it proceeding normally — listed under `view`'s `imports` with its resolved target, the statement getting no view entry (11.4), and a `{text(BASE.a)}` embedding rooted at it recorded by `occurrences --file`, the finding accompanying each answer, exit 1 (11.2, T14-4) — a product handing the ESM block to a parser enforcing early errors reports a parse failure instead and fails the arm; an assignment to a non-simple target `{1 = 2}` (14.16); and the strict-mode restrictions `{let}` and a legacy octal `{010}` (each 14.16) — and the expression grammar — a comma sequence `{a, b}` (14.16) and `d={BASE.a, BASE.b}` (14.8, one expression located whole, T14-11), `{await x}` (14.16, `await` admitted), `{function(){}}` (14.16, no statement lookahead restriction), a spread attribute `{...(a, b)}` (14.17, T2.7-3), and an export declaration holding JSX, `export const x = <b/>` (14.16, the statement whole); in a code-group file, TypeScript's post-parse checks — a rest parameter that is not last (`function f(...r: number[], x: number) {}`), a misplaced modifier (`abstract m(): void` in a non-abstract class), a duplicate declaration (`let a; let a;`), and a type error (`const n: number = "x"`) — each leaving the file well-formed: `build` and `check` exit 0 on the otherwise valid workspace, a marker inside one of the file's units attributed to it and its edge recorded. The forms SPEC.md's 4.5 and 6.5 cite 14.20 to admit are well-formed likewise, each asserted where its section fixes its outcome, never 14.20: an import declaration nested in a function, class static block, namespace, string-named module declaration, or block (T4.5-10, T4.5-11, T6.5-24), one standing as another statement's body (T6.5-25), and decorated and modified import declarations (T6.5-26) — `build` and `check` exit 0 on each valid staging — and nested or decorated export, import-equals, and string-named module declarations (T4-6, T14-11), each its 14.15 finding. The release pin (14.20: TypeScript's grammar at release 5.9.3, language level ESNext, every text that release accepts both as module code and as script code well-formed): a `.ts` code source holding `{ using x = f(); }`, one holding `async function g() { await using y = h(); }`, and one holding `import a from "./a.json" with { type: "json" };` — each well-formed, `build` and `check` exit 0, a marker beside the construct recording its edge — failing a product whose TypeScript parser predates the releases admitting them (the spec-source twin of the import-attributes form stays 14.20, below). The language level (14.20: ESNext, the level deciding which characters an identifier admits, 1.4), which neither those forms nor the negative `010` and `09` below discriminate, that release judging each alike at ES3, ES5, ES2015, and ESNext — its identifier tables alone separate ESNext from ES3 and ES5, which reject an astral identifier character: a `.ts` code source holding, inside one unit, `const 𮯰 = 1` (U+2EBF0, as in T1.4-5(c)) and the marker `S.𮯰`, `S` the default binding of a spec module holding a section `𮯰` (a valid segment, 1.4) — well-formed, `build` and `check` exit 0, the marker's `references` edge to that section recorded — and, judged by the same grammar (14.20), a configuration file importing `import { defineConfig as 𮯰 } from "xspec"` and exporting `export default 𮯰({…})` over an otherwise valid argument (T7-2's aliased import) — loading without error, `build` exit 0 on the otherwise valid workspace — each failing a product that parses code sources, or the configuration file, at ES3 or ES5 and so reports 14.20, or 14.14, for a well-formed file. And the release's other side, which 14.20 fixes for TypeScript as it does not for MDX text (S-9), no other release's acceptance taking part: `const Ᲊx = 1` in a `.ts` file is 14.20 (below), that release admitting U+1C89 neither to begin nor to continue an identifier at any level, failing a product whose scanner takes identifier characters from its runtime's Unicode tables, which admit it once they postdate 15.1.
